pseudo-Lipschitz in each coordinate
i\in[n]
, whereas this latter
separable class does not necessarily satisfy the pseudo-Lipschitz condition

\|f_{t}({\mathbf{x}})-f_{t}({\mathbf{y}})\|_{2}\leq L(1+(\|{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n})^{k}+(\|{\mathbf{y}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n})^{k})\|{\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{y}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

required in the results of
berthier2017
;
Gerbelot2022
.

It is not hard to check that any functions satisfying (
4.7
)
also satisfy Assumption
4.1
. Indeed, applying
(
4.7
) together with the bounds

\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}\|_{\infty}\prec 1
,
\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}\|_{\infty}\prec 1
,

\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\prec\sqrt{n}
, and

\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\prec 1
from (
4.3
), one may check that

(4.8)

\frac{1}{n}\Big|f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:t})^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:s})-f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})\Big|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

Applying (
4.7
) and a Gaussian concentration argument (to
a Lipschitz function that coincides with
f_{t}^{\top}f_{s}
on a high-probability
set
\{{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}:\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}\|_{\infty}\prec 1\}
for
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
),
one may also check that

(4.9)

\frac{1}{n}\Big|f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})-\mathbb{E}f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})\Big|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},

thus verifying (
4.4
). A similar argument verifies
(
4.5
).

(2)

The guarantee
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\prec 1
for the decomposition
(
4.6
) is stronger than the usual statement of
state evolution ensuring that the empirical distribution of rows
of
{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T}
is close to
{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T})
in a metric of weak convergence.
Indeed, for this statement, a bound of the form
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\prec n^{1/2-\epsilon}
for any
\epsilon>0
would suffice to have an asymptotically
negligible effect on this empirical distribution.

Importantly for our purposes, Assumption
4.1
is sufficiently
general to include all BCP-representable polynomial functions. We show this
also in Appendix
B
, by using the BCP to bound the means
and variances of
n^{-1}f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})
and

n^{-1}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})
when
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}
are Gaussian inputs
and
f_{s}(\cdot),f_{t}(\cdot)
are BCP-representable. Combined with Theorem

4.2
, this will show Theorem
2.6
in
the Gaussian setting of
{\mathbf{W}}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)
.

4.2.
Moment-method analysis of tensor networks

The second step then establishes Theorem
2.6
for
general Wigner matrices using a moment-method analysis.
Since
f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}
and the test functions
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}

in Theorem
2.6
are polynomials, it is clear that
the value

(4.10)

\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})=\frac{1}{n}\,\phi_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})

may be expressed as a polynomial function of the entries of
{\mathbf{W}}
.
We will represent this function as a linear combination of contracted values
of tensor networks, defined as follows.

Definition 4.3
.

An
ordered multigraph

G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
is an undirected multigraph
with vertices
{\mathcal{V}}
and edges
{\mathcal{E}}
, having no self-loops and no isolated
vertices, and with a specified ordering
e_{1},\ldots,e_{\deg(v)}

of the edges incident to each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
. Here,
\deg(v)

is the degree of
v
(the total number of edges incident to
v
,
counting multiplicity).

Given a set of tensors
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
where
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\subseteq(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\otimes k}
, a

{\mathcal{T}}
-labeling

{\mathcal{L}}
of
G
is an assignment of a tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\deg(v)}
to each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
, where the order of

{\mathbf{T}}_{v}
equals the degree of
v
. We call
(G,{\mathcal{L}})
a
tensor network
.
The
value
of this tensor network is

(4.11)

\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\mathcal{L})=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\left[i_{e}:e\sim v\right]

where
[i_{e}:e\sim v]
denotes the ordered tuple of indices

[i_{e_{1}},\ldots,i_{e_{\deg(v)}}]
, and
e_{1},\ldots,e_{\deg(v)}
are the
ordered edges incident to
v
.

When
G
is connected (i.e.
({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
consists of a single connected
component),
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})
may be understood as the scalar value
obtained by contracting the tensor-tensor product associated to
each edge. When
G
consists of multiple connected components,
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})

factorizes as the product of each such value across the components.
We note that specifying an edge ordering is needed to define
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})
, as
the tensors
\{{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\}_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}}
need not be symmetric.

Our representation of
\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})
is then
summarized by the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4
.

Fix any constants
T,D,C_{0}>0
. Suppose that
f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}
and

\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
defining
\phi
in (
2.12
)
are polynomial functions that admit a representation (
2.9
)
via a set of tensors
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{D+1}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
. Suppose also that

\{b_{ts}\}
in (
2.1
) satisfy

|b_{ts}|<C_{0}
for all
1\leq s<t\leq T
.

Then there exist constants
C,M>0
, a list of connected ordered
multigraphs
G_{1},\ldots,G_{M}
depending only on
T,D,C_{0}
and independent of

n
, and a list of
\{{\mathcal{T}}\cup{\mathbf{W}}\}
-labelings
{\mathcal{L}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{L}}_{M}
of

G_{1},\ldots,G_{M}
and coefficients
a_{1},\ldots,a_{M}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|a_{m}|<C
,
such that

(4.12)

\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T})=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{a_{m}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m})}{n}.

Lemma
4.4
follows from an elementary unrolling of the AMP
iterates that is similar to previous analyses
of
Bayati2015
;
Wang2024
;
jones2024fourier
, and we provide its proof in
Appendix
F.1
. The primary difference in our
setting is that, since the polynomial functions
f_{t},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}

are non-separable, the resulting tensors
{\mathbf{T}}_{v}
which represent these
polynomials are non-diagonal. This leads to a more involved moment-method
analysis, in which the BCP condition for
{\mathcal{T}}
is used crucially to bound the
moments of
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})
. Universality of the first moment of
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})

is summarized in the following lemma, which underlies the universality of
Theorem
2.6
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
be a set of
tensors satisfying the BCP, and let
{\mathbf{W}},{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}
be two
Wigner matrices satisfying Assumption
2.2
. Fix any
connected ordered multigraph
G
independent of
n
,
let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a
\{{\mathcal{T}}\cup{\mathbf{W}}\}
-labeling of
G
, and let

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
be the
\{{\mathcal{T}}\cup{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}\}
-labeling that replaces
{\mathbf{W}}
by
{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}
.
Then there is a constant
C>0
independent of
n
for which

(4.13)

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})\bigg]\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}.

In Appendix
C
, we prove Lemma
4.5
, and then
strengthen this to a statement of almost-sure convergence by bounding also
the fourth central moment
\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}))^{4}
.
Combining with Lemma
4.4
, this will conclude
the proof of Theorem
2.6
for general Wigner matrices

{\mathbf{W}}
.

Appendix A
Elementary properties of the BCP

We collect in this appendix several closure properties for sets of
tensors
{\mathcal{T}}
that satisfy the BCP.

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
satisfies the BCP, where
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\subseteq(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\otimes k}
.

(1)

If
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
|a_{n}|<C
for a constant
C>0
,
then
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{a_{n}{\mathbf{T}}\}
satisfies the BCP.

(2)

If
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{T}}
is any transposition of
{\mathbf{T}}

(e.g.
\tilde{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}]={\mathbf{T}}[i_{3},i_{1},i_{2}]
for all
i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\in[n]
)
then
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\tilde{\mathbf{T}}\}
satisfies the BCP.

(3)

If
{\mathbf{T}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{k_{1}}
,
{\mathbf{T}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{k_{2}}
, and
{\mathbf{T}}
is a
contraction of
{\mathbf{T}}_{1},{\mathbf{T}}_{2}
, i.e. there exist transpositions
\tilde{\mathbf{T}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{T}}_{2}
of
{\mathbf{T}}_{1},{\mathbf{T}}_{2}
and an index
k\leq\min(k_{1},k_{2})
for
which
{\mathbf{T}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2k}
is given by

(A.1)

{\mathbf{T}}[j_{1},\ldots,j_{k_{1}-k},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{k_{2}-k}]=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathbf{T}}_{1}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{k},j_{1},\ldots,j_{k_{1}-k}]\tilde{\mathbf{T}}_{2}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{k},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{k_{2}-k}],

then
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{{\mathbf{T}}\}
satisfies the BCP.

Proof.

Statements (a) and (b) are immediate from Definition
2.3
. For
statement (c), note that any expression inside the supremum of
(
2.6
) that has
\ell
indices
i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}

and
m^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
copies of
{\mathbf{T}}
may be expanded into an expression
using
{\mathbf{T}}_{1},{\mathbf{T}}_{2}
with
\ell+km^{\prime}
indices, where each additional index

i_{\ell+1},\ldots,i_{\ell+km^{\prime}}
appears twice. Then the BCP for

{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{{\mathbf{T}}\}
follows from the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathrm{Id}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{\otimes 2}
denote the identity matrix, viewed as a
tensor of order 2. If
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP, then so does
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}
.

Proof.

Consider any expression inside the supremum of
(
2.6
) where the first

m^{\prime}
tensors are given by
\mathrm{Id}
and the last
m-m^{\prime}
are tensors in
{\mathcal{T}}
.
Such an expression is equal to
n^{-1}|\mathrm{va\ell}|
for a value of the form

(A.2)

\mathrm{va\ell}=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}=1}^{n}\prod_{a=1}^{m^{\prime}}\mathrm{Id}[i_{\pi(2a-1)},i_{\pi(2a)}]\prod_{a=m^{\prime}+1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}].

For each
a\in\{1,\ldots,m^{\prime}\}
, if
\pi(2a-1)=\pi(2a)
, then
\mathrm{va\ell}
is
unchanged upon removing the factor
\mathrm{Id}[i_{\pi(2a-1)},i_{\pi(2a)}]
.
If
\pi(2a-1)\neq\pi(2a)
, then
\mathrm{va\ell}
is unchanged upon
removing the factor
\mathrm{Id}[i_{\pi(2a-1)},i_{\pi(2a)}]
and
identifying
i_{\pi(2a)}
with
i_{\pi(2a-1)}
(i.e. replacing all instances of

i_{\pi(2a)}
by
i_{\pi(2a-1)}
and then removing
i_{\pi(2a)}
from the
summation). Iterating this procedure for
a=1,\ldots,m^{\prime}
, we reduce either
to a form
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Id}[i,i]
with a single identity tensor, or to
a form where
m^{\prime}=0
and all remaining tensors belonging to
{\mathcal{T}}
. In the
former case we have
n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}=1
, while in the latter case we have

n^{-1}|\mathrm{va\ell}|\leq C
for all large
n
uniformly over all

{\mathbf{T}}_{m^{\prime}+1},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{m}\in{\mathcal{T}}
by the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}
.
Thus the BCP holds for
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}
.
∎

The next lemma considers expressions of the form (
2.6
) in the
definition of the BCP, when a subset of the tensors have order 1 and are given
by standard Gaussian vectors
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. The lemma bounds
the mean and variance of the resulting expression over
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}
.

Lemma A.3
.

Fix any integers
m\geq m^{\prime}\geq 1
,
k_{1}=\ldots=k_{m^{\prime}}=1
, and

k_{m^{\prime}+1},\ldots,k_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
, and define
k_{0}^{+}=0
and

k_{a}^{+}=k_{1}+k_{2}+\ldots+k_{a}
. Fix
\ell\geq 1
and a surjective map

\pi:[k_{m}^{+}]\to[\ell]
satisfying the two conditions of Definition

2.3
. Fix also
t\geq 1
and a coordinate map
\sigma:[m^{\prime}]\to[t]
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{T}}
is a set of tensors satisfying the BCP,
and
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are independent vectors with
i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries. Then there is a
constant
C>0
such that for any
{\mathbf{T}}_{m^{\prime}+1},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{m}\in{\mathcal{T}}
of the
appropriate orders
k_{m^{\prime}+1},\ldots,k_{m}
, the function

(A.3)

\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{a=1}^{m^{\prime}}{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{\pi(a)}]\right)\left(\prod_{a=m^{\prime}+1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}]\right)

satisfies

(A.4)

|\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|\leq Cn,\qquad\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})]\leq Cn.

Proof.

For the expectation, let
\mathscr{P}
be the set of all pairings
\tau
of
[m^{\prime}]

for which every pair
\{a,b\}\in\tau
satisfies
\sigma(a)=\sigma(b)
.
Then applying Wick’s rule (Lemma
F.4
),

(A.5)

{\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})=\sum_{\tau\in\mathscr{P}}\underbrace{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\ell}}\prod_{\{a,b\}\in\tau}\mathrm{Id}[i_{\pi(a)},i_{\pi(b)}]\prod_{a=m^{\prime}+1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}]}_{:=T(\tau)}.

For each
\tau\in\mathscr{P}
, this summand
T(\tau)
is of the form
(
2.6
) with tensors belonging to
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}
, and
continues to satisfy both conditions of Definition
2.3
. Then by the
BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}
given in Lemma
A.2
,
we have
|T(\tau)|\leq Cn
and hence also

|\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|\leq C^{\prime}n
for some constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
.

For the variance, let us write
i_{\ell+1},\ldots,i_{2\ell}
for a duplication
of the indices
i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}
. We duplicate also the set of tensors,
setting
{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(m+a)}={\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}
for
a=1,\ldots,m^{\prime}
and

{\mathbf{T}}_{m+a}={\mathbf{T}}_{a}
for
a=m^{\prime}+1,\ldots,m
, having orders
k_{m+a}=k_{a}
for all

a=1,\ldots,m
. Then, defining
k_{a}^{+}=k_{1}+\ldots+k_{a}
for each
a\in[2m]

and extending
\pi
to a map
\pi:[2k_{m}^{+}]\to[2\ell]
by

\pi(k_{m}^{+}+k)=\pi(k)+\ell
for all
k\in[k_{m}^{+}]
, we have

(A.6)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{2\ell}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{a=1}^{m^{\prime}}{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{\pi(a)}]\prod_{a=m+1}^{m+m^{\prime}}{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{\pi(a)}]\Bigg]

(A.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\prod_{a=m^{\prime}+1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}]\prod_{a=m+m^{\prime}+1}^{2m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}].

Let
\mathscr{P}
be the set of all pairings
\tau
of
\{1,\ldots,m^{\prime}\}\cup\{m+1,\ldots,m+m^{\prime}\}
for which every pair
\{a,b\}\in\tau
satisfies

\sigma(a)=\sigma(b)
. Then again by Wick’s rule,

(A.8)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})^{2}]=\sum_{\tau\in\mathscr{P}}T(\tau)

where

(A.9)

T(\tau)=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{2\ell}}\prod_{\{a,b\}\in\tau}\mathrm{Id}[i_{\pi(a)},i_{\pi(b)}]\prod_{a=m^{\prime}+1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}]\prod_{a=m+m^{\prime}+1}^{2m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}].

Now let
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{P}
be those pairings for which
each pair
\{a,b\}
has both elements in
\{1,\ldots,m^{\prime}\}
or both elements in

\{m+1,\ldots,m+m^{\prime}\}
, and observe similarly by Wick’s rule that

(A.10)

\displaystyle\big({\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{2\ell}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\prod_{a=1}^{m^{\prime}}{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{\pi(a)}]\cdot\mathbb{E}\prod_{a=m+1}^{m+m^{\prime}}{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{\pi(a)}]\Bigg)

(A.11)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\prod_{a=m^{\prime}+1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}]\prod_{a=m+m^{\prime}+1}^{2m}{\mathbf{T}}_{a}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}]

(A.12)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\in\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}T(\tau).

Thus

(A.13)

\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})]=\sum_{\tau\in\mathscr{P}\setminus\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}T(\tau).

For each
\tau\in\mathscr{P}
, this summand
T(\tau)
in (
A.9
) is of the form
(
2.6
) with tensors belonging to
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}
.
The first even cardinality condition of Definition
2.3
holds for

T(\tau)
, because it holds for the original expression (
A.3
).
The second connectedness condition of Definition
2.3
also holds for

T(\tau)
: This is because, by the given condition that
\pi
defining
(
A.3
) satisfies Definition
2.3
,
there is no partition of
i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}
or of

i_{\ell+1},\ldots,i_{2\ell}
into two index sets that appear on disjoint
sets of tensors, and furthermore since
\tau\notin\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
,
there is also at least one
pair
\{a,b\}\in\tau
for which
i_{\pi(a)}
is one of
i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}

and
i_{\pi(b)}
is one of
i_{\ell+1},\ldots,i_{2\ell}
.
Then by the BCP property for
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}
given in
Lemma
A.2
, we have
T(\tau)\leq Cn
and hence also

\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})]\leq C^{\prime}n
for some constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
.
∎

Corollary A.4
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP and has cardinality
|{\mathcal{T}}|\leq C
for a
constant
C>0
independent of
n
. Let
{\bm{\xi}}_{s}\equiv{\bm{\xi}}_{s}(n)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
for

s=1,\ldots,t
be independent vectors with i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries,
viewed as tensors of order 1, where
t
is also independent of
n
.
Then
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\}
satisfies the BCP almost surely with
respect to
\{{\bm{\xi}}_{1}(n),\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}(n)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.

Proof.

Consider any expression inside the supremum of (
2.6
), where the
first
m^{\prime}
tensors belong to
\{{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\}
and the last
m-m^{\prime}

belong to
{\mathcal{T}}
. Such an expression is given by
n^{-1}|\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|
where

\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})
is a value of the form (
A.3
).
Lemma
A.3
implies
\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})]\leq Cn^{-1}

for some constant
C>0
.
As
n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})
is a polynomial of degree
m^{\prime}
in
the standard Gaussian variables
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}
, it follows from Gaussian
hypercontractivity (Lemma
F.5
) that
there exist constants
C^{\prime},c>0
for which, for any
\epsilon>0
,

(A.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[|n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})-n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|>\epsilon]

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}e^{-(c\epsilon^{2}n)^{1/m^{\prime}}}.

Applying this and the bound
|n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|\leq C
from
Lemma
A.3
, we obtain for some constants
C,C^{\prime},c>0
that

(A.15)

\mathbb{P}[|n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|>C]\leq C^{\prime}e^{-(cn)^{1/m^{\prime}}}.

As
|{\mathcal{T}}|
is bounded independently of
n
, the number of choices for

{\mathbf{T}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{m}\in{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\}

in (
2.6
)
is also bounded independently of
n
. Taking the union bound of
(
A.15
) over all such choices and applying the Borel-Cantelli
lemma, we obtain that (
2.6
) holds almost surely, and thus

{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\}
almost surely satisfies the BCP.
∎

Appendix B
State evolution for Gaussian matrices

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
4.2
on the state evolution
for AMP algorithms defined by stable functions
f_{0},\ldots,f_{T-1}

when
{\mathbf{W}}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)
.
We then show Theorem
2.6
in this Gaussian setting.

Recall the notation
X\prec n^{-a}
from (
4.1
). We will
use throughout the basic properties that

X,Y\prec n^{-a}\Rightarrow X+Y\prec n^{-a}

and
X\prec n^{-a},Y\prec n^{-b}\Rightarrow XY\prec n^{-(a+b)}
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Consider the following statements,
where the constant
C\equiv C(D)>0
underlying
\prec
may depend on
t
.

(I
t
)

There exist random vectors
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t},{\mathbf{E}}_{1:t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}
in the probability space of
{\mathbf{W}}
,
with
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\otimes\mathrm{Id})

and
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1:t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\prec 1
, such that

(B.1)

{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}={\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:t}.

Here
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
-measurable for some
\sigma
-algebra
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}

generated by
{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1:t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}

and auxiliary random variables independent of
{\mathbf{W}}
.

(II
t
)

For all
s,\tau\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
,

(B.2)

\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{z}}_{s},{\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{u}}_{s},{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}\rangle\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

(III
t
)

For all
s,\tau\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
,

(B.3)

\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{z}}_{s},{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau+1}\rangle-\sum_{r=1}^{\tau}b_{\tau+1,r}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{z}}_{s},{\mathbf{z}}_{r}\rangle\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

We will show inductively that (I
t
) holds for
t=1
, and that
for each
t=1,\ldots,T-1
, (I
t
) implies (II
t
,III
t
),
and (I
t
,II
t
,III
t
) imply (I
t+1
).
The theorem then follows from (I
t
) for
t=T
.

Base case (I
t
for
t=1
):

Recall from (
2.1
) that
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{u}}_{2}=f_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1})
.
As each entry of
{\mathbf{W}}
is a mean-zero Gaussian random variable, so is
each entry of
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}
.
A direct calculation using the law of
{\mathbf{W}}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)

shows that the covariance of
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
is given by

(B.4)

{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{z}}_{1}{\mathbf{z}}_{1}^{\top}]={\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{W}}]=\frac{1}{n}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Id}+\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\top}.

Note that
n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}
, which is strictly positive by
assumption. Let
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}}={\mathbf{u}}_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\top}/\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}
be the projection
onto the span of
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
, and
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}}^{\perp}=\mathrm{Id}-{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}}
. Let

{\bm{\xi}}_{1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a
Gaussian vector independent of
{\mathbf{W}}
, and set

(B.5)

{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}={\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{z}}_{1}+{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\qquad{\mathbf{E}}_{1}={\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}}{\mathbf{z}}_{1}-{\bm{\xi}}_{1}.

Then
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}={\mathbf{Z}}_{1}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1}
, where
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Id})={\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{Id})

and
{\mathbf{E}}_{1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,3n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\top})
.
Letting
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
be
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
({\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1},{\bm{\xi}}_{1})
, we note that
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}
is

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
-measurable. Also
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1}\|_{2}
is equal in law to

(3n)^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}\cdot|\xi|
where
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
, so

\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1}\|_{2}\prec 1
by the assumption

n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C
and a Gaussian tail bound.
This establishes (I
1
).

Induction step: (I
t
)
\Rightarrow
(II
t
,
III
t
)
Suppose (I
t
) holds for some
t\leq T-1
.
For any
s,\tau\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
, note that

n^{-1}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s},{\mathbf{Z}}_{\tau}\rangle=n^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s},{\mathbf{Z}}_{\tau}\rangle+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

by a standard concentration argument for Gaussian vectors. Here

n^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s},{\mathbf{Z}}_{\tau}\rangle={\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,\tau]\leq C
. Then by
(I
t
), the bounds
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{s}\|_{2},\|{\mathbf{E}}_{\tau}\|_{2}\prec 1
, and
Cauchy-Schwarz,

(B.6)

\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{z}}_{s},{\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}\rangle=\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}+{\mathbf{E}}_{s},{\mathbf{Z}}_{\tau}+{\mathbf{E}}_{\tau}\rangle={\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,\tau]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Recall that
{\mathbf{u}}_{s}=f_{s-1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:(s-1)})
.
Then by (I
t
), also

(B.7)

\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{u}}_{s},{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}\rangle=\frac{1}{n}\langle f_{s-1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:(s-1)}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:(s-1)}),f_{\tau-1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:(\tau-1)}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:(s-1)})\rangle={\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,\tau]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

where the last equality applies condition (
4.4
) of Assumption

4.1
and
Definition
2.1
for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}
. Combining these two
statements shows (II
t
).
To show (III
t
), using (I
t
) and condition (
4.5
) of
Assumption
4.1
, we have for
any
s,\tau\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
that

(B.8)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{z}}_{s},{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau+1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}+{\mathbf{E}}_{s},\,f_{\tau}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:\tau}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:\tau})\rangle=\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s},f_{\tau}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:\tau})\rangle+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Stein’s lemma (c.f. Lemma
F.3
) gives, for each
coordinate
i=1,\ldots,n
,

\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}[i]f_{\tau}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:\tau})[i]]=\sum_{r=1}^{\tau}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{{\mathbf{Z}}_{r}[i]}f_{\tau}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:\tau})[i]]\cdot{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,r]
. Then

(B.9)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{s},f_{\tau}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:\tau})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{\tau}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits_{r}f_{\tau}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:\tau})]\cdot{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,r]=\sum_{r=1}^{\tau}b_{\tau+1,r}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,r]

where
b_{\tau+1,r}
is defined in (
2.4
).
Combining this with
n^{-1}\langle{\mathbf{z}}_{s},{\mathbf{z}}_{r}\rangle={\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[s,r]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})
as shown above and the assumption

|b_{\tau+1,r}|\leq C
, this shows (III
t
).

Induction step: (I
t
, II
t
, III
t
)

\Rightarrow
(I
t+1
)

Suppose (I
t
,II
t
,III
t
) hold for some
t\leq T-1
.
Recall that
{\mathbf{u}}_{s}=f_{s-1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:(s-1)})
. Then
by the induction hypothesis (I
t
),
the condition (
4.4
) of Assumption
4.1
, and
Definition
2.1
for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
,

(B.10)

n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}[s,\tau]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})\text{ for any }s,\tau\in\{1,\ldots,t+1\}.

Define the event

(B.11)

{\mathcal{E}}=\{n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)}\text{ is invertible}\}.

The bound (
B.10
) and assumption

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1})>c
imply that

(B.12)

\mathbb{P}[{\mathcal{E}}]>1-n^{-D}

for any fixed
D>0
and all large
n
.
To ease notation, let us denote

(B.13)

{\mathbf{u}}={\mathbf{u}}_{1:t}=({\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{u}}_{t})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t},\quad{\mathbf{z}}={\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}=({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t},

and also introduce

(B.14)

{\mathbf{b}}=(b_{t+1,1},\ldots,b_{t+1,t})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t},\quad{\mathbf{y}}=\bigg({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\,{\mathbf{z}}_{2}+b_{2,1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\,\ldots,\,{\mathbf{z}}_{t}+\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}b_{t,s}{\mathbf{u}}_{s}\bigg)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}.

On the event
{\mathcal{E}}
,
let
{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}={\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}
be the projection onto the column
span of
{\mathbf{u}}
, and
{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}=\mathrm{Id}-{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}
.
By definition of the AMP algorithm (
2.1
) and the above quantities,
we have
{\mathbf{y}}={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}
. This implies that on
{\mathcal{E}}
,

(B.15)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}+{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}+{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}

(B.16)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{y}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}+{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}.

Let
{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}={\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}
and

{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}={\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}={\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}
.
Then applying the definition of
{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}
in the AMP algorithm (
2.1
)
and (
B.16
) gives

(B.17)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{b}}={\mathbf{y}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}+{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}-{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{b}}.

Using
{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{s}=0
for all
s\leq t

and the definition of
{\mathbf{y}}
,
we have
{\mathbf{y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}={\mathbf{z}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}
, so on
{\mathcal{E}}
,

(B.18)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{y}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{z}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}+{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}-{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{b}}

(B.19)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{({\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{z}})({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}+{\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{z}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}-{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{b}}}_{:={\mathbf{v}}_{1}}

(B.20)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{{\mathbf{z}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}+{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}}_{:={\mathbf{v}}_{2}}.

We first establish that

(B.21)

1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot\|{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\|_{2}\prec 1.

Restricting to the event
{\mathcal{E}}
, since

{\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{z}}
belongs to the column span of
{\mathbf{u}}
,
we have
{\mathbf{v}}_{1}=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha_{s}{\mathbf{u}}_{s}
for some
coefficients
\alpha_{s}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Let us calculate these
coefficients. The
\tau
-th column of
{\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{z}}
contains
{\mathbf{u}}_{s}
only when

\tau>s
, with the corresponding coefficient being
b_{\tau,s}
. Therefore,

(B.22)

\alpha_{s}=\sum_{\tau=s+1}^{t}b_{\tau,s}\Big(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}\Big)\left[\tau\right]+\Big(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{z}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\Big)\left[s\right]-b_{t+1,s}.

Defining
\beta_{\tau}=(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel})\left[\tau\right]
for each
\tau=1,\ldots,t
, we have

{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}={\mathbf{u}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}=\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\beta_{\tau}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}
, and correspondingly

{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}={\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\beta_{\tau}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}
.
This allows us to expand the second term on the right side of
(
B.22
) as

\begin{split}\Big(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{z}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\Big)\left[s\right]&=\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\left(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}\left[s,\tau\right]\right){\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\\
&=\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\left(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}\left[s,\tau\right]\right)\bigg({\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-\sum_{r=1}^{t}\beta_{r}{\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{r}\bigg).\end{split}

Using the induction hypotheses (II
t
, III
t
), we have for any

\tau,r\leq t
that

(B.23)

\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{r+1}=\sum_{q=1}^{r}b_{r+1,q}\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{q}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})=\sum_{q=1}^{r}b_{r+1,q}\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{q}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Using (I
t
), the bound
n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
,
and a Gaussian tail bound, we have also

(B.24)

\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{Z}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{E}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\prec n^{-1/2}.

Combining (
B
),
(
B.23
), (
B.24
),
and the bound
1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\|n^{-1}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
which follows from (
B.10
)
and
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t})>c
, we have

(B.25)

\displaystyle 1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Big(({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{z}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\Big)\left[s\right]

(B.26)

\displaystyle=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}\bigg)^{-1}\left[s,\tau\right]\bigg(\sum_{r=1}^{t}b_{t+1,r}\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{r}-\sum_{r=2}^{t}\beta_{r}\sum_{q=1}^{r-1}b_{r,q}\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{q}\bigg)+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Plugging (
B.26
) back into (
B.22
), and
rearranging terms, we get

(B.27)

\displaystyle 1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot\alpha_{s}

\displaystyle=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Bigg(\sum_{\tau=s+1}^{t}\beta_{\tau}b_{\tau,s}+\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}\bigg)^{-1}\left[s,\tau\right]\bigg(\sum_{r=1}^{t}b_{t+1,r}\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{r}-\sum_{r=1}^{t}\beta_{r}\sum_{q=1}^{r-1}b_{r,q}\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{q}\bigg)

(B.28)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-b_{t+1,s}\Bigg)+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

(B.29)

\displaystyle=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Bigg(\sum_{\tau=s+1}^{t}\beta_{\tau}b_{\tau,s}-\sum_{r=1}^{t}\beta_{r}\sum_{q=1}^{r-1}b_{r,q}\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}\bigg)^{-1}\left[s,\tau\right]\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{q}

(B.30)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{r=1}^{t}b_{t+1,r}\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}\bigg)^{-1}\left[s,\tau\right]\cdot\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{r}-b_{t+1,s}\Bigg)+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

(B.31)

\displaystyle=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Bigg(\sum_{\tau=s+1}^{t}\beta_{\tau}b_{\tau,s}-\sum_{r=1}^{t}\beta_{r}\sum_{q=1}^{r-1}b_{r,q}\mathop{\mathds{1}}\{s=q\}+\sum_{r=1}^{t}b_{t+1,r}\cdot\mathop{\mathds{1}}\{s=r\}-b_{t+1,s}\Bigg)+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

(B.32)

\displaystyle=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Hence
1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot\alpha_{s}\prec n^{-1/2}
for all
s=1,\ldots,t
.
Moreover, we have
n^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{s}\|_{2}\prec 1
by (
B.10
),
and thus (
B.21
) holds.

Next, let us define the Gaussian vector
{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
and
\sigma
-algebra

{\mathcal{F}}_{t+1}
. Note that by definition of the AMP algorithm (
2.1
),

{\mathbf{u}}_{2}=f_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1})
is a function of
{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
,

{\mathbf{u}}_{3}=f_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},{\mathbf{z}}_{2})
is then a function of
{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1:2}
, etc., and

{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}=f_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t})
is then a function of
{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1:t}
.
Thus by the assumption for
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
in the induction hypothesis (I
t
),

{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}
and the above event
{\mathcal{E}}
are
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
-measurable. On this
event
{\mathcal{E}}
, we construct a vector
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
as follows:
Let
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}
be the projection onto the column span of

{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}
, and let
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}=\mathrm{Id}-{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}
.
Let
{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a function of
{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}
and some auxiliary
randomness independent of
{\mathbf{W}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, such that conditional on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}

and on the event
{\mathcal{E}}
,
we have that
{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}
and
{\mathbf{W}}
are independent with

{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}})
. Define

(B.33)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}}{n^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}}+{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}.

Note that by rotational invariance of
\operatorname{GOE}(n)
,
the law of
{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}
conditioned on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}

is equal to that conditioned on
({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}})
, which is
Gaussian and equal to that of
{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}
where

\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)
is independent of
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
. Then conditional on

{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, the law of
{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}={\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}

is that of a mean-zero Gaussian vector with covariance given by

(B.34)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big({\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\big)\big({\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\big)^{\top}\Big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\Big]

(B.35)

\displaystyle\qquad={\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\big)\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\big)^{\top}{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}\Big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\Big]

(B.36)

\displaystyle\qquad={\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Id}+\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}^{\top}\bigg){\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}=\frac{1}{n}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp},

the second equality applying a calculation for the expectation over
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}
that is similar to (
B.4
). Then, applying this
and the definition of
{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}
, conditional on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
and on the event

{\mathcal{E}}
,

(B.37)

\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}=\frac{{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1:(t+1)}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}}{n^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}}+{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathrm{Id}).

On the complementary event
{\mathcal{E}}^{c}
, let us simply set
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
to be
equal to an auxiliary
{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathrm{Id})
random vector that is independent of

{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
and
{\mathbf{W}}
. Then, since the law of
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
conditional on

{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
does not depend on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, we have that

\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
is independent of
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
and

\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathrm{Id})
unconditionally. Now
let
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}
, and
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}
denote
the entries in the last row/column of
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
, and set

(B.38)

{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}={\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}+\Big({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}.

Then by the induction hypothesis (I
t
) that
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
is

{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
-measurable with
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\otimes\mathrm{Id})
,
we may check that
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:(t+1)}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}\otimes\mathrm{Id})
.
Furthermore, letting
{\mathcal{F}}_{t+1}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by

{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
,
{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}
, and the auxiliary randomness defining

\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
above, we have that
{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{t+1}
-measurable.

To conclude the proof of
(\mathrm{I}_{t+1})
, it remains to show for
{\mathbf{v}}_{2}

in (
B.20
) that

(B.39)

1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{2}-{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\prec 1.

On the event
{\mathcal{E}}
, recall that
{\mathbf{v}}_{2}={\mathbf{z}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}+{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}
.
For the first term, note by (
B.10
) that
n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}=n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

and
1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\|(n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec n^{-1/2}
.
Combining these bounds with

{\mathbf{z}}={\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:t}
by
(\mathrm{I}_{t})
where

\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}\|_{2}\prec n^{1/2}
and
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{s}\|_{2}\prec 1

for each
s=1,\ldots,t
, we see that

(B.40)

1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\big\|{\mathbf{z}}({\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}-{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}\big\|_{2}\prec 1.

For the second term, recall the definition of
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
from
(
B.33
). Let us approximate the denominator

n^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}
: By (
B.10
),

n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

and
1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}\|^{2}=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot(n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})(n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}})^{-1}(n^{-1}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1})=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})
. Then

(B.41)

\displaystyle 1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Big(n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\parallel}\|^{2}\Big)

(B.42)

\displaystyle=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Big({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}\Big)+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

We note that
({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1})^{-1}
is the lower-right entry
of
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
, which is bounded below by

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1})>c
. So the above implies also

(B.43)

1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}}=1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Big({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}\Big)^{-1/2}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

In the definition of
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
in (
B.33
),
we have
\|{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\prec n^{1/2}
, and
\|{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\sim\chi^{2}_{t+1}
conditional on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
,
hence
\|{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\prec 1
. Applying these statements to
(
B.33
) shows

(B.44)

1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\Big\|{\mathbf{P}}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{\perp}{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1,\perp}-\Big({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,t+1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1,1:t}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1:t,t+1}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}\Big\|_{2}\prec 1.

Then combining (
B.40
) and
(
B.44
) shows (
B.39
) as claimed.

Applying (
B.21
) and (
B.39
) to (
B.20
)
gives
1\!\!1\{{\mathcal{E}}\}\cdot\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}-{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\prec 1
. Then, defining

{\mathbf{E}}_{t+1}={\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}-{\mathbf{Z}}_{t+1}
and applying also the probability bound
(
B.12
) for
{\mathcal{E}}^{c}
, we have
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\prec 1
,
establishing (I
t+1
) and completing the induction.
∎

We now show Theorem
2.6
in the Gaussian case,
by checking the conditions of Theorem
4.2
.

Lemma B.1
.

In the AMP algorithm (
2.1
),
suppose
{\mathcal{P}}=\{f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}\}
is a BCP-representable set of
polynomial functions, where
f_{0}(\cdot)\equiv{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
. Then there is a constant

C>0
such that
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C
and
|b_{ts}|<C
for all
1\leq s<t\leq T
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
be the set of
tensors satisfying the BCP which represent
{\mathcal{P}}
. We induct on
t
.

Base case (
t=1
):

{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
by assumption, as the constant function
f_{0}(\cdot)\equiv{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
belongs to
{\mathcal{P}}
. Then

(B.45)

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\frac{1}{n}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{1}[i]u_{1}[i]\leq C

for a constant
C>0
, by the definition of the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}
.
The bound for
b_{ts}
is vacuous when
t=1
.

Induction step, bound for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
:
Assume the lemma holds up to
some iteration
t\leq T-1
.
Fixing any tensors
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
of some orders
d+1,d^{\prime}+1

and a coordinate map
\sigma:[d+d^{\prime}]\to[t]
, consider first
the expression

(B.46)

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})=n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big\langle{\mathbf{T}}[{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot],{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d+1)},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d+d^{\prime})},\cdot]\Big\rangle

where
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\overset{iid}{\sim}{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathrm{Id})
.
By the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}
and Lemma
A.3
,

|n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|\leq C
for a constant
C>0
.
Observe that each entry of

{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
takes a form
n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[f_{r}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:r})^{\top}f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})]
for some
r,s\in\{0,\ldots,t\}
. Applying the
representation (
2.9
) of
f_{r}
and

f_{s}
, this is a linear combination of terms of the form

(B.47)

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big\langle{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot],{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(d+1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(d+d^{\prime})},\cdot]\Big\rangle

over tensors
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
(of some orders
d+1,d^{\prime}+1
) and coordinate maps

\sigma:[d+d^{\prime}]\to[t]
. Writing

[{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]=[{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}]{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{1/2}
,
this is further a linear combination of terms of the
form (
B.46
), with coefficients given
by products of entries of
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{1/2}
. The inductive hypothesis
implies that
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{1/2}
is bounded independently of
n
,
so this and the boundedness of (
B.46
) argued above shows that

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
for some constant
C>0
independent of
n
.

Induction step, bound for
b_{t+1,1},\ldots,b_{t+1,t}
:

Fix any
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}
of some order
d+1
and a coordinate map
\sigma:[d]\to[t]
, and consider the expression

(B.48)

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})=n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\,{\mathbf{T}}[{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(k-1)},{\mathbf{e}}_{j},{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(k+1)},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d)},{\mathbf{e}}_{j}]

with the standard basis vector
{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
in positions
k
and
d+1
.
Then again by Lemma
A.3
,
|n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:t})|\leq C

for a constant
C>0
. For any such
{\mathbf{T}}
and
\sigma
, note that
the function
({\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{t})\mapsto{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot]

has divergence with respect to
{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}
given by

(B.49)

\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits_{s}{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot]=\sum_{k\in\sigma^{-1}(s)}\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(k-1)},{\mathbf{e}}_{j},{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(k+1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(d)},{\mathbf{e}}_{j}]

Thus, applying the representation (
2.9
) of
f_{t}
,
observe that
b_{t+1,s}
is a linear combination of terms of this form, scaled
by
n^{-1}
. Again using the representation

[{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]=[{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}]{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{1/2}
,
it follows from linearity, the inductive
hypothesis for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}
, and the boundedness of
(
B.48
) argued above that

|b_{t+1,s}|<C
for each
s=1,\ldots,t
and some constant
C>0
independent of

n
. This completes the induction.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

In the AMP algorithm (
2.1
),
suppose
{\mathcal{P}}=\{f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}\}
is a BCP-representable set of
polynomial functions, where
f_{0}(\cdot)\equiv{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
.
Then Assumption
4.1
holds.

Proof.

For any two tensors
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
of orders
d+1,d^{\prime}+1
and
any coordinate map
\sigma:[d+d^{\prime}]\to[T]
, define a function

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times T}\to\mathbb{R}
by

(B.50)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{x}}_{1:T})=\frac{1}{n}\Big\langle{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot],{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d+1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d+d^{\prime})},\cdot]\Big\rangle.

Letting
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathrm{Id}_{T}\otimes\mathrm{Id})
,
Lemma
A.3

implies
\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})]\leq C/n
for some constant

C>0
. As
f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})
is a polynomial of degree
d+d^{\prime}
in
the standard Gaussian variables
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
, it follows from Gaussian
hypercontractivity (Lemma
F.5
) that
there exist constants
C^{\prime},c>0
such that, for any
\epsilon>0
,

(B.51)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[|f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})|>\epsilon]

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}e^{-(c\epsilon^{2}n)^{\frac{1}{d+d^{\prime}}}}.

Applying this with
\epsilon=(\log n)^{C}/\sqrt{n}
for sufficiently large

C>0
shows

(B.52)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

Recall that
[{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}]=[{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{T}]{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}^{1/2}

where

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C
for a constant
C>0
by Lemma
B.1
.
Then by linearity, if (
B.52
) holds for every
\sigma:[d+d^{\prime}]\to[T]
, then also for every
\sigma:[d+d^{\prime}]\to[T]
we have

(B.53)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

Defining similarly

(B.54)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{x}}_{1:T})=\frac{1}{n}\Big\langle{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot],{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d+1)}\Big\rangle=\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{T}}[{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d)},{\mathbf{x}}_{\sigma(d+1)}],

we have by Lemma
A.3
that

\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\bm{\xi}}_{1:T})]\leq C/n
.
Then by a similar application of Gaussian hypercontractivity and linearity,
for any
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
\sigma:[d+1]\to[T]
,

(B.55)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

Now consider the error

(B.56)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})-f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}).

where
{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}
is any random matrix satisfying the assumption

\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}\|_{2}\prec 1
in (
4.3
).
Using multi-linearity and the form of
f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}
from
(
B.50
), we can expand

(B.57)

\displaystyle f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})-f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})

(B.58)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\sum_{S\subseteq[d+d^{\prime}]}}_{S\neq\varnothing}\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{d+d^{\prime}}}\sum_{j\in[n]}{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d},j]{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[i_{d+1},\ldots,i_{d+d^{\prime}},j]\prod_{a\in S}{\mathbf{E}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{a}]\prod_{a\in[d+d^{\prime}]\setminus S}{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{a}]}_{:=A(S)}.

Here, the removal of the summand for
S=\varnothing
corresponds to the
subtraction of
f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})
. For each summand
A(S)
,
we apply Cauchy-Schwarz over indices
{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{S}
to give

(B.59)

\displaystyle|A(S)|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{S}}\prod_{a\in S}{\mathbf{E}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{a}]^{2}}_{:=A_{1}(S)}\Bigg)^{1/2}\times

(B.60)

\displaystyle\Bigg(\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{S}}\Bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{[d+d^{\prime}]\setminus S}}\sum_{j\in[n]}{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d},j]{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[i_{d+1},\ldots,i_{d+d^{\prime}},j]\prod_{a\in[d+d^{\prime}]\setminus S}{\mathbf{Z}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{a}]\Bigg)^{2}}_{:=A_{2}(S)}\Bigg)^{1/2}

Here,
A_{1}(S)=n^{-1}\prod_{a\in S}\|{\mathbf{E}}_{\sigma(a)}\|_{2}^{2}\prec n^{-1}

by the given condition (
4.3
) for
{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}
. For
A_{2}(S)
, we write

[{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}]=[{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{T}]{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}^{1/2}
. Then

A_{2}(S)
is a linear combination of terms of the form

(B.61)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}\in[n]^{[d+d^{\prime}]}\\
j,j^{\prime}\in[n]\end{subarray}}{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d},j]{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[i_{d+1},\ldots,i_{d+d^{\prime}},j]{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,i_{d}^{\prime},j^{\prime}]{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}[i_{d+1}^{\prime},\ldots,i_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\prime},j^{\prime}]

(B.62)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\times\prod_{a\in S}\mathrm{Id}[i_{a},i_{a}^{\prime}]\prod_{a\in[d+d^{\prime}]\setminus S}{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(a)}[i_{a}]{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma^{\prime}(a)}[i_{a}^{\prime}]

for some
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}:[d+1]\to[T]
,
with coefficients given by products of entries of
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}^{1/2}
.
For each such term (
B.62
),
both conditions of Definition
2.3
hold, where the
second condition holds
because the first two tensors
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
have a shared index
j
,
the last two tensors
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
have a shared index
j^{\prime}
,
and either the first and third tensors
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}
or the second and fourth
tensors
{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
have indices
(i_{a},i_{a}^{\prime})

for some
a\in S
since
S
is non-empty. Thus, by Lemma
A.3
,

|{\mathbb{E}}A_{2}(S)|\leq C
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}A_{2}(S)\leq C/n
for a constant
C>0
. Then
Gaussian hypercontractivity implies as above that
A_{2}(S)\prec 1
.

Combining these bounds
A_{1}(S)\prec n^{-1}
and
A_{2}(S)\prec 1
gives

A(S)\prec n^{-1/2}
, so also

(B.63)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})-f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

A similar argument applied to the functions
f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}
of
(
B.54
) shows

(B.64)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})-f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

Combining (
B.53
) and (
B.55
),

(B.65)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}f_{{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

Applying the tensor representations (
2.9
),
the left side of (
4.4
) for any
s,t\leq T-1

is a sum of such quantities
over a number of tuples
({\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},\sigma)
independent of
n
.
Hence (
4.4
) follows this bound and linearity.
Similarly, combining (
B.55
) with
(
B.64
),

(B.66)

f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}f_{{\mathbf{T}},\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\prec n^{-1/2}.

The left side of (
4.5
) for
s\leq T
and
t\leq T-1

is a sum of such
quantities over a number of tuples
({\mathbf{T}},\sigma)
also independent of
n
,
showing (
4.5
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.6
when

{\mathbf{W}}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)
.

The given conditions of Theorem
2.6
together with
Lemmas
B.1
and
B.2
verify the
assumptions of Theorem
4.2
. Thus
Theorem
4.2
shows a decomposition

(B.67)

{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T}={\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}

where
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}\otimes\mathrm{Id})
and

\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\prec 1
. For the functions

\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
of Theorem
2.6
that also belong to
the BCP-representable set
{\mathcal{P}}
,
the same argument as in Lemma
B.2
shows
that (
4.4
) holds for
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
, i.e.

(B.68)

\displaystyle\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}+{\mathbf{E}}_{1:T})

(B.69)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})={\mathbb{E}}[\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Then in particular

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-{\mathbb{E}}[\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})]=0
a.s. by the
Borel-Cantelli lemma.
∎

Appendix C
Moment-method analysis of tensor networks

In this appendix, we now carry out the moment method analyses that prove Theorem

2.6
in the setting of a general Wigner matrix
{\mathbf{W}}
.
Appendix
C.1
proves Lemma
4.5
on the
first moment of the tensor network value
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})
,
Appendix
C.2
bounds
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}))^{4}]
, and
Appendix
C.3
concludes the proof of
Theorem
2.6
.

C.1.
Universality in expectation

We begin by providing a tensor network interpretation of
the Bounded Composition Property from Definition
2.3
.
Denote the identity tensor as
\mathrm{Id}^{k}\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\otimes k}
with entries

(C.1)

\mathrm{Id}^{k}\left[i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\right]=1\!\!1\{i_{1}=\cdots=i_{k}\}.

Definition C.1
.

An ordered multigraph
G=({\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T},{\mathcal{E}})
is
bipartite
if
its vertex set is the disjoint union of two sets
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}},{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, and
each edge of
{\mathcal{E}}
connects a vertex of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
with a vertex of
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
.
A
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labeling

{\mathcal{L}}
of such a multigraph
G
is
a tensor labeling where each vertex
u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
is labeled
with
\mathrm{Id}^{\deg(u)}
, and each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
has a label

{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\in{\mathcal{T}}
.

Definition
2.3
of the BCP
is then equivalent to the following definition.

Definition C.2

(Alternative definition of BCP)
.

Let
G=({\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T},{\mathcal{E}})
be any bipartite ordered multigraph
(independent of
n
) such that
G
is connected and all vertices in

{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
have even degree. Then there exists a constant
C>0

independent of
n
such that

(C.2)

\sup_{\mathcal{L}}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq Cn

where the supremem is over all
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labelings
{\mathcal{L}}
of
G
.

Indeed, the value
n^{-1}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|
is equivalent to the expression
inside the supremum of
(
2.6
), where
m=|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|
and
\ell=|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|
. The condition
that each vertex
u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
has even degree is equivalent to the
first condition of Definition
2.3
that

|\{k:\pi(k)=j\}|
is even for each
j\in[\ell]
, and condition that
G
is
connected is equivalent to the second condition
of Definition
2.3
that the tensors
{\mathbf{T}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{m}
do not
partition into two sets with disjoint indices.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Throughout the proof, we fix the ordered multigraph
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})

and a decomposition of its vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}_{W}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, where vertices
of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
have degree 2. It suffices to
prove the result for
\{{\mathcal{T}}\cup{\mathbf{W}}\}
-labelings
{\mathcal{L}}
that
assign label
{\mathbf{W}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
and labels in
{\mathcal{T}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, for each fixed
decomposition
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}_{W}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
.
By Lemma
A.2
,
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}\}

augmented with the identity matrix
\mathrm{Id}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
also satisfies the
BCP. Thus,
by inserting an additional degree-2 vertex with label
\mathrm{Id}
between each
pair of adjacent vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
, we will assume without loss of
generality that no two vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
are adjacent in
G
.

For any such decomposition
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}_{W}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
and
labeling
{\mathcal{L}}
, taking the expectation over
{\mathbf{W}}
in the definition of
the value (
4.11
),

(C.3)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]=\frac{1}{n^{1+|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|/2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}}n^{1/2}{\mathbf{W}}[i_{e}:e\sim v]\Bigg]\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{e}:e\sim v].

Let
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
be the set of all partitions of the edge set
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Let
\pi_{\mathbf{i}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
denote the partition
that is induced by the index tuple
{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}
: edges
e,e^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}

belong to the same block of
\pi_{\mathbf{i}}
if and only if
i_{e}=i_{e^{\prime}}
. We write

[e]
for the block of
\pi
that contains edge
e
. Then the
above summation may be decomposed as

(C.4)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]=\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}\frac{1}{n^{1+|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|/2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}}n^{1/2}{\mathbf{W}}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v]\Bigg]\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v].

Here, the first summation is over all possible edge partitions
\pi=\pi({\mathbf{i}})
,
and the second summation
\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}

is over a distinct index
i_{[e]}\in[n]
for each distinct block

[e]\in\pi
, where
*
denotes that indices
i_{[e]},i_{[e^{\prime}]}
must be
distinct for different blocks
[e]\neq[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{V}}_{W})
be the set of all partitions of the vertex subset
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
.
Given a partition
\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
, we associate to it a
partition
\pi_{W}(\pi)\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{V}}_{W})
where
v,u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
belong to the same
block of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
if their incident edges belong to the same two blocks of

\pi
. More precisely:

Definition C.3
.

For any
v,u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
, let
e,e^{\prime}
be the two edges incident to
v
, and

f,f^{\prime}
the two edges incident to
u
. The partition
\pi_{W}(\pi)\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{V}}_{W})

associated to

\pi
is such that
v,u
belong to the
same block of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
if and only if

(C.5)

\{[e],[e^{\prime}]\}=\{[f],[f^{\prime}]\}

(as equality of unordered sets, where possibly
[e]=[e^{\prime}]
and
[f]=[f^{\prime}]
).

Writing
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
for the block of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
containing
v
,
we say that these blocks
[e],[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
are
incident to
the block

[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
and denote this by
[e]\sim[v]
.

This definition is such that for any
{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}

of the summation
\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}
,
the entries
{\mathbf{W}}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v]
and
{\mathbf{W}}[i_{[e]}:e\sim u]
of

{\mathbf{W}}
are equal if
v,u
belong to the same block of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
, and are
independent otherwise. Thus each block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
corresponds to a
different independent entry of
{\mathbf{W}}
.
For each
k\geq 1
, define
{\mathbf{M}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
as the
matrix with entries

(C.6)

{\mathbf{M}}_{k}[i,j]=\mathbb{E}[n^{k/2}{\mathbf{W}}[i,j]^{k}],

where Assumption
2.2
guarantees that
{\mathbf{M}}_{k}
is symmetric and

|{\mathbf{M}}_{k}[i,j]|<C_{k}
for a constant
C_{k}>0
.
Then evaluating the expectation over
{\mathbf{W}}
in (
C.4
) gives

(C.7)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]=\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}\frac{1}{n^{1+|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|/2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{[e]}:[e]\sim[v]]\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v].

Here, the first product is over all blocks
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
,
k[v]
denotes
the number of vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
in the block
[v]
, and

[i_{[e]}:[e]\sim[v]]
is the index pair
[i_{[e]},i_{[e^{\prime}]}]
for the blocks

[e],[e^{\prime}]
incident to
[v]
.

Definition C.4
.

\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
is
single
if some block

[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
has a single vertex, i.e.
k[v]=1
.
A block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
is
paired
if
k[v]=2
and if
its incident blocks
[e],[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
are such that
[e]\neq[e^{\prime}]
.

(Thus if
\pi
is not single and
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
is not paired, then
either
k[v]\geq 3
or
k[v]=2
and
[e]=[e^{\prime}]
.)

By the vanishing of first moments of
{\mathbf{W}}[i,j]
in Assumption
2.2
,
if
\pi
is single then there is some
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
for
which
k[v]=1
and hence
{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}=0
. By the assumption for second moments
of off-diagonal entries
{\mathbf{W}}[i,j]
,
if
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
is paired then
k[v]=2
and

M_{k[v]}[i_{[e]}:[e]\sim[v]]=1
.
Applying these observations above,

(C.8)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]=\mathop{\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}}_{\text{not single}}\frac{1}{n^{1+|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|/2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{[e]}:[e]\sim[v]]\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v].

Next, we apply an inclusion-exclusion argument followed by
Cauchy-Schwarz to bound the difference of (
C.8
) between
{\mathcal{L}}
and

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
. Endow
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
with the partial ordering
\tau\geq\pi
if

\pi
refines
\tau
(i.e. each block of
\tau
is a union of one or more
blocks of
\pi
). We will use

\langle e\rangle\in\tau
to denote the block of
\tau
containing edge
e
, to avoid
notational confusion with the block
[e]\in\pi
. Note that if
v,u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}

belong to the same block of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
, then the two edges incident to

v
and those incident to
u
belong to the same blocks
[e],[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
, and
hence also the same blocks
\langle e\rangle,\langle e^{\prime}\rangle\in\tau
since
\tau\geq\pi
.
Analogous to Definition
C.3
, we continue to say that

\langle e\rangle,\langle e^{\prime}\rangle\in\tau
are the blocks
incident to

[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)

and denote this by
\langle e\rangle\sim[v]
.

Let
\mu(\pi,\tau)
be the inclusion-exclusion (i.e. Möbius inversion)
coefficients such that, for any fixed
\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
whose blocks we denote
momentarily by
[e_{1}],\ldots,[e_{m}]
(where
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
are any choices of
a representative edge in each block), and for any function
f:[n]^{\pi}\to\mathbb{R}
,

(C.9)

\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}f(i_{[e_{1}]},\ldots,i_{[e_{m}]})=\sum_{\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}):\tau\geq\pi}\mu(\pi,\tau)\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau}}f(i_{\langle e_{1}\rangle},\ldots,i_{\langle e_{m}\rangle}).

The sum
\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau}}
on the right side is over one index

i_{\langle e\rangle}\in[n]
for each block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau
, and no longer restricts
indices for different blocks
\langle e\rangle\in\tau
to be distinct.
Applying this inclusion-exclusion relation to (
C.8
),

(C.10)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}}_{\text{not single}}\sum_{\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}):\tau\geq\pi}\frac{\mu(\pi,\tau)}{n^{1+|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|/2}}\underbrace{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau}}\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]}_{:=\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})}.

We clarify that here,
\pi_{W}(\pi)
in the first product of

\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})
continues to be defined by the partition

\pi
(not by
\tau
), and
[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]
is the index
tuple
[i_{\langle e\rangle},i_{\langle e^{\prime}\rangle}]
for the blocks
\langle e\rangle,\langle e^{\prime}\rangle\in\tau
that are
incident to
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
. For later reference in the proof, it is
helpful to interpret
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})
in (
C.10
) as
the value of a
(\pi,\tau)
-dependent
tensor network
(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})
constructed as follows:

–

\check{G}=(\check{\mathcal{V}},\check{\mathcal{E}})
has three disjoint sets of vertices

\check{\mathcal{V}}=\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, and each
edge
e\in\check{\mathcal{E}}
connects a vertex of

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
with a vertex of either
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
or
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
.

–

The vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
are the blocks of
\tau
.
Each vertex
\langle e\rangle\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\equiv\tau
is labeled by the identity
tensor
\mathrm{Id}^{k}
of the appropriate order, and the
ordering of its edges is arbitrary (as the tensor
\mathrm{Id}^{k}
is symmetric).

–

The vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
are the blocks of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
.
Each vertex
[v]\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}\equiv\pi_{W}(\pi)
is labeled by

\mathrm{Id}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
if
[v]
is paired or by
{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}
if
[v]
is not paired,
and this vertex has two edges (ordered arbitrarily) connecting to the blocks

\langle e\rangle,\langle e^{\prime}\rangle\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\equiv\tau
that are incident to
[v]
.

–

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
is the same as the vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
of
G
, with the same
tensor labels.
For each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
with ordered edges
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
in
G
,
the vertex
v\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}\equiv{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
has ordered edges connecting to

\langle e_{1}\rangle,\ldots,\langle e_{m}\rangle\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\equiv\tau
.

An example of this construction of
(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})
from

(G,{\mathcal{L}},\pi,\tau)
is depicted in Figure
3
. It is direct to check
that the quantity
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})
defined in (
C.10
)
indeed equals the value of this tensor network
(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})
,
where the label
\mathrm{Id}^{k}
on each vertex
\langle e\rangle\equiv\tau\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
ensures that
only summands which have the same index value
i_{\langle e\rangle}\in[n]
for all edges
incident to
\langle e\rangle
contribute to the tensor network value in (
4.11
).

Then, defining
{\mathbf{M}}_{k}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})
as in
(
C.6
) and (
C.10
)
with
{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}
in place of
{\mathbf{W}}
, we have

(C.11)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})\bigg]\bigg|\leq\mathop{\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}}_{\text{not single}}\sum_{\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}):\tau\geq\pi}\frac{|\mu(\pi,\tau)|}{n^{1+|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|/2}}\times

(C.12)

\displaystyle\Bigg|\underbrace{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau}}\bigg(\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]-\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}^{\prime}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]\bigg)\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]}_{=\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})}\Bigg|.

{\mathbf{W}}
{\mathbf{W}}
{\mathbf{W}}
{\mathbf{W}}
{\mathbf{W}}
{\mathbf{T}}_{1}
{\mathbf{T}}_{2}
{\mathbf{T}}_{3}
{\mathbf{T}}_{4}
{\mathbf{T}}_{5}
[1]
[2]
[3]
[1]
[1]
[3]
[2]
[1]
[3]
[1]
\rightarrow
\mathrm{Id}
{\mathbf{M}}_{3}
\mathrm{Id}
\mathrm{Id}
\mathrm{Id}

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}

\tilde{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
\overset{}{\rightarrow}
(G,{\mathcal{L}})
(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})
(\tilde{G},\tilde{\mathcal{L}})

Figure 3
.
An example conversion from
(G,{\mathcal{L}})\to(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})\to(\tilde{G},\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
. (Top left) The initial graph
G
with labels
{\mathcal{L}}

in
{\mathbf{T}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{5},{\mathbf{W}}
, and an edge partition
\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
consisting
of three blocks
[1],[2],[3]
. This induces two blocks
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
, one
which is paired and has incident blocks
[1],[2]\in\pi
, and a second with

k[v]=3
and incident blocks
[1],[3]\in\pi
.
(Top right) The graph
(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})
representing
(
C.10
) in the case
\tau=\pi
and
\langle e\rangle=[e]
for each

e=1,2,3
. The vertices of
\check{G}
are
partitioned as
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
.
Two vertices in
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
correspond to the blocks of

\pi_{W}(\pi)
, one paired and labeled with
\mathrm{Id}
and the second unpaired and
labeled with
{\mathbf{M}}_{3}
.
One vertex of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
corresponds to each block of
\tau
. (Bottom)
The graph
(\tilde{G},\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
representing (
C.17
).
Here
\langle 2\rangle\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
is good and thus corresponds to
two vertices in
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
, while

\langle 1\rangle,\langle 3\rangle\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
are bad and each
correspond to a single vertex in
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
.

Definition C.5
.

Given partitions
\pi,\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
with
\tau\geq\pi
, a
block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau
is
bad
if there exists at least one block

[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
that is not paired and that is incident to
\langle e\rangle
,
and
good
otherwise. We write
\tau=\tau^{b}\sqcup\tau^{g}
where
\tau^{b}
and

\tau^{g}
are the sets of bad and good blocks, respectively.

Note that if
|\tau^{b}|=0
, i.e. all blocks of
\tau
are good, then every
block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
must be paired, so the products

\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi):\text{not paired}}
defining

\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}),\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})
are both trivial
and equal to 1, and
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})=0
. When
|\tau^{b}|\neq 0
, these products
involve only indices corresponding to
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}
and not

\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
. Thus

(C.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}\Bigg[\bigg(\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]-\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}^{\prime}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]\bigg)\times

(C.14)

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{g}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]\Bigg]1\!\!1\{|\tau^{b}|\neq 0\}.

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz over the outer summation
\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}
,

(C.15)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}\bigg(\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]-\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}^{\prime}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}\times

(C.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\Bigg[\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{g}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}1\!\!1\{|\tau^{b}|\neq 0\}.

Then applying that
|{\mathbf{M}}_{k}[i,j]|\leq C_{k}
for a constant
C_{k}>0
and all
i,j\in[n]
, there exists a constant
C(\pi,\tau)>0
for
which the first factor is at most
C(\pi,\tau)n^{|\tau^{b}|/2}
, so

(C.17)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})|\leq 1\!\!1\{|\tau^{b}|\neq 0\}C_{\pi,\tau}n^{|\tau^{b}|/2}\Bigg[\underbrace{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{g}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]\bigg)^{2}}_{:=\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})}\Bigg]^{1/2}.

We interpret the quantity
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
in
(
C.17
) as the value of a
(\pi,\tau)
-dependent
bipartite tensor network
\tilde{G}=(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{T},\tilde{\mathcal{E}})
with
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labeling
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
, constructed as follows:

–

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
has one vertex for each block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}
, which
we denote also by
\langle e\rangle\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
, and two
vertices for each block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
, which we denote by

\langle e\rangle^{1},\langle e\rangle^{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
. These are labeled by
\mathrm{Id}
,
and the ordering of their edges is arbitrary.

–

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{T}={\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{2}
consists of two copies
of the original vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
of
G
, with the same tensor labels.
For each
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, we denote
its copies by
v^{1}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}
and
v^{2}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{2}
.
Suppose
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
has ordered edges
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
in the original
graph
G
. If
\langle e_{i}\rangle\in\tau^{b}
, then the
i^{\text{th}}
edge of both

v^{1}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}
and
v^{2}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{2}
connect to

\langle e_{i}\rangle\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
. If
\langle e_{i}\rangle\in\tau^{g}

then the
i^{\text{th}}
edge of
v^{1}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}
connects to

\langle e_{i}\rangle^{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
, and the
i^{\text{th}}
edge of

v^{2}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{2}
connects to
\langle e_{i}\rangle^{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
.

An example of this construction is also illustrated in Figure
3
.
Note that since each edge
e\in{\mathcal{E}}
of the original graph
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
is
incident to at least one vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
(under our starting assumption
that no two vertices of

{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
are adjacent), each block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}\sqcup\tau^{g}
has also at
least one vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
that is incident to an edge of that block. Then
it is direct to check that the quantity
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
of
(
C.17
) is indeed the value of this tensor network
(\tilde{G},\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
as defined in (
4.11
).

Finally, we bound
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
using the given BCP property of

{\mathcal{T}}
and a combinatorial argument.
Fixing any
\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
that is not single, we categorize
the possible types of blocks
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
based on

k[v]
(the number of vertices belonging to
[v]
) and on its
incident blocks
[e],[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
:

–

Let
N_{3}
