be the number of blocks
[v]
with
k[v]\geq 3

–

Let
N_{2}
be the number of paired blocks
[v]
, i.e. with
k[v]=2
and

[e]\neq[e^{\prime}]

–

Let
N_{1}
be the number of blocks
[v]
with
k[v]=2
and
[e]=[e^{\prime}]
.

Let
\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})
be the number of connected components of
\tilde{G}
.
We claim the following combinatorial properties:

(1)

The number of vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
satisfies
|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|\geq 3N_{3}+2N_{2}+2N_{1}
.

(2)

The number of blocks of
\tau^{b}
satisfies
|\tau^{b}|\leq 2N_{3}+N_{1}
.

(3)

The degree of each vertex of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
in
\tilde{G}
is even.

(4)

If
|\tau^{b}|\neq 0
, then the number of connected components of

\tilde{G}
satisfies
\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})\leq 1+2N_{2}+N_{3}
.

Let us verify each of these claims: (1) holds because each block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
counted by
N_{1}
or
N_{2}
contains exactly
k[v]=2
vertices of

{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
, and each block counted by
N_{3}
contains
k[v]\geq 3
vertices.

(2) holds because any block of
\tau^{b}
must be incident to some
block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
that is not paired.
Each non-paired block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
that is counted by
N_{3}
is
incident to two distinct blocks
[e],[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
— hence at most two
blocks in
\tau^{b}
because
\tau\geq\pi
— and each non-paired
block counted by
N_{1}
is incident to one distinct block
[e]\in\pi
— hence
also one block in
\tau^{b}
.

For (3), consider first a bad block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}
. By construction, the
edges of
its corresponding vertex
\langle e\rangle\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
come in pairs, connecting to pairs of
vertices
(v^{1},v^{2})
. Thus
\langle e\rangle
has even degree. Now consider a good block

\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
and its corresponding vertices
\langle e\rangle^{1},\langle e\rangle^{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
. Let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
be the edges of
G

that belong to this block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
. If such an edge
e_{i}
connects two
vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, then there are two corresponding edges in
\tilde{G}
that
connect these vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}
with
\langle e\rangle^{1}
. Otherwise
e_{i}

connects a vertex
u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
with a vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
. (This is the case
for the block
\langle 2\rangle
in Figure
3
.)
Since
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
is good, the block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)

containing this vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
must be paired — thus, there is exactly
one other vertex
v^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
that belongs to
[v]
. If
v
is incident
to exactly one edge in this block
\langle e\rangle
, then so is
v^{\prime}
, and if
v
is
incident to two edges both in
\langle e\rangle
(which may occur if its incident blocks

[e]\neq[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
are merged into a single block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau
)
then so is
v^{\prime}
. This shows that the edges among
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
that connect

{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
come in pairs, and each pair contributes two edges of

\tilde{G}
between
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}
and
\langle e\rangle^{1}
. So

\langle e\rangle^{1}
has even degree. Similarly
\langle e\rangle^{2}
has even degree, which shows (3).

For (4), note that
(\tilde{G},\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
may be obtained from

(\check{G},\check{\mathcal{L}})
by removing all vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
and their
incident edges from
\check{G}
, duplicating the
remaining graph on the vertex set
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\cup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
into two
disjoint copies on
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{1}\cup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}
and

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{2}\cup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{2}
, and merging the vertices of

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{1}
representing bad blocks
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}
with their
copies in
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{2}
while keeping the remaining vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{1},\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{2}
(representing good blocks
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
)
distinct. We may then bound
\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})
via the following observations:

–

\check{G}
is a connected graph, because the original graph
G
is
connected by assumption.

–

For any connected component
K
of
\check{G}
, call it
good
if
all vertices of
K\cap\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
represent good blocks
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
, and
bad
if at least one vertex of
K\cap\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}

represents a bad block
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}
. We track the number
N_{g}
of good
connected components and
N_{b}
of bad connected components
as we sequentially remove vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
from
\check{G}

one at a time:

Supposing that
|\tau^{b}|\neq 0
as assumed in claim (4), the starting
connected graph
\check{G}
is bad, so
N_{g}=0
and
N_{b}=1
. Each vertex

[v]\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
counted by
N_{1}
can be connected to only one vertex of

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
, so its removal does not change
(N_{g},N_{b})
. Each
vertex
[v]\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
counted by
N_{3}
is connected to at most 2
vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
, both of which are bad by definition, so its
removal does not change
N_{g}
and increases
N_{b}
by at most 1. Each vertex

[v]\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
counted by
N_{2}
is connected to at most 2 vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}

which may be either good or bad, so its removal increases the total number of
connected components
N_{b}+N_{g}
by at most
1. Thus, after removing all vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
from
\check{G}
, we have

(C.18)

N_{b}+N_{g}\leq 1+N_{2}+N_{3},\qquad N_{g}\leq N_{2}.

–

After removing all vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
and
applying the above duplication process to obtain
\tilde{G}
,
each component counted by
N_{b}
results in one
connected component of
\tilde{G}
, while each component counted by
N_{g}
results
in two connected components of
\tilde{G}
. Thus

(C.19)

\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})=N_{b}+2N_{g},

and applying the above bounds gives
\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})\leq 1+2N_{2}+N_{3}
which is
claim (4).

We apply these combinatorial claims and the BCP property to conclude the proof:
Suppose
\pi,\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
are such that
\pi
is not single,

\tau\geq\pi
, and
|\tau^{b}|\neq 0
.
Recalling that
\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})
factorizes as the product of the
values across connected components of
\tilde{G}
, and applying claims (3–4) and
the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}
in the form of Definition
C.2
to each
connected component of
\tilde{G}
, we have

(C.20)

\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})\leq C(\tilde{G})n^{\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})}\leq C(\tilde{G})n^{1+2N_{2}+N_{3}}

for a constant
C(\tilde{G})>0
. Since
\tilde{G}
is
determined by
\pi
and
\tau
,
applying (
C.20
) and claim (2) to (
C.17
)
gives, for some different constant
C(\pi,\tau)>0
,

(C.21)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})|\leq C(\pi,\tau)\cdot n^{\frac{2N_{3}+N_{1}}{2}}\cdot n^{\frac{1+2N_{2}+N_{3}}{2}}.

Applying this and claim (1) back to (
C.12
),
and noting that the number of such partitions
\pi,\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
is a
constant independent of
n
, we obtain as desired

(C.22)

\bigg|{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})\bigg]\bigg|\leq C\cdot\frac{1}{n^{1+\frac{3N_{3}+2N_{2}+2N_{1}}{2}}}\cdot n^{\frac{2N_{3}+N_{1}}{2}}\cdot n^{\frac{1+2N_{2}+N_{3}}{2}}\leq Cn^{-1/2}.

∎

C.2.
Almost-sure convergence

We now strengthen Lemma
4.5
to an almost-sure convergence
statement.

Lemma C.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
,
{\mathbf{W}},{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}
, and
{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
be as in
Lemma
4.5
. Then almost surely

(C.23)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\frac{1}{n}\,\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})=0.

Proof.

We will show that for a constant
C>0
,

(C.24)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg)^{4}\bigg]\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}.

We again fix the ordered multigraph
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})

and a decomposition
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}_{W}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
of its vertices,
and consider a labeling
{\mathcal{L}}
that assigns
{\mathbf{W}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
and elements of

{\mathcal{T}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
. We again assume without loss of generality that no two
vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
are adjacent.

Let
G^{\sqcup 4}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\sqcup 4},{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4})

be the ordered multigraph consisting of four disjoint copies of
G
, where

{\mathcal{V}}^{\sqcup 4}={\mathcal{V}}^{1}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}^{2}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}^{3}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}^{4}
are the four
copies of
{\mathcal{V}}
decomposed as
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}={\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{j}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{j}
for
j=1,2,3,4
,
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}={\mathcal{E}}^{1}\sqcup{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\sqcup{\mathcal{E}}^{3}\sqcup{\mathcal{E}}^{4}
are the four copies of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Let

{\mathbf{W}}^{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{W}}^{4}
be four independent copies of the Wigner matrix
{\mathbf{W}}
.
For any word
a=a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}
with letters
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,
define
{\mathcal{L}}_{a}
as the tensor labeling of
G^{\sqcup 4}
such
that for each
j=1,2,3,4
, vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{j}
are labeled by the matrix

{\mathbf{W}}^{a_{j}}
, and vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{j}
have the same labels as
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}

under
{\mathcal{L}}
. Then

(C.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}))^{4}]

(C.26)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})^{4}]-4\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})^{3}]\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]+6\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})^{2}]\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]^{2}-3\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]^{4}

(C.27)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G^{\sqcup 4}}({\mathcal{L}}_{1111})-4\mathrm{va\ell}_{G^{\sqcup 4}}({\mathcal{L}}_{1112})+6\mathrm{va\ell}_{G^{\sqcup 4}}({\mathcal{L}}_{1123})-3\mathrm{va\ell}_{G^{\sqcup 4}}({\mathcal{L}}_{1234})]

where the expectation on the last line is over the independent Wigner
matrices
{\mathbf{W}}^{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{W}}^{4}
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4})
be the set of all partitions of the combined
edge set
{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}
. For any
a=a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}
, we have analogously
to (
C.4
)

(C.28)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n^{4}}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G^{\sqcup 4}}({\mathcal{L}}_{a})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4})}\underbrace{\frac{1}{n^{4+2|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{j=1}^{4}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{j}}n^{1/2}{\mathbf{W}}^{a_{j}}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v]\bigg]\prod_{j=1}^{4}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{j}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v]}_{:=V_{a}(\pi)}.

Let us split
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4})
into three disjoint sets:

–

{\mathcal{A}}
: Partitions
\pi
such that every block
[e]\in\pi
satisfies
[e]\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{j}
for a single copy
j=1,2,3,4
.

–

{\mathcal{B}}
: Partitions
\pi
for which there is a
decomposition
\{1,2,3,4\}=\{j_{1},j_{2}\}\sqcup\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
such that every block

[e]\in\pi
satisfies either
[e]\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{j_{1}}
,

[e]\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{j_{2}}
, or

[e]\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{1}}\cup{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{2}}
, and at least one block

[e]\in\pi
has a nonempty intersection with both
{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{1}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{2}}
.

–

{\mathcal{C}}
: All remaining partitions of
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4})
.

We write correspondingly

(C.29)

V_{a}({\mathcal{A}})=\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}}V_{a}(\pi),\qquad V_{a}({\mathcal{B}})=\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{B}}}V_{a}(\pi),\qquad V_{a}({\mathcal{C}})=\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{C}}}V_{a}(\pi)

so that

\mathbb{E}[n^{-4}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G^{\sqcup 4}}({\mathcal{L}}_{a})]=V_{a}({\mathcal{A}})+V_{a}({\mathcal{B}})+V_{a}({\mathcal{C}})
. Then

(C.30)

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg)^{4}\bigg]=\sum_{{\mathcal{S}}\in\{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}\}}V_{1111}({\mathcal{S}})-4V_{1112}({\mathcal{S}})+6V_{1123}({\mathcal{S}})-3V_{1234}({\mathcal{S}}).

We now analyze separately the terms of (
C.30
) for

{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}
: For
{\mathcal{A}}
, observe that for any
\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}
, since the edge
sets
{\mathcal{E}}^{1},{\mathcal{E}}^{2},{\mathcal{E}}^{3},{\mathcal{E}}^{4}
are unions of disjoint blocks of
\pi
, the
indices of each of the matrices
{\mathbf{W}}^{1},{\mathbf{W}}^{2},{\mathbf{W}}^{3},{\mathbf{W}}^{4}
are distinct in
(
C.28
). Then
V_{a}(\pi)
has the same value for all words

a=a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}
, so
V_{1111}(\pi)=V_{1112}(\pi)=V_{1123}(\pi)=V_{1234}(\pi)
,
and hence

(C.31)

V_{1111}({\mathcal{A}})-4V_{1112}({\mathcal{A}})+6V_{1123}({\mathcal{A}})-3V_{1234}({\mathcal{A}})=0.

For
{\mathcal{B}}
, recall that each
\pi\in{\mathcal{B}}
corresponds to a (unique) associated
decomposition
\{1,2,3,4\}=\{j_{1},j_{2}\}\sqcup\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
where each block
[e]\in\pi
belongs to
{\mathcal{E}}^{j_{1}}
,
{\mathcal{E}}^{j_{2}}
, or
{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{1}\cup k_{2}}
.
We further decompose

(C.32)

V_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}}({\mathcal{B}})=V_{\underline{a_{1}}\underline{a_{2}}a_{3}a_{4}}+V_{\underline{a_{1}}a_{2}\underline{a_{3}}a_{4}}+V_{\underline{a_{1}}a_{2}a_{3}\underline{a_{4}}}+V_{a_{1}\underline{a_{2}}\underline{a_{3}}a_{4}}+V_{a_{1}\underline{a_{2}}a_{3}\underline{a_{4}}}+V_{a_{1}a_{2}\underline{a_{3}}\underline{a_{4}}}

where each term is a summation over those
\pi\in{\mathcal{B}}
corresponding to a
single such decomposition
\{1,2,3,4\}=\{j_{1},j_{2}\}\sqcup\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
,
and the underlined positions indicate the indices
\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
while the
non-underlined positions indicate the indices
\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
. So for instance,

V_{\underline{a_{1}}a_{2}\underline{a_{3}}a_{4}}
is the summation of
V_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}}(\pi)
over
those
\pi\in{\mathcal{B}}
for which each block
[e]\in\pi
belongs to either

{\mathcal{E}}^{1}\cup{\mathcal{E}}^{3}
,
{\mathcal{E}}^{2}
, or
{\mathcal{E}}^{4}
. Note that for any such
\pi
,
the indices of
{\mathbf{W}}^{2}
and
{\mathbf{W}}^{4}
in (
C.28
) are distinct
from those of
\{{\mathbf{W}}^{1},{\mathbf{W}}^{3}\}
, and hence for any
a_{1},a_{3}\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,
the value
V_{\underline{a_{1}}a_{2}\underline{a_{3}}a_{4}}
is the same for all choices of
a_{2},a_{4}
.
This type of observation, together with symmetry of

V_{\underline{a_{1}}a_{2}\underline{a_{3}}a_{4}}
under permutations of the four indices and
relabelings of the copies
\{1,2,3,4\}
, yields the identities

(C.33)

\displaystyle V_{1111}({\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle=6V_{\underline{1}\underline{1}11}=6V_{\underline{1}\underline{1}23}

(C.34)

\displaystyle V_{1112}({\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle=3V_{\underline{1}\underline{1}12}+3V_{\underline{1}11\underline{2}}=3V_{\underline{1}\underline{1}23}+3V_{\underline{1}\underline{2}34}

(C.35)

\displaystyle V_{1123}({\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle=V_{\underline{1}\underline{1}23}+2V_{\underline{1}1\underline{2}3}+2V_{\underline{1}12\underline{3}}+V_{11\underline{2}\underline{3}}=V_{\underline{1}\underline{1}23}+5V_{\underline{1}\underline{2}34}

(C.36)

\displaystyle V_{1234}({\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle=6V_{\underline{1}\underline{2}34}.

Applying these identities shows

(C.37)

V_{1111}({\mathcal{B}})-4V_{1112}({\mathcal{B}})+6V_{1123}({\mathcal{B}})-3V_{1234}({\mathcal{B}})=0.

Finally, for
{\mathcal{C}}
, we claim that there is a constant
C>0
such that
for any
a=a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}
, we have

(C.38)

|V_{a}({\mathcal{C}})|\leq Cn^{-2}.

The proof is similar to the analysis in Lemma

4.5
: Fix any
a=a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}
. Associated to any edge partition

\pi\in{\mathcal{C}}
, consider the vertex partition
\pi_{W}(\pi)\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{1}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{2}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{3}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{4})
such that
v,u
belong to the same block
of
\pi_{W}(\pi)
if and only if their incident edges belong to the same two
incident blocks of
\pi
and, in addition,
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{j}
and
u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{W}^{k}

for two indices
j,k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
such that
a_{j}=a_{k}
(i.e.
v,u

correspond to the same Wigner matrix
{\mathbf{W}}^{a_{j}}={\mathbf{W}}^{a_{k}}
). Let
k[v]
be the
number of vertices in the block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
, call
\pi
single if some
block
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
has
k[v]=1
, and call
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)
paired if

k[v]=2
and its incident blocks
[e],[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
satisfy
[e]\neq[e^{\prime}]
.
Then evaluating the expectation over
{\mathbf{W}}^{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{W}}^{4}
in (
C.28
), we
get analogously to (
C.8
) and (
C.10
)

(C.39)

\displaystyle V_{a}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{C}}}}_{\text{not single}}\frac{1}{n^{4+2|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\pi}}^{*}\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{[e]}:[e]\sim[v]]\prod_{j=1}^{4}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{j}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{[e]}:e\sim v]

(C.40)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{C}}}}_{\text{not single}}\sum_{\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}):\tau\geq\pi}\frac{\mu(\pi,\tau)}{n^{4+2|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|}}\underbrace{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau}}\mathop{\prod_{[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)}}_{\text{not paired}}{\mathbf{M}}_{k[v]}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:\langle e\rangle\sim[v]]\prod_{j=1}^{4}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{j}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]}_{\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})}.

Let
\tau^{b},\tau^{g}
denote the sets of bad and good blocks of
\tau
defined in
the same way as Definition
C.5
. Then applying Cauchy-Schwarz over

\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}
, we obtain analogously to (
C.17
)

(C.41)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{\check{G}}(\check{\mathcal{L}})|\leq C(\pi,\tau)n^{|\tau^{b}|/2}\bigg[\underbrace{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{b}}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\tau^{g}}}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{j}}{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{\langle e\rangle}:e\sim v]\bigg)^{2}}_{:=\mathrm{va\ell}_{\tilde{G}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})}\bigg]^{1/2}.

Now let
N_{3}
,
N_{2}
, and
N_{1}
be the numbers of blocks
[v]\in\pi_{W}(\pi)

with
k[v]\geq 3
, with
k[v]=2
and incident blocks
[e]\neq[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
,
and with
k[v]=2
and incident blocks
[e]=[e^{\prime}]\in\pi
, respectively. Then
the same arguments as in Lemma
4.5
show that

(1)

4|{\mathcal{V}}_{W}|\geq 3N_{3}+2N_{2}+2N_{1}
.

(2)

|\tau^{b}|\leq 2N_{3}+N_{1}
.

(3)

The degree of each vertex of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
in
\tilde{G}
is even.

Furthermore we may count the number of connected components
\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})
of

\tilde{G}

by the following extension of the argument in Lemma
4.5
:
Analogous to Lemma
4.5
,
\check{G}
above
is an ordered multigraph with three disjoint sets of
vertices
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}\equiv\pi_{W}(\pi)
,
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\equiv\tau
,
and
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}\equiv{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{1}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{2}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{3}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{4}
,
and
\tilde{G}
is again obtained from
\check{G}
by removing all vertices of

\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
, duplicating the resulting graph on
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\cup\check{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, and merging the two copies of vertices in
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
that
correspond to bad blocks
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{b}
. Observe that:

–

By definition,
G^{\sqcup 4}
consists of 4 connected components. For
any
\pi\in{\mathcal{C}}
, there are at least two different pairs of indices

1\leq j<k\leq 4
for which a block of
\pi
has non-empty intersection with
both
{\mathcal{E}}^{j}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{k}
. (Otherwise, we would have
\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}
or

\pi\in{\mathcal{B}}
.) Then
\check{G}
has at most 2 connected components.

–

Call a connected component
K
of
\check{G}
good if all vertices

K\cap\check{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
represent good blocks
\langle e\rangle\in\tau^{g}
, and bad
otherwise. We again track the numbers
N_{g}
and
N_{b}
of good and bad connected
components of
\check{G}
as we sequentially remove vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
.
The 1 or 2 connected components of the starting graph
\check{G}
can be either
good or bad. Removing a vertex
[v]\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
counted by
N_{1}
does not
change
(N_{g},N_{b})
, removing a vertex
[v]\in\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
counted by
N_{3}

does not change
N_{g}
and increases
N_{b}
by at most 1, and removing a vertex
counted by
N_{2}
increases
N_{b}+N_{g}
by at most 1. Hence, after removing all
vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
from
\check{G}
, we have

(C.42)

N_{b}+N_{g}\leq 2+N_{2}+N_{3},\qquad N_{g}\leq 2+N_{2}.

–

After removing all vertices of
\check{\mathcal{V}}_{W}
and applying the duplication
procedure to obtain
\tilde{G}
, we have
\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})=N_{b}+2N_{g}
.

Thus we have also

(4)

\textbf{c}(\tilde{G})\leq 4+2N_{2}+N_{3}
.

Applying these properties (1–4) and the BCP condition to (
C.40
) and
(
C.41
),

(C.43)

|V_{a}({\mathcal{C}})|\leq C\cdot\frac{1}{n^{4+\frac{3N_{3}+2N_{2}+2N_{1}}{2}}}\cdot n^{\frac{2N_{3}+N_{1}}{2}}\cdot n^{\frac{4+2N_{2}+N_{3}}{2}}\leq Cn^{-2}

as claimed. Thus

(C.44)

|V_{1111}({\mathcal{C}})-4V_{1112}({\mathcal{C}})+6V_{1123}({\mathcal{C}})-3V_{1234}({\mathcal{C}})|\leq C^{\prime}n^{-2}.

Applying (
C.31
), (
C.37
) and (
C.44
) to
(
C.30
) proves the fourth moment bound (
C.24
).

Then by Markov’s inequality, for any
\epsilon>0
,

(C.45)

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\bigg|>\epsilon\bigg)\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}n^{2}}.

This bound is summable in
n
, so by the Borel-Cantelli Lemma, almost surely

(C.46)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\right]=0.

The same statement holds for
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
, and combining this with
Lemma
4.5
concludes the proof.
∎

C.3.
Concluding the proof

We now conclude the proof of Theorem
2.6
on the
universality of polynomial AMP for general Wigner matrices
{\mathbf{W}}
.

Proof of Theorem
2.6
.

Let
{\mathbf{W}}
be the given Wigner matrix, and let
{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)
.
Let
{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T}
and
{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T}^{\prime}
denote the iterates of the AMP
algorithm (
2.1
) applied with
{\mathbf{W}}
and
{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}
.

By assumption,
{\mathcal{P}}=\{f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}
admit
representations (
2.9
) by a set of tensors
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfying
the BCP. Lemma
B.1
then ensures that
|b_{ts}|
are uniformly
bounded for all
1\leq s<t\leq T
, so Lemma
4.4
yields
representations of the test function values

(C.47)

\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{a_{m}}{n}\,\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m}),\qquad\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T}^{\prime})=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{a_{m}}{n}\,\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\prime})

where
|a_{m}|<C
for each
m=1,\ldots,M
, and
C,M>0
are constants independent
of
n
. By Lemma
C.6
, for each fixed
m=1,\ldots,M
,
almost surely

(C.48)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m})-\frac{1}{n}\,\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\prime})=0.

Thus, almost surely

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}^{\prime})=0
.
The theorem follows from this and the statement

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})=0

for the iterates driven by
{\mathbf{W}}^{\prime}\sim\operatorname{GOE}(n)
,
as already shown in Appendix
B
.
∎

In settings where the condition
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t})>c
of Theorem

2.6
may not hold, let us establish the following
corollary showing that the theorem holds for a random Gaussian
perturbation of the functions
f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}
.

Corollary C.7
.

Fix any
T\geq 1
, and let
{\mathcal{P}}=\{f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}

and
{\mathbf{W}}
satisfy all assumptions of Theorem
2.6

except possibly the condition
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t})>c
for each

t=1,\ldots,T
.

Let
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{T}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

be random vectors with i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries,
independent of each other and of
{\mathbf{W}}
. Fix any
\delta>0
, and
consider the perturbed algorithm

(C.49)

{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\delta}={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t}^{\delta}-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}b_{ts}^{\delta}{\mathbf{u}}_{s}^{\delta},\qquad{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}^{\delta}=f_{t}^{\delta}({\mathbf{z}}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\delta})\equiv f_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\delta})+\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}

with initialization

(C.50)

f_{0}^{\delta}(\cdot)\equiv{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\delta}={\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{1}.

Here, we define
b_{ts}^{\delta}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta}
, and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}^{\delta}
as in
Definition
2.1
for the function

f_{0}^{\delta},\ldots,f_{T-1}^{\delta}
,
with all expectations taken conditional on the realization of
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
.
Then for the test function
\phi=n^{-1}\phi_{1}^{\top}\phi_{2}
, almost surely

(C.51)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T}^{\delta})-\mathbb{E}[\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}^{\delta})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]=0.

Proof.

The corollary follows directly from
Theorem
2.6
upon checking that the
perturbed functions
\{f_{0}^{\delta},\ldots,f_{T-1}^{\delta},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}

are BCP-representable almost surely, and that

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta})>c
for a constant
c>0
and each

t=1,\ldots,T
almost surely for all large
n
.

For BCP-representability, note that
\{f_{0},\ldots,f_{T-1},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}

must admit the representations (
2.9
) for a set of tensors

{\mathcal{T}}
satisfying the BCP that has finite cardinality independent of
n
.
Then
\{f_{0}^{\delta},\ldots,f_{T-1}^{\delta},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}

admit the representations (
2.9
) for the set of tensors

{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{T}\}
. By Lemma

A.1
and Corollary

A.4
, this set satisfies the BCP
almost surely with respect to
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{T}
. Thus

\{f_{0},\ldots,f_{T-1},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}
is almost surely BCP-representable.

To check that

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta})>c
for each
t=1,\ldots,T
, we induct on
t
.
The state evolution covariances
\{{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta}\}_{t=1}^{T}

are defined conditionally on
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
by

(C.52)

\displaystyle{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\delta}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2},

(C.53)

\displaystyle{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}[r+1,s+1]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\,\mathbb{E}[f_{r}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:r}^{\delta})^{\top}f_{s}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}^{\delta})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:(t+1)}]

(C.54)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\,\mathbb{E}[(f_{r}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:r}^{\delta})+\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{r+1})^{\top}(f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}^{\delta})+\delta{\bm{\xi}}_{s+1})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:(t+1)}]\text{ for }r,s=0,\ldots,t,

where
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}^{\delta}
has i.i.d. rows with law
{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta})

and
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta}
depends on
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}
.
For the base case of
t=1
, writing

\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}=\mathbb{E}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}=n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\delta^{2}
,
we have

(C.55)

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{2\delta}{n}\,|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{\xi}}_{1}-\mathbb{E}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{\xi}}_{1}|+\frac{\delta^{2}}{n}\,\big|\|{\bm{\xi}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\|{\bm{\xi}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\big|

Since
n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}<C
for all large
n
, this implies

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=0
a.s. by
a standard tail bound for
{\bm{\xi}}_{1}
. Then since

\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta})\geq\delta^{2}
,
we have
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}^{\delta})>\delta^{2}/2
a.s. for all large
n
.

Now suppose inductively that
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta})>c

for some
t\leq T-1
a.s. for all large
n
. Define

\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}]
with
expectation over only
{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}
. Then observe that

(C.56)

\displaystyle{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}[r+1,s+1]-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}[r+1,s+1]

(C.57)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }r,s\leq t-1\\
\frac{\delta}{n}{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}^{\delta})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:(s+1)}]&\text{ if }r=t\text{ and }s\leq t-1\\
\frac{\delta}{n}\mathbb{E}[f_{r}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:r}^{\delta})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:(r+1)}]^{\top}{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}&\text{ if }r\leq t-1\text{ and }s=t\\
\frac{2\delta}{n}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}^{\delta})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}]^{\top}{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}+\delta^{2}(\frac{1}{n}\|{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-1)&\text{ if }r=s=t.\end{cases}

Since
\{f_{0}^{\delta},\ldots,f_{t-1}^{\delta},f_{t}\}
is
BCP-representable a.s. for all large
n
,
we have by Lemma
B.1
that
for a constant
C>0
, a.s. for all large
n
,

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|f_{s}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}^{\delta})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:(s+1)}]<C

for each
s=0,\ldots,t-1
and

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}^{\delta})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}]<C
.
Then a standard tail bound for
{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}
implies again that

(C.58)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=0\text{ a.s.}

To analyze
\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}
, observe that

(C.59)

\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}=\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta}&{\mathbf{v}}_{t}\\
{\mathbf{v}}_{t}^{\top}&\sigma_{t}^{2}\end{pmatrix}}_{:={\mathbf{A}}_{t+1}}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\delta^{2}\end{pmatrix}

where

(C.60)

{\mathbf{v}}_{t}=\Big(n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}^{\delta})^{\top}f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}^{\delta})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}]\Big)_{s=0}^{t-1},\quad\sigma_{t}^{2}=n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}^{\delta})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}].

Applying again the above bounds

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|f_{s}^{\delta}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s}^{\delta})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:(s+1)}]<C

and
n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}^{\delta})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:t}]<C

a.s. for all large
n
, we have for a constant
C_{t}>0
that

(C.61)

\|{\mathbf{v}}_{t}\|_{2}<C_{t}.

Observe that
{\mathbf{A}}_{t+1}
is the conditional covariance
of
(f_{0}^{\delta},\ldots,f_{t-1}^{\delta},f_{t})
, and hence is positive semidefinite.
Furthermore, by the inductive hypothesis, there is a constant
c_{t}>0
for which

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta})>c_{t}
a.s. for all large
n
.
Consider any unit vector
{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}=({\mathbf{w}}_{t},w)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t+1}
.
If
|w|>\min(c_{t}/(8C_{t}),1/2)
then let us
lower-bound
{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}\geq\delta^{2}w^{2}
. If
|w|\leq\min(c_{t}/(8C_{t}),1/2)
, then let us bound

(C.62)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}_{t+1}{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}\geq{\mathbf{w}}_{t}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\delta}{\mathbf{w}}_{t}-2|w\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{t}|

(C.63)

\displaystyle\geq c_{t}(1-w^{2})-2C_{t}|w|\sqrt{1-w^{2}}\geq 3c_{t}/4-2C_{t}|w|\geq c_{t}/2.

Combining these cases,
{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta}{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}\geq c^{\prime}
for all unit vectors
{\mathbf{w}}_{t+1}
and some constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Thus

\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}^{\delta})>c^{\prime}
a.s. for all large
n
,
completing the induction and the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Polynomial approximation

In this appendix, we prove
Theorem

2.9
and
Corollary
2.10
on the universality of
AMP algorithms with BCP-approximable Lipschitz functions, using a polynomial
approximation argument.

Under the condition (
2.23
) for
f_{0},\ldots,f_{T-1}
and Definition

2.1
for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}
, there exists a constant
C_{0}>0

(depending on
T
and
L
) for which

(D.1)

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+1<C_{0}

for all
t=1,\ldots,T
. Fixing this
C_{0}>0
and
any small constant
\epsilon>0
, let
{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}

and
{\mathcal{Q}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
be the sets of polynomial functions given in
Definition
2.7
for BCP-approximability.
We introduce random vectors

{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{T}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
having i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries independent of
each other and of
{\mathbf{W}}
, and define an auxiliary AMP algorithm

(D.2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}&={\mathbf{W}}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\tilde{b}_{ts}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{s},\qquad\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}=p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t})\equiv p_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t})+\epsilon{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\end{aligned}

with initialization

(D.3)

\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=p_{0}^{\epsilon}(\cdot)\equiv p_{0}(\cdot)+\epsilon{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}=n^{-1}\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Throughout this section, we will condition on a realization of

{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}\equiv{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}(n)
and establish statements which hold
almost surely over
\{{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}(n)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.
The above coefficients
\tilde{b}_{ts}
and polynomial
functions
p_{t}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
are defined as follows:

(1)

Given
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}
(defined conditionally on
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
), let

\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{n})
,
and let
p_{t}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
be a polynomial function such that

(D.4)

\frac{1}{n}\,{\mathbb{E}}[\|f_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})-p_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<\epsilon\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

(For
t=0
, this is a constant vector
p_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{0}
for which

n^{-1}\|f_{0}-p_{0}\|_{2}^{2}<\epsilon
.)
For sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
,
Lemma
D.1
below implies inductively that
\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\iota(\epsilon)<C_{0}

a.s. for all large
n
, so such a polynomial

p_{t}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
exists a.s. for all large
n
by
Definition

2.7
.
If
f_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})
depends only on the preceding iterates
\{{\mathbf{z}}_{s}:s\in S_{t}\}

for a subset
S_{t}\subset\{1,\ldots,t\}
, then Definition
2.7

guarantees that so does
p_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})
. We set

(D.5)

p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\cdot)=p_{t}(\cdot)+\epsilon{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}.

Note that since
\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\|{\bm{\xi}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}=1
,
(
D.4
) implies also

(D.6)

\frac{1}{n}\,{\mathbb{E}}[\|f_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})-p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<2(\epsilon+\epsilon^{2})\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

(2)

Then given
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}
and

p_{1}^{\epsilon},\ldots,p_{t}^{\epsilon}
, define

\{\tilde{b}_{t+1,s}\}_{s\leq t}
in (
D.2
)
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)}

as in
Definition

2.1
by

(D.7)

\tilde{b}_{t+1,s}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits_{s}p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}],\quad\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}[r+1,s+1]=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[p_{r}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})^{\top}p_{s}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}].

The following lemma shows that the iterates of this auxiliary AMP algorithm are
well-defined and close to the original iterates.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose the conditions of Theorem
2.9
hold.
Then there are constants
C>0
and
\iota(\epsilon)>0
satisfying

\iota(\epsilon)\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0
such that
for the auxiliary AMP algorithm (
D.2
) defined with any

\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, for each
t=1,\ldots,T
,
almost surely for all large
n
,

(D.8)

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\iota(\epsilon),\qquad\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}<\iota(\epsilon),\qquad\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}<C.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
t
the following claims, for constants
C>0
and

\iota(\epsilon)>0

satisfying
\iota(\epsilon)\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0
:

(1)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\|_{2}<\iota(\epsilon)
and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\|_{2}<C
almost surely for all large

n
;

(2)

\max_{s=1}^{t-1}|b_{ts}-\tilde{b}_{ts}|<\iota(\epsilon)
;

(3)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}<\iota(\epsilon)

and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}<C
almost surely for all large
n
;

(4)

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\iota(\epsilon)
.

For the base case
t=1
, (1) holds by the bounds

n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
,

n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}-\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2n^{-1}\|p_{0}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+2\epsilon^{2}n^{-1}\|{\bm{\xi}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}
,
and a standard chi-squared tail bound for
\|{\bm{\xi}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}
.
(2) is vacuous. Since
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1}={\mathbf{W}}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
,
(3) holds by (1) and the operator norm bound
\|{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<3

a.s. for all large
n
. (4) holds by (1) and the definitions

{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}=n^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}=n^{-1}\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}
.

Now suppose inductively that statements (1–4) all hold for
1,\ldots,t
, where

t\leq T-1
. We
write
C>0
and
\iota(\epsilon)>0
for constants changing from instance to
instance, where
\iota(\epsilon)\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0
.
To check (1) for iteration
t+1
, observe from the
definition of
{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}
that

(D.9)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|f_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-f_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|f_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}.

The first term of (
D.9
)
is at most
\iota(\epsilon)
a.s. for all large
n

by the Lipschitz condition (
2.23
) and the induction hypothesis.
For the second term, note that for any
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
with degrees bounded
independently of
n
, Definition
2.7
ensures that

\{p_{0},\ldots,p_{t},q_{1},q_{2}\}
is BCP-representable.
Then by Corollary
C.7
,

(D.10)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}q_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})^{\top}q_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[q_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})^{\top}q_{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]=0\text{ a.s.}

Then condition (2) of Definition
2.7
further ensures that

(D.11)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\|f_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}<\epsilon\text{ a.s.,}

so (
D.4
) and the statement

n^{-1}\|p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}<\iota(\epsilon)

a.s. for all large
n
together imply that the second
term of (
D.9
) is at most
\iota(\epsilon)
. Thus

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}\|_{2}<\iota(\epsilon)
a.s. for
all large
n
. The bound

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}\|_{2}<C
follows directly from
the Lipschitz condition (
2.23
) and the induction hypothesis.

For (2), let
S_{t}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,t\}
be the subset for which

f_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\equiv f_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{S_{t}})

and
p_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\equiv p_{t}({\mathbf{z}}_{S_{t}})
depend only on

{\mathbf{z}}_{S_{t}}=\{{\mathbf{z}}_{s}:s\in S_{t}\}
. Note that for each
s\notin S_{t}
, we have

b_{t+1,s}=\tilde{b}_{t+1,s}=0
. For
s\in S_{t}
, by definition we have

(D.12)

\displaystyle(b_{t+1,s}-\tilde{b}_{t+1,s})_{s\in S_{t}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}[\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits_{s}f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})]-\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}[\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\nolimits_{s}p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]\bigg)_{s\in S_{t}}

(D.13)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg({\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}[i]}f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]]-{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{s}[i]}p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]\bigg)_{s\in S_{t}}

For
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, the induction hypothesis and given
condition
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}])>c
imply that both

{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]
are non-singular
a.s. for all large
n
.
Then, applying Stein’s lemma (Lemma
F.3
) to each function

f_{t}(\cdot)[i]
and
p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\cdot)[i]
, we have

(D.14)

\displaystyle(b_{t+1,s}-\tilde{b}_{t+1,s})_{s\in S_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]]-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]\bigg).

For
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, the induction hypothesis and condition

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}])>c
imply also

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\iota(\epsilon)
,
and there exists a coupling of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
(independent of
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
such that

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<\iota(\epsilon)
a.s. for
all large
n
. Then, together with the Lipschitz
condition (
2.23
) for
f_{t}
, the approximation bound
(
D.6
), and Cauchy-Schwarz, this implies

(D.15)

\displaystyle\|(b_{t+1,s}-\tilde{b}_{t+1,s})_{s\in S_{t}}\|_{2}

(D.16)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]]\right\|_{2}

(D.17)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt+\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg({\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]f_{t}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]f_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]\bigg)\right\|_{2}

(D.18)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt+\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}[S_{t},S_{t}]^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg({\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]f_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]-{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}}[i]p_{t}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{t}})[i]\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]\bigg)\right\|_{2}<\iota(\epsilon)

for some
\iota(\epsilon)>0
a.s. for all large
n
, establishing (2).

For (3), from the definition of
{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}
,

(D.19)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t+1}\|_{2}

(D.20)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{W}}({\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}-\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1})\|_{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\Big(|b_{t+1,s}|\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{s}-\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{s}\|_{2}+|b_{t+1,s}-\tilde{b}_{t+1,s}|\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{s}\|_{2}\Big),

so (3) follows from the bound
\|{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<3
a.s. for all large
n

and (1) and (2) already shown.

For (4), the entries of
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
are given by

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[f_{s}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})^{\top}f_{r}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:r})]
, while those of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t+1}
are given by

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[p_{s}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:s})^{\top}p_{r}^{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:r})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]
.
The induction hypothesis implies that there exists a coupling of

{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
(independent of
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
) with
\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
for which

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<\iota(\epsilon)
.
Then (4) follows this coupling,
the Lipschitz condition (
2.23
) for
f_{t}
, the approximation bound
(
D.6
), and Cauchy-Schwarz, analogous to the above
argument for (2). This completes the induction.
∎

We now prove Theorem
2.9
and Corollary

2.10
.

Proof of
Theorem

2.9
.

Let
{\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T}
denote the iterates of the given AMP algorithm.
Fixing the constant
C_{0}>0
satisfying (
D.1
)
and any
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small,
let
\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{T}

denote the iterates of the auxiliary AMP algorithm (
D.2
).
We write
C>0
and
\iota(\epsilon)>0
for constants changing from instance to
instance, where
\iota(\epsilon)\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0
.

We may decompose

(D.21)

\displaystyle\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})

\displaystyle=[\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})]+[\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]]

(D.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt+[\mathbb{E}[\phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]-\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})].

For the first term of (
D.22
),
since both
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
defining
\phi
satisfy the Lipschitz condition
(
2.23
), we have

(D.23)

\displaystyle|\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})|

(D.24)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\bigg|

(D.25)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}\cdot\|\phi_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n}\|\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}\cdot\|\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}

(D.26)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\bigg(\sqrt{n}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}\bigg)

for a constant
C>0
depending on
L
.
Then by
Lemma

D.1
,

(D.27)

\displaystyle|\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})|<\iota(\epsilon)\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

For the second term of (
D.22
),
let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{T}
be the polynomials guaranteed by

Definition

2.7
for which

(D.28)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\,{\mathbb{E}}[\|\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<\epsilon,\quad\frac{1}{n}\,{\mathbb{E}}[\|\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<\epsilon

almost surely for all large
n
.
Writing
\psi=n^{-1}\psi_{1}^{\top}\psi_{2}
, let us further decompose

(D.29)

\displaystyle\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]

\displaystyle=[\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})]+[\psi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}[\psi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]]

(D.30)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt+[\mathbb{E}[\psi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]].

For the first term of (
D.30
),
we apply the same decomposition as above to get

(D.31)

\displaystyle|\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})|

(D.32)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\frac{1}{n}\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\frac{1}{n}\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}\psi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\bigg|

(D.33)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}\cdot\|\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n}\|\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}\cdot\|\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}.

We will apply (
D.28
) to further bound the right side.
To do so, note that by Definition
2.7
,

\{p_{0},\ldots,p_{T-1},q_{1},q_{2}\}
is BCP-representable
for any
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{T}
of degrees bounded independently of
n
.
Then by Corollary
C.7
,

(D.34)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}q_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})^{\top}q_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}[q_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})^{\top}q_{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]

\displaystyle=0\text{ a.s.}

Then condition (2) of Definition
2.7
ensures

(D.35)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\|\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}^{2}<\epsilon\text{ a.s.},

and the same holds with
\phi_{2},\psi_{2}
in place of
\phi_{1},\psi_{1}
.
It then follows from (
D.28
) that
almost surely for all large
n
,

(D.36)

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\|\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}^{2},\frac{1}{n}\|\phi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}<\iota(\epsilon).

Moreover,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|\phi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}<C
a.s. for all
large
n
by the Lipschitz property (
2.23
) for
\phi_{1}
and

Lemma

D.1
, and similarly for
\phi_{2}
.
Combining this with (
D.36
), also

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|\psi_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})\|_{2}<C
a.s. for all large
n
, and
similarly for
\psi_{2}
.
Then, applying these bounds to (
D.33
),

(D.37)

\displaystyle|\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\psi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})|<\iota(\epsilon)\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

For the second term of (
D.30
),
we have from Corollary
C.7
that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\psi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}[\psi(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]=0
.
The third term of (
D.30
) is bounded via
(
D.28
) and an argument analogous to the preceding argument
for the first term.
Combining these bounds for the three terms of (
D.30
), we obtain
for the second term of (
D.22
) that

(D.38)

\displaystyle|\phi(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]|<\iota(\epsilon)\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

Finally, for the third term of (
D.22
), we note that
the bound
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\iota(\epsilon)
of Lemma

D.1
implies there exists a coupling of

{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}
(independent of
{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}
) with
\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}
such that

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]<\iota(\epsilon)
. Applying
this coupling, the Lipschitz condition (
2.23
) for
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}

defining
\phi=n^{-1}\phi_{1}^{\top}\phi_{2}
,
and Cauchy-Schwarz, we obtain that

(D.39)

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})\mid{\bm{\xi}}_{1:T}]-\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})|<\iota(\epsilon)\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

Collecting (
D.22
), (
D.27
),
(
D.38
), and (
D.39
), we have

(D.40)

\displaystyle|\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})|<\iota(\epsilon)\text{ a.s.\ for all
large }n.

Since
\epsilon>0
is arbitrary,
this implies
\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})=0

a.s. as desired.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2.10
.

Denote the AMP algorithm defined by
\{\bar{b}_{ts}\}
as

(D.41)

\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}={\mathbf{W}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\bar{b}_{ts}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{s},\qquad\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}=f_{t}(\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}),

with initialization
\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{1}={\mathbf{u}}_{1}
. Using
\|{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<3
a.s. for
all large
n
and the Lipschitz condition (
2.23
) for
f_{t}(\cdot)
,
a straightforward induction on
t
(omitted for brevity) shows that for each

t=1,\ldots,T
,

–

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\|_{2}=0
a.s. and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\|_{2}<C
a.s. for all large
n
.

–

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}=0
a.s. and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{2}<C
a.s. for all large
n
.

Then, applying the Lipschitz condition (
2.23
) for
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}

and Cauchy-Schwarz, also

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\phi(\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})=0
a.s. Letting

\bar{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}
have i.i.d. rows with distribution
{\mathcal{N}}(0,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T})
,
since
\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}=0
,
there is a coupling of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}
with
\bar{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}-\bar{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\to 0
a.s.
Applying this coupling, the condition (
2.23
) for
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
,
and Cauchy-Schwarz again, we have also

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\phi({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}\phi(\bar{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})=0
. Thus

(D.42)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi(\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})-\mathbb{E}\phi(\bar{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:T})=0\text{ a.s.}

∎

Appendix E
Verification of BCP-representability and BCP-approximability

In this section, we verify the conditions of BCP-representability and
BCP-approximability for the three function classes of Section

2.3
.
We prove Proposition
2.14
in Appendix
E.1
,
Proposition
2.17
in Appendix
E.2
,
and Proposition
2.19
in Appendix
E.3
.

E.1.
Local functions

Recall the classes of polynomial and Lipschitz local functions from
Definitions
2.12
and
2.13
.
We first show Proposition
2.14
(a),
that a set
{\mathcal{P}}
of polynomial local functions is BCP-representable,
via the following lemma.

Lemma E.1
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
is a class of tensors such that for a
constant
C_{0}>0
, every
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
satisfies the condition, for each fixed
position
\ell\in[k]
and fixed index
j\in[n]
,

(E.1)

\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell+1},\ldots,i_{k}=1}^{n}|{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell-1},j,i_{\ell+1},\ldots,i_{k}]|<C_{0}.

(For
k=1
, this means
|{\mathbf{T}}[j]|<C_{0}
for each
j\in[n]
.)
Then for any connected tensor network
(G,{\mathcal{L}})
with tensors
in
{\mathcal{T}}
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
G
and
C_{0}
such
that

(E.2)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq Cn.

In particular,
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be any tensor labeling of
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
with tensors
\{{\mathbf{T}}_{v}:v\in{\mathcal{V}}\}
belonging to
{\mathcal{T}}
. We apply the upper bound

(E.3)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}}|{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\left[i_{e}:e\sim v\right]|.

To analyze this bound, we may reduce to the case where
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
is a
connected tree:
If
{\mathcal{E}}
contains a cycle, pick any edge
e=(u,v)\in{\mathcal{E}}
of the
cycle, and replace the sum over the shared index
i_{e}\in[n]
of
{\mathbf{T}}_{u}
and

{\mathbf{T}}_{v}
in (
E.3
)
by sums over two distinct indices
i_{e^{\prime}}\in[n]
for
{\mathbf{T}}_{u}

and
i_{e^{\prime\prime}}\in[n]
for
{\mathbf{T}}_{v}
. This does not decrease the upper bound,
as the terms with
i_{e^{\prime}}=i_{e^{\prime\prime}}
correspond precisely to
(
E.3
) and the additional terms with
i_{e^{\prime}}\neq i_{e^{\prime\prime}}
are
non-negative. The resulting bound corresponds to (
E.3
)
for a graph in which we add vertices
w,x
with the all-1’s label

{\bf 1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, add edges
e^{\prime}=(u,w)
and
e^{\prime\prime}=(v,x)
, and remove the edge

(u,v)
. Repeating this process until the resulting graph is a tree, and
replacing
{\mathcal{T}}
by
{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{{\bf 1}\}
(where
{\bf 1}
also
satisfies the condition (
E.1
) for
k=1
), it suffices to
bound (
E.3
) when
G
is a connected tree.

In the case where
G
is a connected tree, pick any leaf vertex
u
and suppose

u
is connected to
v
via the edge
e=(u,v)
. Let
{\mathcal{E}}_{v}
denote the set of
all edges incident to
v
.
Then we may remove
e
and contract
u,v
into a single vertex

w
, labeled by the contracted tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
having entries

(E.4)

{\mathbf{T}}_{w}[i_{e^{\prime}}:e^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{v}\setminus e]=\sum_{i_{e}=1}^{n}|{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i_{e}]|\cdot|{\mathbf{T}}_{u}[i_{e},\,i_{e}^{\prime}:e^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{v}\setminus e|.

We note that the condition (
E.1
) for
{\mathbf{T}}_{v}
implies

|{\mathbf{T}}_{v}[i]|\leq C_{0}
for
all
i\in[n]
. Then the condition (
E.1
)
holds with the constant
C_{0}^{2}
for
{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
, since it holds with
C_{0}

for for
{\mathbf{T}}_{u}
.
Denoting by
G^{\prime}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
the contracted tree graph with
u,v
replaced by

w
, (
E.3
) becomes

(E.5)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}}|{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\left[i_{e}:e\sim v\right]|

where each
\{{\mathbf{T}}_{v}:v\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}\}
satisfies (
E.1
) with
constant
C_{0}^{2}
. Iterating this contraction procedure until
G^{\prime}
has only two
vertices
w,x
, we obtain

(E.6)

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|{\mathbf{T}}_{w}[i]|\cdot|{\mathbf{T}}_{x}[i]|

where
{\mathbf{T}}_{w},{\mathbf{T}}_{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
have all entries bounded by
a constant depending only on
C_{0}
and
G
. This shows

|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq Cn
.

By Definition
C.2
,
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP if

\sup_{\mathcal{L}}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq Cn
where the supremum is taken
over all
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labelings
{\mathcal{L}}
of certain
bipartite multigraphs
G=({\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T},{\mathcal{E}})
. The identity tensor

\mathrm{Id}
of any order trivially satisfies the condition
(
E.1
), so the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}
follows from
the above bound applied to

{\mathcal{T}}\cup\{\mathrm{Id}^{1},\ldots,\mathrm{Id}^{k}\}
where
k
is the maximum vertex degree
of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.14
(a).

Let
{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
where
{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
consists of the functions

p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Letting
D,B>0
be the degree and coefficient bounds of
Definition
2.12
, any
p\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
admits a
representation (
2.9
) with this value of
D
, where
each entry of
{\mathbf{T}}^{(0)},{\mathbf{T}}^{(\sigma)}
is a coefficient of
p
and hence has
magnitude at most
B
. Let
{\mathcal{T}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{D+1}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
be the set of all
tensors arising in this representation for all
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
.
For any
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
, the locality condition implies that for
each fixed output index
i\in[n]
, we have

(E.7)

\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1}=1}^{n}|{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1},i]|=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1}\in A_{i}}|{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1},i]|\leq A^{k-1}\cdot B

where
A\geq|A_{i}|
for every
i\in[n]
.
Then also fixing the first input index
j\in[n]
,

(E.8)

\sum_{i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}=1}^{n}|{\mathbf{T}}[j,i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}]|=\sum_{i:j\in A_{i}}\sum_{i_{2},\ldots,i_{k-1}\in A_{i}}|{\mathbf{T}}[j,i_{2},\ldots,i_{k-1},i]|\leq A^{k-1}\cdot B

where also
A\geq|\{i:j\in A_{i}\}|
for every
j\in[n]
. Since
A,B
are
constants independent of
n
,
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP by Lemma

E.1
, so
{\mathcal{P}}
is BCP-representable.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.14
(b).

Let
{\mathcal{F}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, where
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
consists
of the functions
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Given any
C_{0},\epsilon>0
in Definition
2.7
,
let
\zeta,\iota>0
be constants depending on
L,C_{0},\epsilon
to be specified
later. We will track explicitly the dependence of our bounds
on
\zeta,\iota
, and write
C,C^{\prime},c>0
for constants changing from instance
to instance that
do not
depend on
\zeta,\iota
.

We first construct a set of polynomial local functions
{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
to verify condition (1) in
Definition

2.7
.
For
t=0
and each constant vector
f=(\mathring{f}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
,
we simply include
p=f
in
{\mathcal{P}}_{0}
, where
p
has degree 0 and bounded entries
by the condition (2) of Definition
2.13
.
For
t=1,\ldots,T
and each
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, we construct an
approximating polynomial
p
to include in
{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
via the following two steps:

(i)

For each
a=0,1,\ldots,A
, define

(E.9)

\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{a}=\{\mathring{f}:{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}:\mathring{f}\text{ is
$L$-Lipschitz with }|\mathring{f}(0)|\leq L\}.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\subseteq\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{a}
be a
\zeta
-net under the sup-norm over
the Euclidean ball of radius
1/\zeta^{2}
, i.e., for any

\mathring{f}\in\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{a}
, there
exists
\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
such that

(E.10)

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}:\|{\mathbf{x}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq(1/\zeta)^{2}}|\mathring{g}({\mathbf{x}})-\mathring{f}({\mathbf{x}})|^{2}<\zeta.

The definitions and cardinalities of
{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
depend only on

L,\zeta,a,t
and are independent of
n
.
For each
i\in[n]
, let
\mathring{g}_{i}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{|A_{i}|}
be the net
approximation for
\mathring{f}_{i}
satisfying (
E.10
),
and define
g=(\mathring{g}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
.

(ii)

For each
a=0,1,\ldots,A
and each
\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
, let

\mathring{p}:{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a polynomial function that
approximates
\mathring{g}
in the sense

(E.11)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})}[|\mathring{g}({\mathbf{Z}})-\mathring{p}({\mathbf{Z}})|^{2}]<\iota

for every
{\bm{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}
satisfying
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
.
We may construct this approximation as follows: First fixing any
\delta>0
,
Lemma
F.6
implies there exists a polynomial

\mathring{p}:{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
which satisfies

(E.12)

\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{a\times t}}e^{-\sum_{i,j}|{\mathbf{z}}[i,j]|^{3/2}}|\mathring{g}({\mathbf{z}})-\mathring{p}({\mathbf{z}})|\leq\delta.

Then, for any
{\bm{\Sigma}}
with
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
, letting

{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}
,

(E.13)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[|\mathring{g}({\mathbf{Z}})-\mathring{p}({\mathbf{Z}})|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}[|\mathring{g}({\mathbf{Z}})-\mathring{p}({\mathbf{Z}})|^{2}>x]dx

(E.14)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\sum_{i,j}|{\mathbf{Z}}[i,j]|^{3/2}>\log(x^{1/2}/\delta)\bigg]dx

(E.15)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}2\delta^{2}y\cdot\mathbb{P}\bigg[\sum_{i,j}|{\mathbf{Z}}[i,j]|^{3/2}>\log y\bigg]dy<C\delta^{2}

for a constant
C>0
depending only on
C_{0},a,t
. Then choosing

\delta\equiv\delta(\iota)>0
small enough
ensures (
E.11
). We note that
for each
\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
, the construction of this polynomial

\mathring{p}
depends only on
\iota,C_{0},a,t
and is again independent of
n
.

Letting
g=(\mathring{g}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be the construction of step (i), we set

\mathring{p}_{i}
to be this approximation of
\mathring{g}_{i}

that satisfies (
E.11
), and
include
p=(\mathring{p}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
in
{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
.

The components of
p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

constructed in this way are independent of
n
, and hence the maximum degree of

p
and maximum magnitude of its coefficients are also independent of
n
.
By definition,
p
satisfies the same locality condition as
f
.
Thus
{\mathcal{P}}
is a set of polynomial local functions in the
sense of Definition
2.12
, which is BCP-representable by
Proposition
2.14
(a).

To verify condition (1) of
Definition

2.7
,
it remains to bound the error of the approximation of
f
by
p
.
Let
{\bm{\Sigma}}
satisfy

\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
, and let
{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}
. Denoting

{\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|A_{i}|\times t}
as the rows of
{\mathbf{Z}}
belonging to
A_{i}
,
we have

(E.16)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|f({\mathbf{Z}})-p({\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{p}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])\right|^{2}\right]

(E.17)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])|^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{p}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])|^{2}\right]

(E.18)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])|^{2}\right]+2\iota

where the last inequality follows from the approximation guarantee
(
E.11
) for each
\mathring{p}_{i}
.
For the first term of (
E.18
),
we split the expectation into two parts based on whether

\|{\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 1/\zeta^{2}
or not, and then
apply the guarantee in (
E.10
)
and definition of the class
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
in (
E.9
)
to get

(E.19)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])|^{2}\cdot\mathop{\mathds{1}}\{\|{\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}>(1/\zeta)^{2}\}\right]+2\zeta

(E.20)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(1+\|{\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})\cdot\mathop{\mathds{1}}\{\|{\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}>(1/\zeta)^{2}\}\right]+2\zeta.

Further applying Cauchy-Schwarz and Markov’s inequality to bound the first term, we obtain

(E.21)

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{Z}}[A_{i}])|^{2}\right]\leq C^{\prime}\zeta.

Choosing
\zeta,\iota>0
small enough depending on
\epsilon
,
the resulting bound of (
E.18
) is at most
\epsilon
.
Thus
{\mathcal{P}}
satisfies condition (1) of
Definition

2.7
.

We next verify condition (2) of
Definition

2.7
. Let

{\mathcal{Q}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
be the set
of all polynomial functions
q=(\mathring{q}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
with
coefficients bounded in magnitude by 1 and satisfying the locality condition (1)
of Definition
2.12
.
For any
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
with uniformly bounded degrees, note that

{\mathcal{P}}\cup\{q_{1},q_{2}\}
is a set of polynomial local functions satisfying
Definition
2.12
, and hence remains BCP-representable.
Consider any
{\bm{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}

with
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
and any random
{\mathbf{z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}
satisfying,
for any
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
of uniformly bounded degrees, almost surely

(E.22)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}q_{1}({\mathbf{z}})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{z}})-\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{n})}[q_{1}({\mathbf{Z}})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{Z}})]=0.

To control
\|f({\mathbf{z}})-p({\mathbf{z}})\|_{2}^{2}
, we have

(E.23)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|f({\mathbf{z}})-p({\mathbf{z}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{p}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\big)^{2}

(E.24)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\big)^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{p}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\big)^{2}.

For the first term, applying a similar argument as above,

(E.25)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\zeta+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\Big)^{2}1\!\!1\{\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}>(1/\zeta)^{2}\}

(E.26)

\displaystyle\leq 2\zeta+C\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\!\!1\{\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}>(1/\zeta)^{2}\}\bigg)^{1/2}

(E.27)

\displaystyle\leq 2\zeta+C\zeta\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Applying (
E.22
) with

q_{1}({\mathbf{z}})=q_{2}({\mathbf{z}})=(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})_{i=1}^{n}
, there exists a
constant
C>0
such that

(E.28)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})^{2}<C\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

Then

(E.29)

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathring{f}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\big)^{2}\leq C^{\prime}\zeta\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

For the second term of (
E.24
),
define for each
a=0,1,\ldots,A
and each
\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
the index set

(E.30)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}=\{i\in[n]:|A_{i}|=a\text{ and }\mathring{g}_{i}=\mathring{g}\}.

Clearly
[n]=\sqcup_{a=0}^{A}\sqcup_{\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}
.
Note that there is a common polynomial approximation
\mathring{p}_{i}\equiv p_{\mathring{g}}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}
, so

(E.31)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{p}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a=0}^{A}\sum_{\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\underbrace{\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}}\Big(\mathring{g}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-p_{\mathring{g}}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\Big)^{2}}_{E_{a,\mathring{g}}}.

For each
a=0,1,\ldots,A
and
\mathring{g}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
, we claim that

(E.32)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}E_{a,\mathring{g}}\leq 2\iota\text{ a.s.}

Assuming momentarily this claim, we may apply it
to each pair
(a,\mathring{g})
above to show

(E.33)

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\Big(\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-\mathring{p}_{i}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\Big)^{2}<C(\zeta)\iota\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n

for some constant
C(\zeta)>0
that depends on
\zeta

via the cardinalities
|{\mathcal{N}}_{a}|
for
a=0,1,\ldots,A
.
Applying (
E.29
)
and (
E.33
) to
(
E.24
), and first
choosing
\zeta>0
sufficiently small followed by
\iota>0
sufficiently small
depending on
\zeta
, this is also at most
\epsilon
,
verifying condition (2) of
Definition

2.7
.

To complete the proof, it remains to show the claim (
E.32
).
Suppose by contradiction that there exists
a positive probability event
\Omega
(in the infinite sequence space as
n\to\infty
) on which
\limsup_{n\to\infty}E_{a,\mathring{g}}>2\iota
. Let
D
be
the maximum degree of polynomials in
{\mathcal{P}}
, and let us
consider an event where

(E.34)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2D})^{2}<C\text{ for all
large }n.

This event holds with probability 1 analogously to (
E.28
),
by applying (
E.22
) with

q_{1}({\mathbf{z}})=q_{2}({\mathbf{z}})=(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2D})_{i=1}^{n}
.
Let us consider also the class of test functions

q(\cdot)=q_{1}(\cdot)^{\top}q_{2}(\cdot)
where
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
are given by

(E.35)

\displaystyle q_{1}({\mathbf{z}})[i]=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\qquad q_{2}({\mathbf{z}})[i]=\begin{cases}\mathring{q}&\text{if $i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

and
\mathring{q}:{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a fixed monomial (of arbitrary
multivariate degree) with
coefficient 1. Then the event where (
E.22
) holds
for
q(\cdot)=q_{1}(\cdot)^{\top}q_{2}(\cdot)
defined by each such monomial

\mathring{q}:{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}
also has probability 1, as the set of such
monomials
\mathring{q}
is countable. Letting
\Omega^{\prime}
be the intersection of

\Omega
with these two probability-1 events,
\Omega^{\prime}
must be non-empty.

For any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, let
\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
be a (random,

\omega
-dependent) subsequence for which
E_{a,\mathring{g}}>2\iota
for each

n_{j}
. Since
|{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}|/n\in[0,1]
and since
{\bm{\Sigma}}
belongs to
a fixed compact domain, passing to a further subsequence, we may assume that
along this subsequence
\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
, we have

|{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}|/n_{j}\to\alpha
for some
\alpha\in[0,1]

and
{\bm{\Sigma}}\to\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}
for some
\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}
as

n_{j}\to\infty
. If
\alpha=0
, then
using the condition (
E.9
) for

\mathring{g}
and the fact that
p_{\mathring{g}}
has degree at most
D

and coefficients of magnitude at most
B
for some constants
D,B>0
,
for a constant
C(L,B,D)>0
we have

(E.36)

\displaystyle E_{a,\mathring{g}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(L,B,D)}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}}(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2D})

(E.37)

\displaystyle\leq C(L,B,D)\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1+\|{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2D})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\frac{|{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}|}{n}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Applying
\alpha=0
and the bound (
E.34
),
we have
E_{a,\mathring{g}}\to 0
along the subsequence

\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
, contradicting
E_{a,\mathring{g}}>2\iota
for each
n_{j}
.
If instead
\alpha>0
, then the statement (
E.22
) for each
function
q_{1}(\cdot)^{\top}q_{2}(\cdot)
in the class
(
E.35
), together with the convergence
{\bm{\Sigma}}\to\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}
,
imply

(E.38)

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot\lim_{n_{j}\to\infty}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}|}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}}\mathring{q}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])

\displaystyle=\lim_{n_{j}\to\infty}\frac{1}{n_{j}}\,q_{1}({\mathbf{z}})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{z}})

(E.39)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n_{j}\to\infty}\frac{1}{n_{j}}\mathbb{E}[q_{1}({\mathbf{Z}})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{Z}})]=\alpha\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})}[\mathring{q}({\mathbf{Z}})].

This holds for each fixed monomial
\mathring{q}:{\mathbb{R}}^{a\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
so the empirical distribution of

\{{\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}]\}_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}}
converges to

{\mathcal{N}}(0,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
weakly and in Wasserstein-
k
for every
order
k\in[1,\infty)
(c.f.
[
13
, Theorem 30.2]

and
[
67
, Definition 6.8, Theorem 6.9]
).
Since
\mathring{g}-p_{\mathring{g}}
is a fixed continuous function of polynomial
growth, this then implies

(E.40)

\displaystyle\lim_{n_{j}\to\infty}E_{a,\mathring{g}}

\displaystyle=\alpha\cdot\lim_{n_{j}\to\infty}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}|}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{a,\mathring{g}}}\Big(\mathring{g}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])-p_{\mathring{g}}({\mathbf{z}}[A_{i}])\Big)^{2}=\alpha\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})}\Big[\Big(\mathring{g}(\bar{\mathbf{Z}})-p_{\mathring{g}}(\bar{\mathbf{Z}})\Big)^{2}\Big],

which is at most
\iota
by the bound
\alpha\leq 1
and
the approximation guarantee
(
E.11
) for
p_{\mathring{g}}
.
This again contradicts
E_{a,\mathring{g}}>2\iota
for each
n_{j}
.
Thus (
E.32
) holds, concluding the proof.
∎

E.2.
Anisotropic functions

We recall the classes of polynomial and Lipschitz anisotropic functions
from Definitions
2.15
and
2.16
.

{\mathbf{D}}^{(\sigma)}
{\mathbf{K}}
{\mathbf{K}}
{\mathbf{K}}
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
{\mathbf{T}}^{(\sigma)}
=

Figure 4
.
An example of a tensor
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}
for the class of polynomial
anisotropic functions.

Proof of Proposition
2.17
(a).

Let
{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
, where
{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
consists of the functions

p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Consider any
p\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
given by

(E.41)

p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})={\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}q({\mathbf{K}}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}),

where
q:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is separable with degree at most
D
and
all entries bounded in magnitude by
B
. Then
q
admits a representation
of the form (
2.9
),

(E.42)

q({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})={\mathbf{D}}^{(0)}+\sum_{d=1}^{D}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{S}}_{t,d}}{\mathbf{D}}^{(\sigma)}[{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot]

where each tensor
{\mathbf{D}}^{(0)},{\mathbf{D}}^{(\sigma)}
has entries bounded in magnitude by

B
and is diagonal because
q
is separable. So
p
admits the representation
(
2.9
), where

(E.43)

{\mathbf{T}}^{(0)}={\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{D}}^{(0)}

and
{\mathbf{T}}^{(\sigma)}
for each
\sigma\in{\mathcal{S}}_{t,d}

is a contraction of
{\mathbf{D}}^{(\sigma)}
with
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{K}}
in each dimension,
having entries

(E.44)

{\mathbf{T}}^{(\sigma)}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d+1}]=\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbf{D}}^{(\sigma)}[j,\ldots,j]{\mathbf{K}}[i_{1},j]\ldots{\mathbf{K}}[i_{d},j]{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}[i_{d+1},j]

This is visualized in Figure
4
. We let
{\mathcal{T}}
be the set of all
such tensors
{\mathbf{T}}^{(0)},{\mathbf{T}}^{(\sigma)}
arising in this representation for all

p\in{\mathcal{P}}
. Then the cardinality of
|{\mathcal{T}}|
is bounded independently of

n
, by the boundedness of
|{\mathcal{P}}|
and of the degree of each
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
.

By Definition
C.2
,
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP if

\sup_{\mathcal{L}}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq Cn
for any connected bipartite multigraph

G=({\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T},{\mathcal{E}})
such that each
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
has even degree,
where the supremum is over all
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labelings
{\mathcal{L}}
of
G
.
In light of the forms (
E.43
) and (
E.44
), we see that any
such value
\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})
has a form

(E.45)

\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{j}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e}:e\sim v]\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}}\mathrm{Id}^{\deg(u)}[i_{e}:e\sim u]\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathbf{K}}_{e}[i_{e},j_{e}]

where
{\mathbf{D}}_{v}
is one of the above diagonal tensors
{\mathbf{D}}^{(0)},{\mathbf{D}}^{(\sigma)}

for each
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, and
{\mathbf{K}}_{e}
is a matrix in
{\mathcal{K}}
for each
e\in{\mathcal{E}}
.
Under condition (1) where
\|{\mathbf{K}}\|_{\ell_{\infty}\to\ell_{\infty}}=\max_{i}\sum_{j}|{\mathbf{K}}[i,j]|
and
\|{\mathbf{K}}^{\top}\|_{\ell_{\infty}\to\ell_{\infty}}
are uniformly bounded by a constant over
{\mathbf{K}}\in{\mathcal{K}}
,
all tensors in (
E.45
) satisfy the property (
E.1
).
Then
\sup_{{\mathcal{L}}}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\leq Cn
by Lemma
E.1
,
implying that
{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the BCP.

Under condition (2), let
\bar{\mathcal{T}}
be an independent copy of
{\mathcal{T}}
where the
orthogonal matrices
{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}}
