defining
{\mathcal{K}}
are replaced by independent copies

\bar{\mathbf{O}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}
. Given any
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labeling
{\mathcal{L}}
of

G
, denote by
\bar{\mathcal{L}}
the labeling that replaces each label
{\mathbf{T}}\in{\mathcal{T}}
by
its corresponding copy
\bar{\mathbf{T}}\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}
, and write
\mathbb{E}
for the expectation
over
{\mathbf{O}},\bar{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}
. We claim that for any fixed connected
multigraph
G
where all vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
have even degree,

(E.46)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{L}}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]|

\displaystyle\leq Cn

(E.47)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{L}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}})\big)^{4}\big]

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{2}

for a
G
-dependent constant
C>0
, where the suprema are over all

(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labelings
{\mathcal{L}}
.
Assuming momentarily this claim, we then have by Markov’s inequality and
Jensen’s inequality that

(E.48)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\geq(C+1)n]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|\geq n]

(E.49)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}))^{4}]}{n^{4}}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}]}{n^{4}}\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}.

The set of
(\mathrm{Id},{\mathcal{T}})
-labelings of
G
has cardinality bounded by a constant
independent of
n
, by the boundedness of
|{\mathcal{T}}|
. Then taking a union bound,

\mathbb{P}[\sup_{\mathcal{L}}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|>C^{\prime}n]\leq C^{\prime}/n^{2}
for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
.
So by the Borel-Cantelli lemma,
\sup_{\mathcal{L}}|\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})|<C^{\prime}n

almost surely for all large
n
, implying that the BCP holds almost surely
for
{\mathcal{T}}
.

To conclude the proof, it remains to show
(
E.46
–
E.47
). Since
{\mathbf{O}},\bar{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}

are assumed independent, whose densities with respect to Haar measure are
bounded above by a constant, by a change of measure it suffices
to show (
E.46
–
E.47
) in the case where

{\mathbf{O}},\bar{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}
are independent Haar-orthogonal matrices.
We provide an argument that extends the ideas of
[
69
, Appendix C]

using the orthogonal Weingarten calculus: Fix any set
{\mathcal{E}}
of even cardinality,
and let
{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{j}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}
be any two index tuples. Let

{\mathbf{O}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
be a Haar-distributed orthogonal matrix.
Then (c.f.
[
21
, Corollary 3.4]
)

(E.50)

\mathbb{E}\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathbf{O}}[i_{e},j_{e}]=\mathop{\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi,\,\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi^{\prime}}\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})

Here

–

\mathscr{P}
is the lattice of partitions of
{\mathcal{E}}

endowed with the partial ordering
\pi\geq\tau
if
\tau
refines
\pi

(i.e. each block of
\pi
is the union of one or more blocks of
\tau
).

–

\pi,\pi^{\prime}
are pairings in
\mathscr{P}
, i.e. partitions of
{\mathcal{E}}

where each block has size 2.

–

\pi({\mathbf{i}})\in\mathscr{P}
is the partition where
e,e^{\prime}
belong to
the same block of
\pi({\mathbf{i}})
if and only if
i_{e}=i_{e^{\prime}}
.
Thus
\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi
for a pairing
\pi

means
i_{e}=i_{e^{\prime}}
for each pair
(e,e^{\prime})\in\pi
.

–

\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is the orthogonal Weingarten function, admitting
an asymptotic expansion

(E.51)

\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=n^{-|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2}\Big(\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})-n^{-1}\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+O(n^{-2})\Big)

where
\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
and
\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
do not depend on

n
, and
O(n^{-2})
denotes an error at most
Cn^{-2}
for a constant

C\equiv C(|{\mathcal{E}}|,\pi,\pi^{\prime})>0

and all large
n
. Here
d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is a metric on
\mathscr{P}
given by

(E.52)

d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=|\pi|+|\pi^{\prime}|-2|\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}|

where
|\pi|
is the number of blocks of
\pi
, and
\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}
is the join (i.e. least upper bound in
\mathscr{P}
).
For the equivalence between this and the
\ell(\cdot,\cdot)
metric
of
[
21
]
, see
[
69
, Appendix C]
.

–

\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}),\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}),\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})

depend on
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
only via the sizes of the blocks of
\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}
.
Writing these sizes as
2k_{1},2k_{2},\ldots,2k_{M}
(which must all be even),

(E.53)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{k_{m}-1}c_{k_{m}-1},

(E.54)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{k_{m}-1}a_{k_{m}-1}\mathop{\prod_{m^{\prime}=1}^{M}}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}(-1)^{k_{m^{\prime}}-1}c_{k_{m^{\prime}}-1},

where
c_{k}
is the
k^{\text{th}}
Catalan number,
a_{k}
is the total area
under the set of all Dyck paths of length
k
, and we note that
\prod_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{k_{m}-1}=(-1)^{|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-M}=(-1)^{d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2}
. This form of
\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}
is
shown in
[
21
, Theorem 3.13]
, of
\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}

in
[
33
, Theorem 3.13]
, and we refer to

[
20
, Theorem 4.6, Lemmas 4.12 and 4.13]
for a summary.

To show (
E.46
), further expanding
{\mathbf{K}}_{e}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{D}}_{e}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
,
we may express (
E.45
) as

(E.55)

\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})=\sum_{{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{j}},{\mathbf{k}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e}:e\sim v]\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}}\mathrm{Id}^{\deg(u)}[i_{e}:e\sim u]\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathbf{O}}[i_{e},k_{e}]{\mathbf{D}}_{e}[k_{e},k_{e}]{\mathbf{U}}[j_{e},k_{e}].

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be the set of edges of
G
, which has even cardinality because each
vertex of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
has even degree.
Let
\mathscr{P}
be the lattice of partitions of
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Let
\pi_{T},\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\in\mathscr{P}
be the
two distinguished partitions where
e,e^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
belong to the same block
of
\pi_{T}
(or of
\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}
) if they are incident to the same vertex of
{\mathcal{V}}_{T}

(resp. of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}
); thus
|\pi_{T}|=|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|
and
|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|
.
For each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}
, we write
e(v)\in{\mathcal{E}}

for an arbitrary choice of edge incident to this vertex.
Then, since
{\mathbf{D}}_{v}
and
\mathrm{Id}^{\deg(u)}
are diagonal,
(
E.55
) is further equivalent to

(E.56)

\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})=\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{j}},{\mathbf{k}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi_{\mathrm{Id}},\,\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e(v)},\ldots,j_{e(v)}]\times\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}}1\times\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathbf{O}}[i_{e},k_{e}]{\mathbf{D}}_{e}[k_{e},k_{e}]{\mathbf{U}}[j_{e},k_{e}].

Evaluating the expectations over
{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{U}}
using (
E.50
),
noting that
\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}
and

\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi
if and only if
\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}\vee\pi
,
and similarly for
\pi({\mathbf{i}})
and
\pi({\mathbf{k}})
, we have

(E.57)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(E.58)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}\vee\pi}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e(v)},\ldots,j_{e(v)}]\times\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\vee\tau}1\times\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}\in[n]^{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{k}})\geq\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}}\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{e}[k_{e},k_{e}].

To show (
E.46
), we will only use the bound

|\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})|\leq O(n^{-|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2})
implied by (
E.51
). Then,
identifying
\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}}:\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}\vee\pi}
as a
summation over a single index
j\in[n]
for each block of
\pi_{T}\vee\pi
,
and similarly for
{\mathbf{i}}
and
{\mathbf{k}}
, and
applying the uniform boundedness of entries of
{\mathbf{D}}_{v}
and
{\mathbf{D}}_{e}
, we have
for a constant
C>0
and all large
n
,

(E.59)

|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]|\leq C\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}n^{-|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2}\,n^{-|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})/2}\,n^{|\pi_{T}\vee\pi|}\,n^{|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\vee\tau|}\,n^{|\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}|}.

Recalling
|\pi_{T}|=|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|
,
|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|
,
and
|\pi|=|\pi^{\prime}|=|\tau|=|\tau^{\prime}|=|{\mathcal{E}}|/2
since these are pairings, we have
by definition (
E.52
) of the metric
d(\cdot,\cdot)
that

(E.60)

|\pi_{T}\vee\pi|=\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-d(\pi_{T},\pi)}{2},\quad|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\vee\tau|=\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|+|{\mathcal{E}}|/2-d(\pi_{\mathrm{Id}},\tau)}{2},\quad|\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}|=\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})}{2}.

We have also
|\pi_{T}\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=1
because
G
is a connected graph, so
by the triangle inequality for
d(\cdot,\cdot)
,

(E.61)

d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})+d(\tau^{\prime},\tau)+d(\tau,\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})\geq d(\pi_{T},\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})=|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|-2.

Applying this above gives

|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})]|\leq C^{\prime}n^{-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}|}{2}-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}|}{2}+\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+|{\mathcal{E}}|/2}{2}+\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|+|{\mathcal{E}}|/2}{2}+\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}|}{2}-\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|-2}{2}}=C^{\prime}n

for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
. This shows (
E.46
).

To show (
E.47
), let
G^{(s)}=({\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{(s)}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{(s)},{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)})
for

s=1,2,3,4
denote four copies of
G
. Let
G^{\sqcup 4}=({\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{\sqcup 4}\sqcup{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{\sqcup 4},{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4})
denote the (disconnected)
graph formed by their disjoint union. We write
\mathscr{P}
for the lattice of
partitions of the combined edge set
{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}
. Let

\pi_{T},\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\in\mathscr{P}
be the partitions where
e,e^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}

belong to the same block of
\pi_{T}
(or of
\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}
) if
e,e^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}

for the same copy
s\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and are incident to the same vertex of

{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{(s)}
(resp. of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{(s)}
).
Thus
\pi_{T}\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}
has 4 blocks which are exactly

{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}
for
s=1,2,3,4
. Letting
e(v)\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}
be an
arbitrary choice of edge containing each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{\sqcup 4}
,
and applying (
E.56
),

(E.62)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}

(E.63)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}(-1)^{|S|}\prod_{s\in S}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})\prod_{s\notin S}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}})

(E.64)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}(-1)^{|S|}\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{j}},{\mathbf{k}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi_{\mathrm{Id}},\,\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}}\bigg(\prod_{s\in S}\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}}{\mathbf{O}}[i_{e},k_{e}]{\mathbf{U}}[j_{e},k_{e}]\prod_{s\notin S}\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}}\bar{\mathbf{O}}[i_{e},k_{e}]\bar{\mathbf{U}}[j_{e},k_{e}]\bigg)

(E.65)

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{\sqcup 4}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e(v)},\ldots,j_{e(v)}]\times\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{\sqcup 4}}1\times\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}{\mathbf{D}}_{e}[k_{e},k_{e}].

We apply (
E.50
) to take expectations over

{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}}
and
\bar{\mathbf{O}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}
separately.
Let
\pi_{S}\in\mathscr{P}
be the partition with the two blocks

(E.66)

{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\equiv\bigcup_{s\in S}{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}\equiv\bigcup_{s\notin S}{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}

(or with a single block if either
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
or
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
is empty).
The application of (
E.50
) to
{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}}
enumerates
over four pairings of
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
, and the application of
(
E.50
) to
\bar{\mathbf{O}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}
enumerates over four pairings of

{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
, which we may combine into four
pairings
\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}
that refine
\pi_{S}
.
For any such pairings
\pi,\pi^{\prime}
,
we write
\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
for the Weingarten function of
the restrictions of
\pi,\pi^{\prime}
to
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
, as partitions of
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
. Then

(E.67)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}

(E.68)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}(-1)^{|S|}\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{j}},{\mathbf{k}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi_{\mathrm{Id}},\,\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}}\mathop{\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}}_{\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\leq\pi_{S},\,\tau\leq\pi({\mathbf{i}}),\,\pi\leq\pi({\mathbf{j}}),\,\tau^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi({\mathbf{k}})}

(E.69)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(E.70)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\times\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{\sqcup 4}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e(v)},\ldots,j_{e(v)}]\times\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}^{\sqcup 4}}1\times\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}{\mathbf{D}}_{e}[k_{e},k_{e}]

(E.71)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}}\Bigg(\mathop{\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}}_{\pi_{S}\geq\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}(-1)^{|S|}\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\Bigg)

(E.72)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{j}})\geq\pi_{T}\vee\pi}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{T}^{\sqcup 4}}{\mathbf{D}}_{v}[j_{e(v)},\ldots,j_{e(v)}]\times\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{i}})\geq\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\vee\tau}1\times\mathop{\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}\in[n]^{{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}}}_{\pi({\mathbf{k}})\geq\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}}\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}{\mathbf{D}}_{e}[k_{e},k_{e}]

(E.73)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathop{\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}}\Bigg|\underbrace{\mathop{\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}}_{\pi_{S}\geq\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}(-1)^{|S|}\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}_{:=W(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})}\Bigg|

(E.74)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\times n^{|\pi_{T}\vee\pi|}\,n^{|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\vee\tau|}\,n^{|\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}|}.

Analogously to (
E.60
), we have

(E.75)

|\pi_{T}\vee\pi|=\frac{4|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+2|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi_{T},\pi)}{2},\quad|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}\vee\tau|=\frac{4|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|+2|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi_{\mathrm{Id}},\pi)}{2},\quad|\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}|=\frac{4|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})}{2},

so the above gives

(E.76)

\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}\leq C\sum_{\text{pairings }\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}}|W(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})|\cdot n^{2|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+2|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|+4|{\mathcal{E}}|-\frac{d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi_{\mathrm{Id}},\tau)+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})}{2}}.

We recall that
|\pi_{T}\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=4
, with the blocks

\{{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}\}_{s=1}^{4}
. We consider three cases for
\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}
:

Case 1:

|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|\leq 2
. Let
\pi|_{S}
and
\pi|_{\bar{S}}
denote the restrictions
of
\pi
to
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
. We apply again the bound

|\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})|\leq O(n^{-|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|/2-d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})/2})
from (
E.51
),
and similarly for
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
.
Since
|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|+|{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}|=4|{\mathcal{E}}|
and

d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})+d(\pi|_{\bar{S}},\pi^{\prime}|_{\bar{S}})=d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
by definition (
E.52
) of the metric

d(\cdot,\cdot)
, this bound gives

(E.77)

|\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})|\leq Cn^{-2|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2},

and similarly for
\tau,\tau^{\prime}
. Then

|W(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})|\leq Cn^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2-d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})/2}
.
Applying this to
(
E.76
),

(E.78)

\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}\leq Cn^{2|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+2|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|-\frac{d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})+d(\tau^{\prime},\tau)+d(\tau,\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})}{2}}

Here, the triangle inequality
d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})+d(\tau^{\prime},\tau)+d(\tau,\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})\geq d(\pi_{T},\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})
is not tight,
because
\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}
are not all refinements of
\pi_{T}\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}
.
We instead apply the following
observations about the metric
d(\cdot,\cdot)
:

–

By the definition (
E.52
), it is direct to check that

d(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})=d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2},\pi_{2})
.

–

Applying this property and the triangle inequality,

(E.79)

\displaystyle d(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{2},\pi_{3})

(E.80)

\displaystyle=d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2},\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{2},\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+d(\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\pi_{3})

(E.81)

\displaystyle\geq d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2},\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+d(\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\pi_{3})

(E.82)

\displaystyle=d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+d(\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\pi_{3})

(E.83)

\displaystyle\geq d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\pi_{3}).

–

Thus

(E.84)

\displaystyle d(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{2},\pi_{3})+\ldots+d(\pi_{k-1},\pi_{k})

(E.85)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\geq d\Big(\pi_{1},\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i}\Big)+d\Big(\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i},\pi_{k}\Big)=|\pi_{1}|+|\pi_{k}|-2\Big|\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i}\Big|.

This may be shown by the above property and induction on
k
:

(E.86)

\displaystyle d(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})+d(\pi_{2},\pi_{3})+\ldots+d(\pi_{k-1},\pi_{k})

(E.87)

\displaystyle\geq d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+\underbrace{d(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\pi_{3})+d(\pi_{3},\pi_{4})+\ldots+d(\pi_{k-1},\pi_{k})}_{\text{apply induction
hypothesis}}

(E.88)

\displaystyle\geq d(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3})+d\Big(\pi_{1}\vee\pi_{2}\vee\pi_{3},\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i}\Big)+d\Big(\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i},\pi_{k}\Big)

(E.89)

\displaystyle\geq d\Big(\pi_{1},\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i}\Big)+d\Big(\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{i},\pi_{k}\Big).

Applying (
E.85
) gives, under our assumption for Case 1,

(E.90)

\displaystyle d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})+d(\tau^{\prime},\tau)+d(\tau,\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})

\displaystyle\geq|\pi_{T}|+|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|-2|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|

(E.91)

\displaystyle\geq 4|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+4|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|-4.

Applying this to (
E.78
) shows

\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}\leq Cn^{2}
as desired.

Case 2:

|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=3
.
In this case we apply the leading order Weingarten expansion, by
(
E.51
),

(E.92)

\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=n^{-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|}{2}-\frac{d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})}{2}}\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+O(n^{-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|}{2}-\frac{d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})}{2}-1}),

and similarly for
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
and
\tau,\tau^{\prime}
. Then

(E.93)

\displaystyle W(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle=n^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-\frac{d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})}{2}-\frac{d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}{2}}\underbrace{\mathop{\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}}_{\pi_{S}\geq\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}(-1)^{|S|}\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}_{:=W^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})}

(E.94)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+O(n^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-\frac{d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})}{2}-\frac{d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}{2}-1}).

By the explicit form in (
E.53
), we see that

\operatorname{Wg}_{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
factorizes across blocks of
\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}
, so

(E.95)

\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\sqcup 4}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})

which does not depend on
S
, and similarly for
\tau,\tau^{\prime}
.
When
|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=3
,
exactly two blocks
{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}
of
\pi_{T}\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}
are merged in this
partition. Supposing without loss of generality that these are

{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)},{\mathcal{E}}^{(2)}
,
then the summation over
S
defining
W^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})
is over all
subsets
S
containing either both
\{1,2\}
or neither
\{1,2\}
,
and we see that
\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}:\pi_{S}\geq\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}(-1)^{|S|}=0
.
Thus
W^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})=0
, so

(E.96)

|W(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})|\leq Cn^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2-d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})-1}.

Under our assumption for Case 2 we have

(E.97)

\displaystyle d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})+d(\tau^{\prime},\tau)+d(\tau,\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})

\displaystyle\geq|\pi_{T}|+|\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|-2|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|

(E.98)

\displaystyle=4|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+4|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|-6,

and applying these bounds in (
E.76
) shows again

\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}\leq Cn^{2}
.

Case 3:

|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=4
.
In this case we apply the sub-leading order Weingarten expansion,
by (
E.51
),

(E.99)

\operatorname{Wg}_{n,{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=n^{-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|}{2}-\frac{d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})}{2}}\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})-n^{-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|}{2}-\frac{d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})}{2}-1}\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+O(n^{-\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}_{S}|}{2}-\frac{d(\pi|_{S},\pi^{\prime}|_{S})}{2}-2}),

and similarly for
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
and
\tau,\tau^{\prime}
. Then

(E.100)

\displaystyle W(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle=n^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-\frac{d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})}{2}-\frac{d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}{2}}W^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})-n^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-\frac{d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})}{2}-\frac{d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}{2}-1}W^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(E.101)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+O(n^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-\frac{d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})}{2}-\frac{d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}{2}-2})

where
W^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})
is as defined in Case 2 above, and

(E.102)

\displaystyle W^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(E.103)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}}}_{\pi_{S}\geq\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}(-1)^{|S|}\Big[\Big(\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\Big)\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(E.104)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\Big(\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(1)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})+\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(1)}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\Big)\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\Big].

Here, for

|\pi_{T}\vee\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}\vee\tau^{\prime}\vee\tau\vee\pi_{\mathrm{Id}}|=4
,
the blocks
{\mathcal{E}}^{(s)}
remain disjoint in this partition,
so the summations defining
W^{(0)}
and
W^{(1)}
are over all subsets

S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}
. Then we still
have
\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}:\pi_{S}\geq\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}(-1)^{|S|}=0
, so
W^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})=0
as in Case 3 above.
For
W^{(1)}
, letting

2k_{1},\ldots,2k_{M}
be the sizes of the blocks of
|\pi\vee\pi^{\prime}|
, we have
from (
E.53
) and (
E.54
) that

(E.105)

\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{k_{m}-1}a_{k_{m}-1}\mathop{\prod_{m^{\prime}=1}^{M}}_{m^{\prime}\neq m}(-1)^{k_{m^{\prime}}-1}c_{k_{m^{\prime}}-1},

where the summands corresponding to blocks
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
belonging to

{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
come from the second term

\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
,
and those for blocks belong to
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}
come from the first term

\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}^{(0)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{Wg}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bar{S}}}^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
.
This quantity again does not depend on
S
, and similarly for
\tau,\tau^{\prime}
.
Thus
W^{(1)}(\pi,\pi^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime})=0
, so

(E.106)

|W(\pi,\pi^{\prime})|\leq Cn^{-4|{\mathcal{E}}|-d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})/2-d(\tau,\tau^{\prime})-2}.

Under our assumption for Case 3 we have

(E.107)

\displaystyle d(\pi_{T},\pi)+d(\pi,\pi^{\prime})+d(\pi^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})+d(\tau^{\prime},\tau)+d(\tau,\pi_{\mathrm{Id}})

\displaystyle\geq 4|{\mathcal{V}}_{T}|+4|{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{Id}}|-8

(which coincides with the direct bound from the triangle inequality for

d(\cdot,\cdot)
).
Applying these bounds in (
E.76
) shows again

\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})-\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}))^{4}\leq Cn^{2}
. Thus (
E.47
)
holds in all cases, concluding the proof.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

2.17
(b).

The ideas are similar to the proof of Proposition
2.14
(b), and we will omit details
to avoid repetition. Let
{\mathcal{F}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, where
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
consists
of the functions
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Given any
C_{0},\epsilon>0
,
we let
\zeta,\iota>0
be constants depending on

L,C_{0},\epsilon
to be specified later, and denote by
C,C^{\prime}>0
constants that do
not depend on
\zeta,\iota
.

To construct a set of polynomial anisotropic functions
{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
that verifies
condition (1) of Definition
2.7
, for each
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
we
include
p=f
in
{\mathcal{P}}_{0}
. For each
t=1,\ldots,T
and
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, suppose

f(\cdot)={\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}g({\mathbf{K}}^{\top}\cdot)
and
g=(\mathring{g}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
. We
construct an approximating
p\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
as follows:

(1)

For each
i\in[n]
, let
{\mathbf{K}}[i]
denote the
i^{\text{th}}
column of

{\mathbf{K}}
. Define the index set

(E.108)

{\mathcal{I}}=\{i\in[n]:\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}^{2}\geq\zeta\}.

For each
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
, define
\tilde{g}:{\mathbb{R}}^{t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

(E.109)

\tilde{g}_{i}({\mathbf{x}})=\mathring{g}_{i}(\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}{\mathbf{x}})

Let
L^{\prime}
be a constant larger than
L\cdot\|{\mathbf{K}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, and define

(E.110)

{\mathcal{G}}=\{\tilde{g}:{\mathbb{R}}^{t}\to{\mathbb{R}}\text{ such that }\tilde{g}\text{ is $L^{\prime}$-Lipschitz with }|\tilde{g}(0)|\leq L\}.

Then, since
\mathring{g}_{i}
satisfies the Lipschitz property
(
2.36
), we have that

\tilde{g}_{i}\in{\mathcal{G}}
for each
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}
be a
\zeta
-net defined independently of
n
for which,
for each
\tilde{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}
, there exists
h\in{\mathcal{N}}
such that

(E.111)

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t}:\|{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1/\zeta)^{2}}|h({\mathbf{x}})-\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}})|^{2}<\zeta.

For each
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
, let
h_{i}\in{\mathcal{N}}
be this approximation of
\tilde{g}_{i}
.

(2)

Now for each
h\in{\mathcal{N}}
, let
\tilde{q}:{\mathbb{R}}^{t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
polynomial that approximates
h
in the sense

(E.112)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\bm{\Sigma}}}[(h({\mathbf{Z}})-\tilde{q}({\mathbf{Z}}))^{2}]<\iota

for every
{\bm{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}
satisfying
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
.
For each
h\in{\mathcal{N}}
, we may construct this polynomial

\tilde{q}
independently of
n

in the same manner as in Proposition
2.14
(b).
For each
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
, let

\tilde{q}_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be this approximation of
h_{i}
constructed in
step (i), and define

(E.113)

\mathring{q}_{i}({\mathbf{x}})=\tilde{q}_{i}\bigg(\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{x}}\bigg)\text{ for }i\in{\mathcal{I}}.

Thus
\tilde{q}_{i}({\mathbf{x}})=\mathring{q}_{i}(\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}{\mathbf{x}})
, paralleling
(
E.109
). We set

(E.114)

\mathring{q}_{i}({\mathbf{x}})=\mathring{g}_{i}(0)\text{ for }i\notin{\mathcal{I}},

q=(\mathring{q}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, and
p(\cdot)={\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}q({\mathbf{K}}^{\top}\cdot)
, and we
include
p
in
{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
.

Note that the degrees and coefficients of each
(\tilde{q}_{i}:i\in{\mathcal{I}})
are
bounded by a constant independent of
n
.
Then, since
1/\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}
is bounded for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
, the degrees and
coefficients of
q=(\mathring{q}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are also bounded by a constant
independent of
n
. Thus
{\mathcal{P}}
constructed in this way is a set of
polynomial anisotropic functions satisfying Definition
2.15
.
Furthermore,
|{\mathcal{P}}|=|{\mathcal{F}}|
which is finite and independent of
n
.
Thus
{\mathcal{P}}
is BCP-representable by Proposition
2.17
(a).

To analyze the approximation error, consider any
{\bm{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}

with
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
,
and let
{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{n})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}
. Then

(E.115)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|f({\mathbf{Z}})-p({\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}g({\mathbf{K}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}})-{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}q({\mathbf{K}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(E.116)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}})-\mathring{q}_{i}({\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}})|^{2}\right]

(E.117)

\displaystyle=\frac{\|{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\tilde{g}_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\bigg)-\tilde{q}_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\bigg)\right|^{2}\right]+\frac{\|{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\neq{\mathcal{I}}}\mathbb{E}[|\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}})-\mathring{g}_{i}(0)|^{2}]

Applying the bound
\sup_{{\mathbf{K}}\in{\mathcal{K}}}\|{\mathbf{K}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C
,
the Lipschitz property (
2.36
) for
\mathring{g}_{i}
,
the bound
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
, and the condition
\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}^{2}<\zeta
for
all
i\notin{\mathcal{I}}
, the second term is bounded by
C\zeta
. Then, also
decomposing the first term, we have

(E.118)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|f({\mathbf{Z}})-p({\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\tilde{g}_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\bigg)-h_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\bigg)\right|^{2}\right]

(E.119)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|h_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\bigg)-\tilde{q}_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{Z}}\bigg)\right|^{2}\right]+C\zeta

where
h_{i}
is the above net approximation of
\tilde{g}_{i}
.
Here,
({\mathbf{K}}[i]/\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2})^{\top}{\mathbf{Z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t}
has law
{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}})
. Then
the first term of (
E.119
) is at most

C^{\prime}\zeta
by (
E.111
)
and the same argument as in Proposition
2.14
(b), while
the second term of (
E.119
) is at most

C^{\prime}\iota
from the guarantee in (
E.112
).
Thus choosing
\zeta,\iota
sufficiently small based on
\epsilon
shows

(E.120)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|f({\mathbf{Z}})-p({\mathbf{Z}})\|_{2}^{2}\right]<\epsilon,

verifying that condition (1) in
Definition

2.7
holds.

Next, we verify condition (2) in
Definition

2.7
. Let

{\mathcal{Q}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
where
{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
is the set
of all functions of the form
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}q({\mathbf{K}}^{\top}\cdot)
where
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{K}}\in{\mathcal{K}}
,

q=(\mathring{q}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
is a separable polynomial,
and
\mathring{q}_{i}:{\mathbb{R}}^{t}\to{\mathbb{R}}

has all coefficients bounded by 1. For any
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
of bounded degrees,

{\mathcal{P}}\cup\{q_{1},q_{2}\}
is also BCP-representable.
Suppose
{\bm{\Sigma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}
(with
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
) and

{\mathbf{z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}
satisfy, for any

q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
of bounded degrees, almost surely

(E.121)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}q_{1}({\mathbf{z}})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{z}})-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{n})}[q_{1}({\mathbf{Z}})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{Z}})]=0.

Similar to the above, we may bound
n^{-1}\|f({\mathbf{z}})-p({\mathbf{z}})\|_{2}^{2}
as

(E.122)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|f({\mathbf{z}})-p({\mathbf{z}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\left(\tilde{g}_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\bigg)-h_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\bigg)\right)^{2}

(E.123)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\left(h_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\bigg)-\tilde{q}_{i}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\bigg)\right)^{2}+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i\notin{\mathcal{I}}}\Big(\mathring{g}_{i}({\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}{\mathbf{z}})-\mathring{g}_{i}(0)\Big)^{2}.

The first and third terms may be bounded by
C^{\prime}\zeta
using
(
E.121
), (
E.111
),
and the same argument as in Proposition
2.14
(b).
The analysis for the second term is also similar to that in
Proposition
2.14
(b):
For each function
h\in{\mathcal{N}}
, define the index set

(E.124)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{h}=\{i\in{\mathcal{I}}:h_{i}=h\}.

For all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{h}
, the polynomial approximation
\tilde{q}_{i}
of
h_{i}

is the same, and we denote this as
\tilde{q}_{h}
. Then the second term may be
decomposed as

(E.125)

\displaystyle C\sum_{h\in{\mathcal{N}}}\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{h}}\bigg(h\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\bigg)-q_{h}\bigg(\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}}_{:=E_{h}}.

We claim that for each
h\in{\mathcal{N}}
,

E_{h}<2\iota
a.s. for all large
n
. If this does not
hold, we may consider a positive probability event where
E_{h}\geq 2\iota

infinitely often, and (
E.121
) holds for
q_{1},q_{2}
in a
suitably chosen countable subset of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
. We may pass to a subsequence

\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
where
E_{h}\geq 2\iota
,

|{\mathcal{I}}_{h}|/n\to\alpha
, and

{\bm{\Sigma}}\to\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}
. As in Proposition

2.14
, if
\alpha=0
then
E_{h}\to 0
, contradicting
E_{h}\geq 2\iota
. If
\alpha>0
, the convergence (
E.121
) over
a suitably chosen countable subset of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
implies
the convergence in moments of the empirical distribution of

\{\frac{{\mathbf{K}}[i]^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{K}}[i]\|_{2}}{\mathbf{z}}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{h}}
to those of

{\mathcal{N}}(0,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}})
, and hence also
Wasserstein-
k
convergence for any order
k\geq 1
.
Then since
h-q_{h}
is of polynomial growth, this implies

(E.126)

\displaystyle E_{h}\to\alpha\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}})}\left[(h({\mathbf{Z}})-q_{h}({\mathbf{Z}}))^{2}\right].

This limit is at most
\alpha\cdot\iota
by (
E.112
),
again contradicting
E_{h}\geq 2\iota
. Thus

E_{h}<2\iota
a.s. for all large
n
as claimed.
Applying this for each
h\in{\mathcal{N}}

shows that the second term of (
E.123
) is at
most
C(\zeta)\iota
a.s. for all large
n
. Then choosing
\zeta

sufficiently small followed by
\iota
sufficiently small ensures

n^{-1}\|f({\mathbf{z}})-p({\mathbf{z}})\|_{2}^{2}<\epsilon
,
establishing condition (2) of Definition
2.7
and
completing the proof.
∎

E.3.
Spectral functions

We consider the following class of polynomial spectral functions
paralleling Definition
2.18
, where
{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
has a form
r_{0}({\mathbf{G}}_{*})
for a function
r_{0}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
applied
spectrally to a matrix
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
.

Definition E.2
.

{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
is a set of
polynomial spectral functions

with shift
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
if, for some constants
C,K,D>0
:

–

For each
t=0,1,\ldots,T
and each
p\in{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
, there exist polynomial
functions
r_{0},r_{1},\ldots,r_{K}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
and coefficients

\{c_{ks}\}
with
|c_{ks}|<C
for which

(E.127)

p({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{vec}\bigg(r_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{G}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg)

where
r_{k}(\cdot)
is applied spectrally to the singular values of
its input as in (
2.38
).

–

For each
k=0,1,\ldots,K
, the above polynomial
r_{k}(\cdot)
takes a form

r_{k}(\cdot)=N^{1/2}\bar{r}_{k}(N^{-1/2}\,\cdot\,)
where

\bar{r}_{k}
is an odd-degree polynomial given by

(E.128)

\bar{r}_{k}(x)=\sum_{\text{odd }d=1}^{D}a_{kd}x^{d}

with coefficients
\{a_{kd}\}
satisfying
|a_{kd}|<C
.

We note that since the inputs to
r_{k}(\cdot)
will have operator norm on the
order of
N^{1/2}
, the scalings of
N^{-1/2}
and
N^{1/2}
defining

r_{k}(\cdot)
ensure that
\bar{r}_{k}(\cdot)
defined via (
E.128
)
is applied to an input with operator norm of constant order.

We show in Section
E.3.1
that if the shift
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}\equiv{\mathbf{G}}_{*}(n)
has i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries, then any such set
{\mathcal{P}}
with bounded
cardinality is BCP-representable almost surely with respect to

\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*}(n)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
. We then show in Section

E.3.2
that the Lipschitz spectral functions of
Definition
2.18
are BCP-approximable via this polynomial class.

E.3.1.
BCP-representability

To describe a set of tensors representing the polynomial spectral functions of
Definition
E.2
, we will identify each index
i\in[n]
with its equivalent index pair
(j,j^{\prime})\in[M]\times[N]
, and write
interchangeably

(E.129)

{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}]={\mathbf{T}}[(j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,(j_{k},j_{k}^{\prime})]

for a tensor
{\mathbf{T}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{\otimes k}\equiv({\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N})^{\otimes k}
. We represent the above class of
polynomial spectral functions by contractions of
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
with tensors of the
following form.

Definition E.3
.

For each even integer
k\geq 2
, the
alternating tensor of order

\boldsymbol{k}
is the tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{k}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{\otimes k}
with entries

(E.130)

{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{k}\left[(j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,(j_{k},j_{k}^{\prime})\right]=N^{1-k/2}\prod_{\text{odd }\ell\in[k]}1\!\!1\{j_{\ell}^{\prime}=j_{\ell+1}^{\prime}\}\prod_{\text{even }\ell\in[k]}1\!\!1\{j_{\ell}=j_{\ell+1}\},

with the identification
j_{2k+1}\equiv j_{1}
.

Lemma E.4
.

Let
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}\equiv{\mathbf{G}}_{*}(n)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
have i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries,
and let
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{4},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{K}
be the alternating tensors up
to a fixed even order
K\geq 2
. If
MN=n
and
M,N\leq C\sqrt{n}
for a
constant
C>0
, then

{\mathcal{T}}=\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{4},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{K}\}
satisfies
the BCP almost surely with respect to
\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*}(n)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
A.4
, it suffices to consider the set

{\mathcal{T}}=\{{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{K}\}
with
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
removed and show that
{\mathcal{T}}

satisfies the BCP.

Consider any expression inside the supremum of (
2.6
), where
each tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{m}

is given by
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{k}
for some even order
k\geq 2
. This takes the form

n^{-1}|\mathrm{va\ell}|
for a value

(E.131)

\mathrm{va\ell}=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}=1}^{n}\prod_{a=1}^{m}{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{k_{a}}[i_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}],

so we must show for each fixed
m,\ell,k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}
and
\pi
that

|\mathrm{va\ell}|\leq Cn
for a constant
C>0
and all large
n
.
Identifying each index
i\in[n]
with its equivalent index pair
(j,j^{\prime})\in[M]\times[N]
and applying the form of
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{k}
in Definition
E.3
,
we have

(E.132)

\displaystyle\mathrm{va\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}=1}^{M}\sum_{j_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{\ell}^{\prime}=1}^{N}\prod_{a=1}^{m}\Bigg(N^{1-k_{a}/2}

\displaystyle\times 1\!\!1\{j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)}^{\prime}=j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+2)}^{\prime}\}1\!\!1\{j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+3)}^{\prime}=j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+4)}^{\prime}\}\ldots 1\!\!1\{j_{\pi(k_{a}^{+}-1)}^{\prime}=j_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\times 1\!\!1\{j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+2)}=j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+3)}\}1\!\!1\{j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+4)}=j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+5)}\}\ldots 1\!\!1\{j_{\pi(k_{a}^{+})}=j_{\pi(k_{a-1}^{+}+1)}\}\Bigg).

Let us represent this value via a multigraph
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
on the
\ell
vertices

\{v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{\ell}\}
, with edges

{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}_{R}\sqcup{\mathcal{E}}_{B}
having two colors red and blue. For each
equality constraint
1\!\!1\{j_{a}^{\prime}=j_{b}^{\prime}\}
above, we add a red edge
(v_{a},v_{b})
to

{\mathcal{E}}_{R}
; for each equality constraint
1\!\!1\{j_{a}=j_{b}\}
, we add a blue edge

(v_{a},v_{b})
to
{\mathcal{E}}_{B}
. As an illustration, consider an example of
(
E.131
)
with
\ell=4
indices and
m=4
tensors given by

(E.133)

\mathrm{va\ell}=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}=1}^{n}{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2}[i_{1},i_{2}]{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2}[i_{1},i_{2}]{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{6}[i_{2},i_{2},i_{3},i_{3},i_{4},i_{4}]{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2}[i_{4},i_{4}].

Then
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
has 4 vertices
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\}
corresponding to the 4
indices
i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}
. The first two tensors
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2}
produce one red
edge and one blue edge each between
(v_{1},v_{2})
, the last
tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2}
produces one red and one blue self-loop on
v_{4}
,
and the tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{6}
produces a red self-loop on each vertex

v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}
and a blue edge connecting each pair

(v_{2},v_{3}),(v_{3},v_{4}),(v_{4},v_{2})
. The
resulting graph
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
is depicted in Figure
5
.

v_{1}
v_{2}
v_{3}
v_{4}

Figure 5
.
An example of the graph
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
representing the value
of the tensor contraction (
E.133
).

Let
\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},R})
and
\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},B})
be the numbers of connected components
in the subgraphs of
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
given by the red edges and blue edges,
respectively. Each red component corresponds to a distinct index
j^{\prime}\in[N]
of
(
E.132
), and each blue component corresponds to a distinct index
j\in[M]
. Thus

(E.134)

\mathrm{va\ell}=N^{m-\sum_{a=1}^{m}k_{a}/2}\cdot N^{\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},R})}\cdot M^{\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},B})}.

To bound this quantity, we claim the following combinatorial lemma, whose proof
we defer below.

Lemma E.5
.

Let
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
be any multigraph with edges
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}_{R}\sqcup{\mathcal{E}}_{B}
of two colors red and blue. Suppose, in each subgraph
G_{R}
or

G_{B}
of red or blue edges only, each vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
has non-zero even
degree (where a self-loop contributes a degree of 2 to its vertex). Suppose
also that
{\mathcal{E}}
can be
decomposed as a union of
m
edge-disjoint cycles
{\mathcal{E}}=S_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup S_{m}
, where each
S_{a}
for
a=1,\ldots,m
is a non-empty cycle containing an even number of edges
that alternate between red edges of
{\mathcal{E}}_{R}
and blue edges of
{\mathcal{E}}_{B}
. Then the
numbers of connected components of
G_{R},G_{B},G
satisfy

(E.135)

\textbf{c}(G_{R})+\textbf{c}(G_{B})\leq\frac{|{\mathcal{E}}|}{2}-m+2\textbf{c}(G).

We apply the lemma to
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
constructed above:
Each vertex
v_{b}
of
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
has non-zero even
degree in each of the red and blue subgraphs
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},R}
and
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},B}
,
because each appearance of the corresponding index
i_{b}
in (
E.131
)
contributes 1 to both the red and blue degrees of
v_{b}
, and each index

i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}
appears a non-zero even number of times in
(
E.131
) by surjectivity of
\pi
and the first condition of
Definition
2.3
. Each
tensor
\{{\mathbf{T}}_{a}:a=1,\ldots,m\}
contributes an even-length cycle
S_{a}
of edges
of alternating colors, so the decomposition
{\mathcal{E}}=S_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup S_{m}
holds with a number of cycles
m
equal to the number of tensors.
The total number of edges
|{\mathcal{E}}|
of
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
is the total order of all tensors

\sum_{a=1}^{m}k_{a}
. Finally,
G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}
is connected, for
otherwise there is a partition of the indices
i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}
corresponding
to two disjoint sets of tensors in (
E.131
), contradicting the second
condition of Definition
2.3
. Thus
\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}})=1
. Under the
given conditions for
M,N
, there exists a constant
C>0
for which
M/N<C
and

N^{2}<Cn
. Thus, Lemma
E.5
implies

(E.136)

\mathrm{va\ell}\leq CN^{m-\sum_{a=1}^{m}k_{a}/2+\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},R})+\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t},B})}\leq CN^{2\textbf{c}(G_{\mathrm{a\ell t}})}\leq C^{\prime}n

for some constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
, as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.5
.

Let
\deg_{G_{R}}(v)
and
\deg_{G_{B}}(v)
denote the degrees of the vertex

v\in{\mathcal{V}}
in
the subgraphs of red and blue edges only. Note that the assumptions of the lemma
imply
\deg_{G_{R}}(v)=\deg_{G_{B}}(v)
(because each alternating cycle

S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}
must contribute the same degree to
v
in both the red and blue
subgraphs) which is non-zero and even for each
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
.

We induct on the total number of edges
|{\mathcal{E}}|
, which must be even since
each cycle
S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}
is of even length.
For the base case
|{\mathcal{E}}|=2
, we must have
{\mathcal{E}}=S
for a single
alternating cycle
S
, and
{\mathcal{V}}=\{u\}
and
S=((u,u),(u,u))
for a single vertex

u
in order for
\deg_{G_{R}}(v)=\deg_{G_{B}}(v)\geq 2
to hold for all
vertices
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
. In this case
\textbf{c}(G_{R})=\textbf{c}(G_{B})=\textbf{c}(G)=1
,
|{\mathcal{E}}|=2
, and

m=1
, so (
E.135
) holds with equality.

Consider now
|{\mathcal{E}}|\geq 4
, and suppose by induction that the result
holds when the total number of edges is at most
|{\mathcal{E}}|-2
.
Pick any vertex
u\in{\mathcal{V}}
and consider the following cases:

(1)

Some alternating cycle, say
S_{1}
, has only two edges, both of
which are self-loops on
u
:
S_{1}=\{(u,u),(u,u)\}
. Then consider

G^{\prime}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
obtained from
G=({\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{E}})
by removing these two edges
from
{\mathcal{E}}
, and also removing the vertex
u
from
{\mathcal{V}}
if it appears on
no other edge. Clearly
\deg_{G_{R}^{\prime}}(v),\deg_{G_{B}^{\prime}}(v)
remain non-zero
and even for each remaining vertex

v\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}
, each remaining
S_{a}\subset{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
is
a non-empty even alternating cycle, the number of such cycles
constituting
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
is now
m^{\prime}=m-1
, and
|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|=|{\mathcal{E}}|-2
. Thus
the induction hypothesis applied to
G^{\prime}
yields

(E.137)

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})+\textbf{c}(G^{\prime}_{B})\leq\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|-m^{\prime}+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}|-m+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime}).

If
u
appears on another edge in
{\mathcal{E}}
, then

\deg_{G_{R}^{\prime}}(u)=\deg_{G_{B}^{\prime}}(u)>0
so
\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})=c(G_{R})
,

\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G_{B})
, and
\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G)
. If
u
appears only on these two
edges of
{\mathcal{E}}
(meaning
u
was its own connected component in
G
) then

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G_{R})-1
,
\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G_{B})-1
, and
\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G)-1
. In
both cases, (
E.137
) implies that (
E.135
) holds
for
G
.

(2)

Some alternating cycle, say
S_{1}
, has at least 4 edges including
a self-loop
(u,u)
:

(E.138)

S_{1}=\Big\{(u,u),(u,u_{3}),(u_{3},u_{4}),\ldots,(u_{2k},u)\Big\}.

Then consider
G^{\prime}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
obtained by merging
u
and
u_{3}
— i.e. replacing
u_{3}
by
u
in all edges of
{\mathcal{E}}
containing
u_{3}
and then
removing
u_{3}
from
{\mathcal{V}}
— and also replacing the edges of
S_{1}
by

S_{1}^{\prime}=\{(u,u_{4}),\ldots,(u_{2k},u)\}
which removes the first two edges
(now self-loops on
u
) from the cycle. Again

\deg_{G_{R}^{\prime}}(v)=\deg_{G_{B}^{\prime}}(v)
remains non-zero and even for each
v\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}
, and
{\mathcal{E}}
is comprised of
m^{\prime}=m
non-empty alternating cycles of even
length. We have
|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|=|{\mathcal{E}}|-2
, so the induction hypothesis applied to
G^{\prime}
yields

(E.139)

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})+\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|-m^{\prime}+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}|-m+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})-1.

Suppose (without loss of generality)
(u,u_{3})
is red.
Then
\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G)
and
\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G_{R})
, whereas
\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\in\{\textbf{c}(G_{B}),\textbf{c}(G_{B})-1\}
depending on whether
u
and
u_{3}

belong to the same connected component of
G_{B}
.
In particular
\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\geq\textbf{c}(G_{B})-1
, so (
E.139
)
implies that (
E.135
) holds for
G
.

(3)

Some alternating cycle, say
S_{1}
, has at least 4 non-self-loop
edges incident to
u
:

(E.140)

S_{1}=\Big\{(u,u_{2}),(u_{2},u_{3}),\ldots,(u_{j},u),(u,u_{j+2}),\ldots,(u_{2k},u)\Big\}

where
j
is odd. Suppose
(u,u_{2})
is red and
(u_{2k},u)
is blue;
then
(u_{j},u)
is red and
(u,u_{j+2})
is blue. Consider the graph

G^{\prime}
that merges
u
,
u_{2}
, and
u_{2k}
, and that also replaces
the edges of
S_{1}
by those of two alternating cycles

(E.141)

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,u_{3}),\ldots,(u_{j-1},u_{j}),(u_{j},u)\Big\},

(E.142)

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,u_{j+2}),(u_{j+2},u_{j+3}),\ldots,(u_{2k-1},u)\Big\}.

This replaces the two red edges
(u_{j},u),(u,u_{2})
(the latter now a
self-loop on
u
) by a single red
edge
(u_{j},u)
, and the two blue edges
(u_{2k},u),(u,u_{j+2})
(the
former now a self-loop on
u
)
by a single blue edge
(u,u_{j+2})
. Then
S^{\prime}_{1}
and

S^{\prime\prime}_{1}
are both alternating cycles of non-zero even length, and
G^{\prime}

has
|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|=|{\mathcal{E}}|-2
edges comprised of
m^{\prime}=m+1
alternating cycles. The
induction hypothesis applied to
G^{\prime}
yields

(E.143)

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})+\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|-m^{\prime}+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}|-m+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})-2.

We have
\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G)
, because all vertices connected to
u/u_{2}/u_{2k}

in
G
remain connected to
u
in
G^{\prime}
.
We have also
\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})\geq\textbf{c}(G_{R})-1
, because merging
(u,u_{2})

does not change
\textbf{c}(G_{R})
, merging
(u,u_{2k})
decreases
\textbf{c}(G_{R})
by
at most
1, and replacing
(u_{j},u),(u,u_{2})
by the single edge
(u_{j},u)
and
replacing
(u_{2k},u),(u,u_{j+2})
by the single edge
(u,u_{j+2})
do not change
\textbf{c}(G_{R})
.
Similarly,
\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\geq\textbf{c}(G_{B})-1
, and applying these statements to
(
E.143
) shows that (
E.135
) holds for
G
.

(4)

Some alternating cycle, say
S_{1}
, has at least 4 non-self-loop
edges incident to
u
:

(E.144)

S_{1}=\Big\{(u,u_{2}),(u_{2},u_{3}),\ldots,(u_{j},u),(u,u_{j+2}),\ldots,(u_{2k},u)\Big\}

where
j
is even.
Then we may split
S_{1}
into the two cycles,

(E.145)

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,u_{2}),(u_{2},u_{3}),\ldots,(u_{j},u)\Big\}

(E.146)

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,u_{j+2}),(u_{j+2},u_{j+3}),\ldots,(u_{2k},u)\Big\},

both of which are of non-zero even length. This reduces to the final case below, which shows that in fact

(E.147)

\textbf{c}(G_{R})+\textbf{c}(G_{B})\leq\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}|-(m+1)+2\textbf{c}(G).

(5)

Some two alternating cycles, say
S_{1},S_{2}
, each contains at least
two consecutive non-self-loop edges incident to
u
, denoted by:

(E.148)

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,u_{2}),(u_{2},u_{3}),\ldots,(u_{2j},u)\Big\}

(E.149)

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,v_{2}),(v_{2},v_{3}),\ldots,(v_{2k},u)\Big\}

By reversing the orderings of the cycles, we may assume

(u,u_{2}),(u,v_{2})
are red and
(u_{2j},u),(v_{2k},u)
are blue.
Consider the graph
G^{\prime}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
obtained by replacing the edges of

S_{1}\sqcup S_{2}
by

(E.150)

S^{\prime}=\Big\{(u_{2},u_{3}),\ldots,(u_{2j-1},u_{2j}),(u_{2j},v_{2k}),(v_{2k},v_{2k-1}),\ldots,(v_{3},v_{2}),(v_{2},u_{2})\Big\}

and removing
u
from
{\mathcal{V}}
if no other edge of
{\mathcal{E}}
except the above
four edges of
S_{1},S_{2}
are incident to
u
.
This replaces the two red edges
(v_{2},u),(u,u_{2})
by a single red edge

(v_{2},u_{2})
, and the two blue edges
(u_{2j},u),(u,v_{2k})
by a single
blue edge
(u_{2j},v_{2k})
.
These actions do not change the degree of any vertex besides
u
, and
the red/blue degrees of
u
are each decreased by 2.

Note that
S^{\prime}
remains an alternating cycle of non-zero even length, so

G^{\prime}
has
|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|=|{\mathcal{E}}|-2
edges comprised of
m^{\prime}=m-1
alternating cycles.
The induction hypothesis applied to
G^{\prime}
yields

(E.151)

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})+\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}|-m^{\prime}+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}|{\mathcal{E}}|-m+2\textbf{c}(G^{\prime}).

If
S^{\prime}
is disconnected from the component containing
u
in
G^{\prime}
,
then
\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G)+1
.
In this case the component of
G_{R}^{\prime}
containing
(v_{2},u_{2})
is also
disconnected from the component of
G_{R}^{\prime}
containing
u
, so

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})+1
, and similarly
\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})+1
.
Then applying these to (
E.151
) shows that (
E.135
)
holds for
G
.
If
u
is no longer present in
G^{\prime}
or if
S^{\prime}
remains connected to the
component containing
u
in
G^{\prime}
, then
\textbf{c}(G^{\prime})=\textbf{c}(G)
.
In this case, we note simply that the above operation of replacing

(v_{2},u),(u,u_{2})
by
(v_{2},u_{2})
and
(u_{2j},u),(u,v_{2k})
by

(u_{2j},v_{2k})
cannot decrease
\textbf{c}(G_{R})
or
\textbf{c}(G_{B})
, so

\textbf{c}(G_{R}^{\prime})\geq\textbf{c}(G_{R})
and
\textbf{c}(G_{B}^{\prime})\geq\textbf{c}(G_{B})
.
Then applying these to (
E.151
) also shows that
(
E.135
) holds for
G
.

Since
\deg_{G_{R}}(u)=\deg_{G_{B}}(u)\geq 2
, these cases exhaust all
possibilities for the vertex
u
.
So (
E.135
) holds for
G
, completing the induction.
∎

Using Lemma
E.4
, we now verify that polynomial spectral
functions are BCP-representable.

Lemma E.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}
be a set of polynomial spectral functions
as given by Definition
E.2
, with shift
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}\equiv{\mathbf{G}}_{*}(n)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
having i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries. Suppose
|{\mathcal{P}}|<C

for a constant
C>0
independent of
n
. Then
{\mathcal{P}}
is BCP-representable almost
surely with respect to
\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*}(n)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.

Proof.

For any odd integer
d\geq 1
, consider the multivariate monomial

(E.152)

q({\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{d})=N^{1/2-d/2}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}{\mathbf{X}}_{2}^{\top}\ldots{\mathbf{X}}_{d-2}{\mathbf{X}}_{d-1}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{d}.

Writing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
for the Euclidean inner-product in

{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
, observe
for any
{\mathbf{X}}_{d+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
that

(E.153)

\displaystyle\langle q({\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{d}),{\mathbf{X}}_{d+1}\rangle

\displaystyle=N^{1/2-d/2}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}{\mathbf{X}}_{2}^{\top}\ldots{\mathbf{X}}_{d}{\mathbf{X}}_{d+1}^{\top}

(E.154)

\displaystyle=N^{1/2-d/2}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d+1}=1}^{M}\sum_{j_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{d+1}^{\prime}=1}^{N}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}[j_{1},j_{1}^{\prime}]1\!\!1\{j_{1}^{\prime}=j_{2}^{\prime}\}{\mathbf{X}}_{2}[j_{2},j_{2}^{\prime}]1\!\!1\{j_{2}=j_{3}\}\ldots

(E.155)

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt{\mathbf{X}}_{d}[j_{d},j_{d}^{\prime}]1\!\!1\{j_{d}^{\prime}=j_{d+1}^{\prime}\}{\mathbf{X}}_{d+1}[j_{d+1},j_{d+1}^{\prime}]1\!\!1\{j_{d+1}=j_{1}\}

(E.156)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{d+1}=1}^{n}{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d+1}\left[i_{1},\dots,i_{d+1}\right]\prod_{a=1}^{d+1}{\mathbf{X}}_{a}\left[i_{a}\right].

Thus

(E.157)

q({\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{d})={\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d+1}[{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{d},\,\cdot\,].

In (
E.127
), if each
\bar{r}_{k}(x)=x^{d_{k}}
is a single monomial of
odd degree, then
r_{k}(x)=N^{1/2-d_{k}/2}\bar{r}_{k}(x)
, so this implies

(E.158)

\displaystyle p({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d_{k}+1}\Bigg[\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}{\mathbf{z}}_{s}+r_{0}({\mathbf{G}}_{*}),\ldots,\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}{\mathbf{z}}_{s}+r_{0}({\mathbf{G}}_{*}),\,\cdot\,\Bigg]

(E.159)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d_{k}+1}\Bigg[\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}{\mathbf{z}}_{s}+{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d+1}[{\mathbf{G}}_{*},\ldots,{\mathbf{G}}_{*},\,\cdot\,],\ldots,\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}{\mathbf{z}}_{s}+{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d+1}[{\mathbf{G}}_{*},\ldots,{\mathbf{G}}_{*},\,\cdot\,],\,\cdot\,\Bigg]

Then multi-linearity of
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d_{k}+1}
shows that
p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})

takes the form (
2.9
) for tensors
{\mathbf{T}}^{(0)},{\mathbf{T}}^{(\sigma)}

that are given by scalar multiples of contractions of

{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d_{k}+1}
,
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{d+1}
, and
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
. Then again by
multi-linearity, the same holds true for any
p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})
defined by
(
E.127
) where
\bar{r}_{k}
are given by general odd polynomials of the form (
E.128
). Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be the set of all tensors arising
in this representation (
2.9
) for all polynomials
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
. Since the cardinality
|{\mathcal{P}}|
is bounded independently of
n
, so is

|{\mathcal{T}}|
. Each tensor in
{\mathcal{T}}
is a contraction of some number of tensors

\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{D+1}\}
multiplied by a scalar that is
also bounded independently of
n
. By Lemma
E.4
,

\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{D+1}\}
satisfies the BCP almost surely,
and hence by Lemma
A.1
so does
{\mathcal{T}}
. Thus
{\mathcal{P}}
is
almost surely BCP-representable.
∎

E.3.2.
BCP-approximability

We now prove Proposition
2.19
on the BCP-approximability of
Lipschitz spectral functions. As a first step, we show that
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
in Lemma

E.6
may be replaced by a matrix
{\mathbf{X}}_{*}
with the same
singular values as
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
, but with singular vectors satisfying the
conditions of Proposition
2.19
.

Corollary E.7
.

Let
{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
be a shift matrix satisfying
the conditions of Proposition
2.19
.
Suppose
{\mathbf{X}}_{*}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
where
{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{U}}
are the singular vector
matrices of
{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}
, and
{\mathbf{S}}
is independent of
({\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}})
and equal in
law to the matrix of singular values (sorted in increasing order)
of
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
having
i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries. Then Lemma
E.6
holds also with

{\mathbf{X}}_{*}
in place of
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
.

Proof.

In the proof of Lemma
E.4
, the BCP for

\{{\mathbf{G}}_{*},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{K}\}
follows from Corollary

A.4
, which applies Wick’s rule and Gaussian
hypercontractivity to verify that

(E.160)

\mathbb{P}[|n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\mathbf{G}}_{*})|>C]\leq C^{\prime}e^{cn^{1/m}}

for some constants
C,C^{\prime},c,m>0
, where
|n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\mathbf{G}}_{*})|
is any expression
appearing inside the supremum of (
2.6
) viewed as a function of
the Gaussian input
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
. Writing
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}={\mathbf{O}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{S}}{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}}^{\top}
for the singular
value decomposition of
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
,
we note that
{\mathbf{O}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{S}},{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}
are independent, and
{\mathbf{O}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times M}
and
{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N\times N}
are Haar-distributed.
Then, by the given assumption that
{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{U}}
have bounded densities with respect
to Haar measure, (
E.160
) implies also

(E.161)

\mathbb{P}[|n^{-1}\mathrm{va\ell}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})|>C]\leq C^{\prime}e^{cn^{1/m}}

for the given matrix
{\mathbf{X}}_{*}
and a different constant
C^{\prime}>0
.
Then the argument of Corollary

A.4
implies that
\{{\mathbf{X}}_{*},{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{a\ell t}}^{K}\}
also
satisfies the BCP almost surely, and hence Lemma
E.6
holds
equally with
{\mathbf{X}}_{*}
in place of
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}
.
∎

Next, we argue that the singular value matrix
{\mathbf{D}}
of
{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}

may be approximated by
g({\mathbf{S}})
for some Lipschitz
function
g(\cdot)
applied to the singular value matrix
{\mathbf{S}}
of Corollary

E.7
. The idea of the approximation is encapsulated in the
following lemma.

Lemma E.8
.

Fix any constant
C_{0}>0
and any probability distribution
\mu
on an interval

(a,b)
with
0\leq a<b
, where
\mu
has continuous and strictly positive
density on
(a,b)
. Then for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a constant

L_{\epsilon}>0
such that the following holds:

Let
{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
be the set of functions
g:[a,b]\to[0,C_{0}]
such that

(E.162)

g(a)=0,\qquad|g(x)-g(y)|\leq L_{\epsilon}|x-y|\text{ for all }x,y\in[a,b].

Let
s^{(j)}
be the
j/M
-quantile of
\mu
, i.e. the value where

\mu([a,s^{(j)}])=j/M
, for each
j=1,\ldots,M
.
Then for all large enough
M
and for any
0\leq d^{(1)}\leq\cdots\leq d^{(M)}\leq C
, there exists
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
such that

(E.163)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(g(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

Set
s^{(0)}=a
, and note that
s^{(M)}=b
.
For any
0\leq d^{(1)}\leq\cdots\leq d^{(M)}\leq C_{0}
, we construct
g
as
follows: First let
g(s^{(0)})=g(a)=0
. Then for
j=1,\ldots,M
, fixing a small constant

\iota>0
to be determined later, let

(E.164)

\displaystyle g(s^{(j)})=\begin{cases}d^{(j)}&\text{if }d^{(j)}-g(s^{(j-1)})\leq(s^{(j)}-s^{(j-1)})\iota^{-1},\\
g(s^{(j-1)})+(s^{(j)}-s^{(j-1)})\iota^{-1}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

and let
g
be the linear interpolation of the points

(s^{(j)},g(s^{(j)}))
for
j=0,\ldots,M
.
Note that
g
is
\iota^{-1}
-Lipschitz,
g
is monotonically increasing,
and
g(s^{(j)})\leq d^{(j)}
for all
j\in[M]
.

For some small
\delta\in(0,1)
to be determined later, let

j_{0}<j_{1}<\ldots<j_{K}
be all indices in the range

[\delta M,(1-\delta)M]
for which
g(s^{(j)})=d^{(j)}
.
Observe that
g(s^{(j)})=g(s^{(j-1)})+(s^{(j)}-s^{(j-1)})\iota^{-1}

for each
j=\lceil\delta M\rceil,\ldots,j_{0}-1
, so

(E.165)

s^{(j_{0}-1)}-s^{(\lceil\delta M\rceil-1)}=\iota[g(s^{(j_{0}-1)})-g(s^{(\lceil\delta M\rceil-1)})]\leq C_{0}\iota.

Since the density of
\mu
is bounded above and below on compact sub-intervals
of
(a,b)
, there exist constants
C_{\delta},c_{\delta}>0
depending
on
\delta
such that

(E.166)

\mu(x)\in[c_{\delta},C_{\delta}]\text{ for all }x\in[s^{(\lceil\delta M\rceil-1)},s^{(\lfloor(1-\delta)M\rfloor+1)}].

Thus

(E.167)

\frac{j_{0}-\lceil\delta M\rceil}{M}\leq c_{\delta}^{-1}(s^{(j_{0}-1)}-s^{(\lceil\delta M\rceil-1)})\leq C_{0}c_{\delta}^{-1}\iota.

By a similar argument,

(E.168)

\frac{\lfloor(1-\delta)M\rfloor-j_{K}}{M}\leq C_{0}c_{\delta}^{-1}\iota.

We can then decompose the total error as

(E.169)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(g(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{j_{0}-1}(g(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}+\frac{1}{M}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}(g(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}

(E.170)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=j_{K}+1}^{M}(g(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}

(E.171)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}^{2}(\delta+C_{0}c_{\delta}^{-1}\iota)+\frac{1}{M}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\underbrace{\sum_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}(g(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}}_{A_{k}}

where the inequality applies
d^{(j)}-g(s^{(j)})\in[0,C_{0}]
for all
j\in[M]

and the bounds (
E.167
) and (
E.168
) for
j_{0},j_{K}
.

Now for each
k\in[K]
,
\{(s^{(j)},g(s^{(j)}))\}_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}
are
points on the line segment connecting

(s^{(j_{k-1})},g(s^{(j_{k-1})}))=(s^{(j_{k-1})},d^{(j_{k-1})})
and

(s^{(j_{k}-1)},g(s^{(j_{k}-1)}))
with slope
\iota^{-1}
.
Applying
g(s^{(j)})\leq d^{(j)}\leq d^{(j_{k})}
for all

j=j_{k-1}+1,\ldots,j_{k}-1
, we have

(E.172)

\displaystyle A_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}(d^{(j_{k})}-g(s^{(j)}))^{2}=\sum_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}\Big(d^{(j_{k})}-d^{(j_{k-1})}-(s^{(j)}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\iota^{-1}\Big)^{2}.

Since
d^{(j_{k-1})}=g(s^{(j_{k-1})})
,
d^{(j_{k})}=g(s^{(j_{k})})
and
g
is

\iota^{-1}
-Lipschitz, we have
d^{(j_{k})}-d^{(j_{k-1})}\leq(s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\iota^{-1}
.
Meanwhile,
d^{(j_{k})}-d^{(j_{k-1})}\geq(s^{(j)}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\iota^{-1}

for all
j=j_{k-1}+1,\ldots,j_{k}-1
.
Therefore, we can further bound
A_{k}
as

(E.173)

\displaystyle A_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}\Big((s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\iota^{-1}-(s^{(j)}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\iota^{-1}\Big)^{2}=\iota^{-2}\sum_{j=j_{k-1}+1}^{j_{k}-1}(s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j)})^{2}

(E.174)

\displaystyle\leq\iota^{-2}(j_{k}-1-j_{k-1})(s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j_{k-1})})^{2}

where the second inequality holds because
0<s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j)}<s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j_{k-1})}
for all
j=j_{k-1}+1,\ldots,j_{k}-1
.
Next, observe that

(E.175)

d^{(j_{k})}-d^{(j_{k-1})}\geq d^{(j_{k}-1)}-d^{(j_{k-1})}=(s^{(j_{k}-1)}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\iota^{-1}

for all
k\in[K]
.
Applying this bound and (
E.166
),

(E.176)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{j_{k}-1-j_{k-1}}{M}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\delta}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(s^{(j_{k}-1)}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\leq C_{\delta}\iota\sum_{k=1}^{K}(d^{(j_{k})}-d^{(j_{k-1})})\leq C_{0}C_{\delta}\iota,

(E.177)

\displaystyle\max_{k=1}^{K}(s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j_{k-1})})

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k=1}^{K}(s^{(j_{k})}-s^{(j_{k}-1)})+\max_{k=1}^{K}(s^{(j_{k}-1)}-s^{(j_{k-1})})\leq c_{\delta}^{-1}M^{-1}+C_{0}\iota.

Then applying these bounds to (
E.174
)
and (
E.171
), we obtain

(E.178)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(s^{(j)}-d^{(j)})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}^{2}(\delta+C_{0}c_{\delta}^{-1}\iota)+C_{0}C_{\delta}\iota^{-1}(c_{\delta}^{-1}M^{-1}+C_{0}\iota)^{2}.

Finally, for any target error level
\epsilon
, we can choose
\delta\equiv\delta(\epsilon)
small enough followed by
\iota\equiv\iota(\delta,\epsilon)
small enough such that for all large
M
, the above
error is less than
\epsilon
. The Lipschitz constant
L_{\epsilon}
is given by

\iota^{-1}
, completing the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.19
.

We may assume without loss of generality that
M\leq N
, hence
\delta=\lim_{n\to\infty}M/N\in(0,1]
, and
{\mathbf{D}}=\mathrm{diag}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{M})
where
d_{1}\leq\ldots\leq d_{M}
.
Let
{\mathbf{X}}_{*}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
be as defined in Corollary
E.7
,
where
{\mathbf{S}}=\mathrm{diag}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{M})
coincides with the singular values of a matrix

{\mathbf{G}}_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\times N}
having i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries, and

s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{M}
.
Let
\nu
be the Marcenko-Pastur density with aspect ratio
\delta
,
which describes the asymptotic eigenvalue distribution of
{\mathbf{G}}_{*}{\mathbf{G}}_{*}^{\top}/N
,
and let
\mu
be the density of
\sqrt{\lambda}
when
\lambda\sim\nu
.
We note that
\mu
is a continuous and strictly positive density on a single
interval of support
(a,b)
, where
a=0
if
\delta=1
. Then
letting
s^{(j)}
be the
j/M
-quantile of
\mu
,
the almost-sure weak convergence

\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\delta_{s_{j}/\sqrt{N}}\to\mu

(c.f.
[
63
]
) implies the converges of quantiles

(E.179)

\max_{j=1}^{M}|s_{j}/\sqrt{N}-s^{(j)}|\to 0\text{ a.s.}

Let
d^{(j)}=d_{j}/\sqrt{N}
.
By Lemma
E.8
, for any
\epsilon>0
, there
exists a
n
-independent class
{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
of
L_{\epsilon}
-Lipschitz
functions
g:[a,b]\to[0,C]
with
g(a)=0
such that for some
\bar{g}_{0}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
,

(E.180)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\bar{g}_{0}(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}\leq\epsilon.

For each
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
and any constant
B_{\epsilon}\geq b
,
we may extend
g
to an odd function on
[-B_{\epsilon},B_{\epsilon}]
by setting

g(x)=0
for
x\in[0,a]
,
g(x)=g(b)
for
x\in[b,B_{\epsilon}]
, and

g(x)=-g(-x)
for
x\in[-B_{\epsilon},0]
.
By the Weierstrass approximation theorem, we may then construct a

n
-independent net

{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon}
of polynomial functions such that for any
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
,
there exists
r\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon}
for which

(E.181)

\max_{x\in[-B_{\epsilon},B_{\epsilon}]}(g(x)-r(x))^{2}\leq\epsilon.

Replacing
r(x)
by
(r(x)-r(-x))/2
, we may assume that each polynomial
function
r\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon}
is odd.
Then for the Lipschitz function
\bar{g}_{0}
in (
E.180
), the corresponding odd polynomial
\bar{r}_{0}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon}
that approximates

\bar{g}_{0}
in the sense (
E.181
) further satisfies

(E.182)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\bar{r}_{0}(s^{(j)})-d^{(j)})^{2}\leq 4\epsilon.

Set
r_{0}(\cdot)=N^{1/2}\bar{r}_{0}(N^{-1/2}\,\cdot\,)
. Then

\|r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})-{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=N^{1/2}\|\bar{r}(N^{-1/2}{\mathbf{S}})-N^{-1/2}{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,
so this and (
E.179
) imply, almost surely for all large

n
,

(E.183)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})-{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\bar{r}_{0}(s_{j}/\sqrt{N})-d_{j}/\sqrt{N})^{2}<5\epsilon.

Now consider any
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
, which by assumption takes a form

(E.184)

f({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{vec}\bigg(g_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})+{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\bigg)\bigg).

For any
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t\times t}
satisfying
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<C_{0}
,
if
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{n})
, then
there is a constant
B>0
such that

(E.185)

\max_{k=1}^{K}\bigg\|\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}<B\sqrt{N}\text{ a.s.\ for all large }n.

For each
k=1,\ldots,K
, define
\bar{g}_{k}(\cdot)
such that

g_{k}(\cdot)=N^{1/2}\bar{g}_{k}(N^{-1/2}\,\cdot\,)
, and note that
\bar{g}_{k}
is
also
L
-Lipschitz. In the definition of the above net
{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon}
, we may
assume that
L_{\epsilon}
is larger than this Lipschitz constant
L
,
and that
B_{\epsilon}
is larger than this constant
B
.
Let
\bar{r}_{k}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon}
be the approximation for
\bar{g}_{k}
satisfying
(
E.181
), set
r_{k}(\cdot)=N^{1/2}\bar{r}_{k}(N^{-1/2}\,\cdot\,)
, and
consider the polynomial approximation

(E.186)

\displaystyle p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{vec}\bigg(r_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg)

for
f
. Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be the set of polynomial spectral functions
consisting of this approximation for each
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Then
{\mathcal{P}}
is
BCP-representable by Corollary
E.7
. Furthermore, we have

(E.187)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|f({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg\|g_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\bigg)-r_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Since
g_{k}
is
L
-Lipschitz and satisfies
g_{k}(0)=0
, the matrix function
given by applying
g_{k}
spectrally to the singular values of its input
is also
L
-Lipschitz in the Frobenius norm

[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
. Thus

(E.188)

\bigg\|g_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}\bigg)-g_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq L\|{\bm{\Theta}}_{*}-r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sqrt{\epsilon n},

the last inequality holding a.s. for all large
n
by
(
E.183
). By the
approximation property (
E.181
) for
\bar{g}_{k}
and
\bar{r}_{k}

and the operator norm bound (
E.185
) where
B<B_{\epsilon}
, also

(E.189)

\displaystyle\bigg\|g_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)-r_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(E.190)

\displaystyle=N^{1/2}\bigg\|\bar{g}_{k}\bigg(N^{-1/2}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg)-\bar{r}_{k}\bigg(N^{-1/2}\bigg(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})\bigg)\bigg)\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(E.191)

\displaystyle\leq N^{1/2}\cdot M^{1/2}\sqrt{\epsilon}=\sqrt{\epsilon n}

a.s. for all large
n
. Combining (
E.188
) and
(
E.191
),

(E.192)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|f({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{ a.s.\ for all large $n$}.

Applying the dominated convergence theorem, this implies

n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|f({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\mathbf{X}}_{*}]<C\epsilon
for a
constant
C>0
a.s. for all large
n
,
verifying the first condition of BCP-approximability.

For the second condition of BCP-approximability, let
{\mathcal{Q}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
be the
set of all polynomial functions of the form (
E.127
)
where
r_{0}(\cdot)
is as defined above,
\{c_{ks}\}
have the same uniform bound
as in
{\mathcal{P}}
, and
r_{k}(\cdot)=N^{1/2}\bar{r}_{k}(N^{-1/2}\,\cdot\,)
for some
monomial
\bar{r}_{k}(x)=x^{d_{k}}
of odd degree
d_{k}\geq 1
. Then
{\mathcal{P}}\cup\{q_{1},q_{2}\}
continues to satisfy the BCP for any
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
of uniformly
bounded degrees. Let
{\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}
be any random vectors such that

(E.193)

n^{-1}q_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-n^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}[q_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})^{\top}q_{2}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\mid{\mathbf{X}}_{*}]\to 0\text{ a.s.}

for all
q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}
of uniformly bounded degrees. Applying
(
E.188
), for a constant
C_{1}>0
,

(E.194)

\frac{1}{n}\bigg\|f({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{t})-\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{vec}\bigg(g_{k}\bigg(\sum_{s}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*})}_{\tilde{f}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg\|_{2}^{2}<C_{1}\epsilon\text{ a.s.\ for all large $n$}.

Applying (
E.191
) and the dominated convergence theorem, also

(E.195)

\frac{1}{n}\,{\mathbb{E}}[\|\tilde{f}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\mathbf{X}}_{*}]<C_{1}\epsilon\text{ a.s.\ for all large $n$}.

Suppose by contradiction that there is a positive-probability event
\Omega

on which

(E.196)

n^{-1}\|f({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}>5C_{1}\epsilon

infinitely often. Let
\Omega^{\prime}
be the intersection of
\Omega
with
the probability-one
event where (
E.194
) holds, and where
(
E.193
