) holds for all
q_{1},q_{2}
in a suitably chosen countable
subset of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
For any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, we may pass to a subsequence

\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
along which (
E.196
) holds and where
the expectation over
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t}
of the empirical singular value distribution of

N^{-1/2}(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{Z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*}))

converges weakly and in Wasserstein-
j
to a limit
\nu_{k}
, for each

k=1,\ldots,K
and every order
j\geq 1
.
Then the statement (
E.193
) over a suitably chosen
countable subset of
{\mathcal{Q}}

implies that the singular value distribution of
N^{-1/2}(\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{ks}\mathrm{mat}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})+r_{0}({\mathbf{X}}_{*}))
converges weakly and in Wasserstein-
j
to the same limit

\nu_{k}
, for each
k=1,\ldots,K
and
j\geq 1
. Since
(\bar{g}_{k}-\bar{r}_{k})^{2}

is a function of polynomial growth, this implies that

(E.197)

n_{j}^{-1}\|\tilde{f}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}-n_{j}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}[\|\tilde{f}({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{Z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}\mid{\mathbf{X}}_{*}]\to 0

along this subsequence
\{n_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
. Then combining
with (
E.194
) and (
E.195
), we have

(E.198)

\limsup_{j\to\infty}n_{j}^{-1}\|f({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4C_{1}\epsilon,

contradicting (
E.196
).
So
n^{-1}\|f({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})-p({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 5C_{1}\epsilon

a.s. for all large
n
, showing
the second condition of BCP-approximability and completing the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Auxiliary proofs

F.1.
Tensor network representation of polynomial AMP

We prove Lemma
4.4
on the unrolling of polynomial AMP into
tensor network values. It is convenient to introduce the following
object which will represent the vector-valued iterates
{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{u}}_{t}
.

Definition F.1
.

An
open
{\mathcal{T}}
-labeling

{\mathcal{L}}^{*}
of a connected
ordered multigraph
G
is an
assignment of a label
*
to a vertex
v^{*}\in G
with
\deg(v^{*})=1
,
and a tensor label
{\mathbf{T}}_{v}\in{\mathcal{T}}

to each remaining vertex
v\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus\{v^{*}\}
such that

{\mathbf{T}}_{v}
has order equal to
\deg(v)
.

The
vector value

\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
of this open labeling is
the vector satisfying, for any
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(F.1)

\langle\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{*}),{\mathbf{v}}\rangle=\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{v}})

where
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{v}}
is the labeling of
G
that completes
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}
by assigning
the label
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
to
v^{*}
.

One may understand
v^{*}
and the (unique) edge
e^{*}
incident to
v^{*}
as
“placeholders”: the vector value
\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{*})
is obtained by
contracting all tensor-tensor products represented by edges
{\mathcal{E}}\setminus e^{*}
,
with the final index
i_{e^{*}}\in[n]
associated to
e^{*}
left unassigned.

Lemma F.2
.

Fix any
T\geq 1
.
Under the assumptions of Lemma
4.4
, there exist constants

C,M>0
, a list of connected ordered multigraphs
G_{1},\ldots,G_{M}

depending only on
T,D,C_{0}
and
independent of
n
, and a list of open
({\mathcal{T}}\cup{\mathbf{W}})
-labelings

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}^{*},\ldots,{\mathcal{L}}_{M}^{*}
of
G_{1},\ldots,G_{M}
and
coefficients
a_{1},\ldots,a_{M}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|a_{m}|<C
, such that

(F.2)

{\mathbf{u}}_{T}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}a_{m}\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*}).

Proof.

By assumption, each function
f_{0},\ldots,f_{T}
admits a
representation

(F.3)

f_{s}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{s})={\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(0)}+\sum_{d=1}^{D}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{S}}_{s,d}}{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(\sigma)}[{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(d)},\,\cdot\,]

for some tensors
{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(0)},{\mathbf{T}}_{s}^{(\sigma)}\in{\mathcal{T}}
. Then by definition of
the algorithm (
2.1
) and multi-linearity, there exists a constant
M>0

(depending only on
(T,D)
) and coefficients
a_{1},\ldots,a_{M}\in\mathbb{R}
for which

(F.4)

{\mathbf{u}}_{T}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}a_{m}{\mathbf{u}}_{T}^{(m)},

each
a_{m}
is a product of a subset of the Onsager coefficients

\{{-}b_{ts}\}_{s<t\leq T}
,
and each
{\mathbf{u}}_{T}^{(m)}
is the output of an iterative algorithm
with initialization
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{(m)}={\mathbf{u}}_{1}
and

(F.5)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{z}}_{t}

\displaystyle\in\{{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{u}}_{t-1}\}

(F.6)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle\in\begin{cases}{\mathbf{T}}_{t}^{(0)}\\
{\mathbf{T}}_{t}^{(\sigma)}[{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(d)},\cdot]\text{ for some }d\in[D]\text{ and }\sigma\in{\mathcal{S}}_{t,d}\end{cases}

for
t=1,2,\ldots,T-1
. That is, in each iteration,
the algorithm is defined by a single (fixed) choice for
{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\in\{{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{u}}_{t-1}\}
and a non-linear function representable
by a single (fixed) tensor in
{\mathcal{T}}
. Thus it suffices to
show that for any such algorithm and any
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
,
there exists a connected ordered multigraph
G

independent of
n
— in fact, a tree rooted at
v^{*}
—
and an open
({\mathcal{T}}\cup{\mathbf{W}})
-labeling
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}
of
G
for which

(F.7)

{\mathbf{u}}_{t}=\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{*}).

This follows from an easy induction on
t
: For
t=1
,

{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
is given by
\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{*})
for a tree
G

with root
v^{*}
and a single edge connecting to a child with label

{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\cap{\mathcal{T}}
. Assuming that
(
F.7
) holds for
s=1,\ldots,t-1
, let
(G_{s},{\mathcal{L}}_{s}^{*})

be the tree graph and open labeling for which

{\mathbf{u}}_{s}=\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{s}}({\mathcal{L}}_{s}^{*})
, and let
d+1
be the order of the tensor

{\mathbf{T}}_{t-1}^{(\sigma)}
defining
{\mathbf{u}}_{t}
. Then define a tree graph

G
with open labeling
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}
such that
G
is rooted at
v^{*}
, and
v^{*}
has
a single child
v
labeled by
{\mathbf{T}}_{t-1}^{(\sigma)}
, with
\deg(v)=d+1
and ordered
edges
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d+1}
where the last edge
e_{d+1}
connects to
v^{*}
.
For each other edge
e_{i}
with
i\in[d]
:

–

If
{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(i)}={\mathbf{u}}_{j}
for some
j\in[t-1]
, then the
i^{\text{th}}

subtree

v\overset{e_{i}}{-}T_{i}
rooted at
v
coincides with
(G_{j},{\mathcal{L}}_{j}^{*})
with
v

replacing the root of
(G_{j},{\mathcal{L}}_{j}^{*})
.

–

If
{\mathbf{z}}_{\sigma(i)}={\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{u}}_{j}
for
j=\sigma(i)\in[t-1]
, then
this
i^{\text{th}}
subtree has a form

(F.8)

v\overset{e_{i}}{-}v_{i}\overset{e_{i}^{\prime}}{-}T_{i}

where
v_{i}
has
\deg(v_{i})=2
and label
{\mathbf{W}}
, its first edge
e_{i}^{\prime}
connects to

T_{i}
, and its second edge
e_{i}
connects to
v
. The subtree
v_{i}\overset{e_{i}^{\prime}}{-}T_{i}
coincides with
(G_{j},{\mathcal{L}}_{j}^{*})
with
v_{i}

replacing the root of
(G_{j},{\mathcal{L}}_{j}^{*})
.

It is readily checked from the definition of
\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}

and the inductive hypothesis
{\mathbf{u}}_{s}=\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{s}}({\mathcal{L}}_{s}^{*})
for each
s\in[t-1]
that
{\mathbf{u}}_{t}=\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}}^{*})
, completing the induction and the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

By Lemma
F.2
and the given condition that
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}

are also
{\mathcal{T}}
-reprsentable, we have

(F.9)

\displaystyle\phi_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}a_{m}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*})

(F.10)

\displaystyle\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M^{\prime}}a_{m}^{\prime}\mathrm{va\ell}_{G_{m}^{\prime}}({{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*}}^{\prime})

where
|a_{m}|,|a_{m}^{\prime}|\leq C
,
G_{m},G_{m}^{\prime}
are connected ordered multigraphs
(independent of
n
) with open labelings
{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*},{{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*}}^{\prime}
, and

C,M_{1},M_{2}>0
are constants independent of
n
. Then

(F.11)

\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T})=\frac{1}{n}\phi_{1}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T})^{\top}\phi_{2}({\mathbf{z}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{z}}_{T})=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{M^{\prime}}\frac{a_{m}a_{m^{\prime}}}{n}\langle\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{m}}({\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*}),\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{m}^{\prime}}({{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{*}}^{\prime})\rangle.

The lemma then follows from the observation that for any two connected
ordered multigraphs
G_{1},G_{2}
with open labelings
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}^{*},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}^{*}
, we have

(F.12)

\langle\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{1}}({\mathcal{L}}_{1}^{*}),\mathrm{vec\text{-}va\ell}_{G_{2}}({\mathcal{L}}_{2}^{*})\rangle=\mathrm{va\ell}_{G}({\mathcal{L}})

where
(G,{\mathcal{L}})
is the tensor network obtained removing the
distinguished vertex
v^{*}
from both
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, and replacing the edge

v_{1}-v^{*}
in
G_{1}
and the edge
v_{2}-v^{*}
in
G_{2}
by a single edge
v_{1}-v_{2}
.
(If
v_{1}-v^{*}
is the
i^{\text{th}}
ordered edge of
v_{1}
in
G_{1}
and
v_{2}-v^{*}

is the
j^{\text{th}}
ordered edge of
v_{2}
in
G_{2}
,
then
v_{1}-v_{2}
remains the
i^{\text{th}}
ordered edge of

v_{1}
and
j^{\text{th}}
ordered edge of
v_{2}
in
G
.)
∎

F.2.
Extension to asymmetric AMP

We prove Theorem
3.3
on the extension of our main results to AMP
with an asymmetric matrix
{\mathbf{W}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times n}
.

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

We “embed” the asymmetric AMP algorithm (
3
)
into the symmetric AMP algorithm (
2.1
) by setting

(F.13)

{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{sym}}=\sqrt{\frac{m}{m+n}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{A}}&{\mathbf{W}}\\
{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{B}}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n+m)\times(n+m)}

where
{\mathbf{A}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times m}
and
{\mathbf{B}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
have
independent Gaussian entries with mean 0 and variance
1/m
. Then

{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
is a Wigner matrix of size
n+m
,
satisfying Assumption
2.2
.

We consider the initialization

(F.14)

f_{0}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\cdot)\equiv{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}}=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}

and the sequence of non-linear functions
f_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{(n+m)\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
given by

(F.15)

\displaystyle f_{2t-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:(2t-1)}^{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{bmatrix}f_{t}(({\mathbf{z}}_{2j-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}[1:m])_{j=1}^{t})\\
0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle f_{2t}^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:2t}^{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
g_{t}(({\mathbf{z}}_{2j}^{\mathrm{sym}}[(m+1):(m+n)])_{j=1}^{t})\end{bmatrix}.

We then consider the iterates of the symmetric AMP algorithm (
2.1
),

\begin{split}{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}&={\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{sym}}{\mathbf{u}}_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}b_{ts}^{\mathrm{sym}}{\mathbf{u}}_{s}^{\mathrm{sym}}\\
{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}^{\mathrm{sym}}&=f_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{sym}})\end{split}

where
b_{ts}^{\mathrm{sym}}
is as defined in Definition
2.1
for the
function sequence
\{f_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}\}_{t\geq 0}
. It is direct to verify that the
iterates of the asymmetric AMP algorithm (
3
) are embedded within
the iterates of this algorithm as

(F.16)

{\mathbf{z}}_{t}={\mathbf{z}}_{2t-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}[1:m],\quad{\mathbf{y}}_{t}={\mathbf{z}}_{2t}^{\mathrm{sym}}[(m+1):(m+n)],

(F.17)

{\mathbf{u}}_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{m}{m+n}}{\mathbf{u}}_{2t-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}[(m+1):(m+n)],\quad{\mathbf{v}}_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{m}{m+n}}{\mathbf{v}}_{2t}^{\mathrm{sym}}[1:m],

and that the Onsager coefficients and state evolution covariances
of Definition
3.1
are related to those of this symmetric AMP
algorithm (
F.2
) by

(F.18)

b_{ts}=\sqrt{\frac{m}{m+n}}b_{2t-1,2s}^{\mathrm{sym}},\qquad a_{ts}=\sqrt{\frac{m}{m+n}}b_{2t,2s-1}^{\mathrm{sym}},

(F.19)

{\bm{\Omega}}_{t}=({\bm{\Sigma}}_{2t-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}[2j-1,2k-1])_{j,k=1}^{t},\qquad{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}=({\bm{\Sigma}}_{2t}^{\mathrm{sym}}[2j,2k])_{j,k=1}^{t}.

Furthermore, for
i=1,2
, defining

(F.20)

\displaystyle\phi_{i}^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:2T}^{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{bmatrix}\phi_{i}(({\mathbf{z}}_{2j-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}[1:m])_{j=1}^{T})\\
0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:2T}^{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\psi_{i}(({\mathbf{z}}_{2j}^{\mathrm{sym}}[(m+1):(m+n)])_{j=1}^{T})\end{bmatrix},

and setting
\phi^{\mathrm{sym}}=(n+m)^{-1}(\phi_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}})^{\top}(\phi_{2}^{\mathrm{sym}})
and

\psi^{\mathrm{sym}}=(n+m)^{-1}(\psi_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}})^{\top}(\psi_{2}^{\mathrm{sym}})
, we have

(F.21)

\phi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})=\phi^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:2T}),\qquad\psi({\mathbf{z}}_{1:T})=\psi^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:2T}).

Thus Theorem
3.3
follows directly from Theorems

2.6
and
2.9
applied up to
iteration
2T
of the symmetric AMP algorithm (
F.2
), provided
that the assumptions in Theorems

2.6
and
2.9
hold.

To check these assumptions
in the polynomial AMP setting of Theorem
3.3
(a), note that

{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2T}^{\mathrm{sym}}
is block-diagonal with even rows/columns constituting one
block equal to
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}
and odd rows/columns constituting a second block
equal to
{\bm{\Omega}}_{T}
. Then
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{2T}^{\mathrm{sym}})>c
by the given
conditions for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{T}
and
{\bm{\Omega}}_{T}
, implying also that

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}})>c
for each
t=1,\ldots,2T
. To apply
Theorem
2.6
, it remains to check that

{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}=\{f_{0}^{\mathrm{sym}},\ldots,f_{2T-1}^{\mathrm{sym}},\phi_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}},\phi_{2}^{\mathrm{sym}}\}
is
BCP-representable. As
{\mathcal{G}}=\{g_{0},\ldots,g_{T-1},\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\}
is
BCP-representable, there exists a set of tensors

{\mathcal{T}}^{g}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{g}
with
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{g}\subset({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{\otimes k}

that satisfies the BCP,
for which each
g\in{\mathcal{G}}
admits the representation (
2.9
)
with tensors in
{\mathcal{T}}^{g}
. Similarly, there exists a set of tensors

{\mathcal{T}}^{f}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{f}
with
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{f}\subset({\mathbb{R}}^{m})^{\otimes k}

that satisfies the BCP,
for which each
f\in{\mathcal{F}}=\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}
admits
the representation (
2.9
) with tensors in
{\mathcal{T}}^{f}
.
Let
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{g,\mathrm{sym}}\subset({\mathbb{R}}^{m+n})^{\otimes k}
be the embeddings of the
tensors
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{g}
into the diagonal block of
({\mathbb{R}}^{m+n})^{\otimes k}

corresponding to the last
n
coordinates
m+1,\ldots,m+n
, similarly let

{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{sym}}\subseteq({\mathbb{R}}^{m+n})^{\otimes k}
be the embeddings of

{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{f}
into the diagonal block of
({\mathbb{R}}^{m+n})^{\otimes k}
corresponding to
the first
m
coordinates
1,\ldots,m
, and define

(F.22)

{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{sym}}={\mathcal{T}}^{g,\mathrm{sym}}\sqcup{\mathcal{T}}^{f,\mathrm{sym}}=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{g,\mathrm{sym}}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{sym}}.

Then each function
f\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
admits the representation
(
2.9
) with tensors in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
. To see that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{sym}}

satisfies the BCP, consider the expression on the left side of
(
2.6
). If all tensors in this expression belong to

{\mathcal{T}}^{g,\mathrm{sym}}
, then (
2.6
) holds by the BCP for

{\mathcal{T}}^{g}
. Similarly if all tensors belong to
{\mathcal{T}}^{f,\mathrm{sym}}
, then
(
2.6
) holds by the BCP for
{\mathcal{T}}^{f}
.
If there is at least one tensor belonging to both
{\mathcal{T}}^{g,\mathrm{sym}}
and

{\mathcal{T}}^{f,\mathrm{sym}}
, then the second condition of Definition
2.3
requires
that there is at least one index
i_{j}
for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}

that is shared between a tensor
{\mathbf{T}}_{a}\in{\mathcal{T}}^{g,\mathrm{sym}}
and a tensor

{\mathbf{T}}_{b}\in{\mathcal{T}}^{f,\mathrm{sym}}
. Then the
{\mathbf{T}}_{a}
factor is 0 for all summands where

i_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, the
{\mathbf{T}}_{b}
factor is 0 for all summands where
i_{j}\in\{m+1,\ldots,m+n\}
, so (
2.6
) holds trivially as the left
side is 0. This verifies that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
satisfies the BCP. Then

{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
is BCP-representable, and Theorem
3.3
(a)
follows from Theorem
2.6
.

For Theorem
3.3
(b), we check the conditions of
Theorem
2.9
: The boundedness and Lipschitz properties
(
2.23
) for

{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}=\{f_{0}^{\mathrm{sym}},\ldots,f_{2T-1}^{\mathrm{sym}},\phi_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}},\phi_{2}^{\mathrm{sym}}\}
follow
from the given property (
3.6
) for

{\mathcal{F}}=\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\}
and

{\mathcal{G}}=\{g_{0},\ldots,g_{T-1},\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\}
. The condition that

\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}[S_{t},S_{t}])>c
for the set of preceding iterates

S_{t}
on which
f_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}
depends, for each
t=1,\ldots,2T-1
, follows also
from the given conditions for
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{t},{\bm{\Omega}}_{t}
and the above
identifications (
F.19
). For BCP-approximability of
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
,
fix any
C_{0},\epsilon>0
, and let
{\mathcal{P}}^{g},{\mathcal{Q}}^{g}
and
{\mathcal{P}}^{f},{\mathcal{Q}}^{f}
be the sets
of polynomial functions guaranteed by Definition
2.7

for the BCP-approximable families
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
respectively.
For each
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{g}
where
p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
consider the embedding
p^{\mathrm{sym}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{(n+m)\times 2t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
given by

(F.23)

p^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:2t}^{\mathrm{sym}})=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
p(({\mathbf{z}}_{2j}^{\mathrm{sym}}[(m+1):(m+n)])_{j=1}^{t})\end{pmatrix},

and for each
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{f}
where
p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times t}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
consider the embedding
p^{\mathrm{sym}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{(n+m)\times(2t-1)}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
given by

(F.24)

p^{\mathrm{sym}}({\mathbf{z}}_{1:(2t-1)}^{\mathrm{sym}})=\sqrt{\frac{m+n}{m}}\begin{pmatrix}p(({\mathbf{z}}_{2j-1}^{\mathrm{sym}}[1:m])_{j=1}^{t})\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{sym}}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{2T}{\mathcal{P}}_{t}^{\mathrm{sym}}

be the set of such embeddings for all
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{g}
and
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{f}
, and define similarly
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
as the set of such embeddings
for all
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{g}
and
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{f}
.
The preceding argument shows that
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{sym}}\cup\{q_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}},q_{2}^{\mathrm{sym}}\}
for any
q_{1}^{\mathrm{sym}},q_{2}^{\mathrm{sym}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
of uniformly
bounded degrees continues to satisfy the BCP. Then, in light
of (
F.15
) and (
F.20
), both conditions of Definition

2.7
hold for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
and the constants

C_{0},[(m+n)/m]\epsilon>0
, via these sets
{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}
. Thus
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{sym}}
is
BCP-approximable, and Theorem
3.3
(b)
follows from Theorem
2.9
.
∎

F.3.
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma F.3

(Stein’s Lemma)
.

Let
{\mathbf{X}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}})
be a multivariate Gaussian vector in
\mathbb{R}^{t}

with non-singular covariance
{\bm{\Sigma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times t}
,
and let
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{t}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a weakly differentiable function such that

\mathbb{E}|\partial_{j}g({\mathbf{X}})|<\infty
for each
j=1,\ldots,t
. Then

(F.25)

{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{X}}\,g({\mathbf{X}})]={\bm{\Sigma}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\nabla g({\mathbf{X}})

Proof.

See
[
32
, Lemma 6.20]
.
∎

Lemma F.4

(Wick’s rule)
.

Suppose
{\bm{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathrm{Id})
vectors,

{\mathbf{T}}\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\otimes d}
is a deterministic tensor, and

\sigma:[d]\to[t]
is any
index map. Let
\pi=\{\sigma^{-1}(1),\ldots,\sigma^{-1}(t)\}
be the partition
of
[d]
where each block is the pre-image of a single index
s\in[t]
under

\sigma
, and let

(F.26)

{\mathcal{P}}=\{\text{pair partitions }\tau\text{ of }[d]:\tau\leq\pi\}

be the set of pairings of
[d]
that refine
\pi
(where
{\mathcal{P}}=\emptyset
if any
block of
\pi
has odd cardinality). Then

(F.27)

\mathbb{E}\,{\mathbf{T}}[{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d)}]=\sum_{\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}}\,\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in[n]^{d}}{\mathbf{T}}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\prod_{(a,b)\in\tau}1\!\!1\{i_{a}=i_{b}\}.

Proof.

When
{\mathbf{T}}
has a single entry
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})
equal to 1 and remaining
entries 0, we have

(F.28)

\mathbb{E}\,{\mathbf{T}}[{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d)}]=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(1)}[i_{1}]\ldots{\bm{\xi}}_{\sigma(d)}[i_{d}]]=\sum_{\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}}\prod_{(a,b)\in\tau}1\!\!1\{i_{a}=i_{b}\}

by
[
39
]
, and the result for general
{\mathbf{T}}\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\otimes d}
follows from linearity.
∎

Lemma F.5

(Gaussian hypercontractivity inequality)
.

Let
{\bm{\xi}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
have i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries. Then there are absolute
constants
C,c>0
such that for any polynomial
p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
of degree
k

and any
t\geq 0
,

(F.29)

\mathbb{P}[|p({\bm{\xi}})-\mathbb{E}p({\bm{\xi}})|\geq t]\leq Ce^{-(\frac{ct^{2}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}[p({\bm{\xi}})]})^{1/m}}.

Proof.

See
[
62
, Theorem 1.9]
.
∎

Lemma F.6

(Weighted uniform polynomial approximation)
.

Let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
be functions admitting the
representations

(F.30)

p_{k}(x_{k})=p_{k}(1)+\int_{1}^{x_{k}}\frac{w_{k}(t_{k})}{t_{k}}dt_{k}\text{ for all }x_{k}\geq 1

where
w_{k}(t_{k})
is nondecreasing and
\lim_{t_{k}\to\infty}w_{k}(t_{k})=\infty
for each
k=1,\ldots,d
, and such that

\int_{1}^{\infty}(p_{k}(x_{k})/x_{k}^{2})\,dx_{k}=\infty
for each
k=1,\ldots,d
.
Let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{d}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,\infty)
be continuous functions satisfying

q_{k}(x_{k})\geq p_{k}(|x_{k}|)
. If
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is any
continuous function such that

(F.31)

\lim_{\|(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\|_{2}\to\infty}\exp\Big({-}\sum_{k=1}^{d}q_{k}(x_{k})\Big)f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})=0,

then

(F.32)

\inf_{Q}\sup_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\bigg\{\exp\Big({-}\sum_{k=1}^{d}q_{k}(x_{k})\Big)|f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})-Q(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})|\bigg\}=0

where
\inf_{Q}
is the infimum over all polynomial functions
Q:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

See
[
29
, Theorem 1]
.
∎
